"So the questioner said to him: O my Master, were you there in your persons or in your representatives? And he said to him: Rather, in our persons and our representatives."

That is: were they present with their real selves or with their hijabs (veils)? The answer: with the real selves and with the hijabs both.

"wa amma al-Hajar al-Aswad wal-Rukn al-Asad fa-qad yakun wallahu alam anna qawl Sayyidna Sarim al-Din innahu ittasala bihi shayun latif mimma dakhkhiratuhu al-inayat al-ilahiyyah min hujub Amir al-Muminin al-mukanna 'anhu bil-ayn al-azimah al-nuraniyyah."

"As for the Black Stone and the Most Fortunate Corner, it may be, and Allah knows best, that the statement of Sayyidna Sarim al-Din is that a subtle substance was connected with it from what the divine providence stored from the veils of the Commander of the Faithful, who is alluded to as the Great, Luminous 'Ayn."

A subtle substance, drawn from what was stored by divine providence — namely the jussah-e-ibtidaiyyah of 'Ashir, taken from the hijabs of Amir al-Muminin, who is called the Ayn al-Azimah, the luminous one — was connected to his real self.

"wa amma qawluka wa hal yaqiu 'alayhi al-subhaniyyah, faqad sabbahatuhu al-malaikah al-qudsaniyyah haythu tashawwaqat ila tasawwur shakhsihi al-gharr, fa-awjada Allah taala subhanahu fil-samawati mithaluhu, fa-kanat tasbihuhu wa taqdisuhu laylan wa naharan ashiyyatan wa abkara, fa-kana hadha mithal azim."

"As for your statement, does the term Subhaniyyah apply to him: the holy angels have glorified him, when they yearned for the perception of his brilliant person. So Allah the Exalted, glory be to Him, brought into existence his likeness in the heavens, and they glorified and sanctified him night and day, evening and morning. And this was a great example."

The angels yearned to perceive his brilliant person — to behold him. In the spiritual realm they sought from Allah the didar (vision) of Amir al-Muminin. Though he is corporeally present on earth, they longed to behold him in the spiritual world and in the heavens. So Allah created a likeness of him in the heavens, and the angels glorified and sanctified him morning and evening, night and day.

"fa-kayfa bi-khalifat al-Ashir al-Hakim."

"So how is it with the Caliph of the 'Ashir, the Wise?"

As the Quranic verse says:

"fil-samai ilahun wa fil-ardi ilah."

"In the heaven is a god, and on the earth is a god."

This points to the state of Amir al-Muminin: in heaven there is an Ali and his likeness, and on earth there is an Ali whose likeness is in heaven.

"fa-kayfa bi-khalifat al-Ashir al-Hakim wa ma-ndimamuhu fi dimn al-Qaim al-hashir al-nashir."

"So how is it with the Caliph of the 'Ashir, the Wise, and his inclusion within the Qaim, the Gatherer, the Resurrector?"

His inclusion took place with the Qaim; he became the Bab al-Abwab for the Qaim. The Aimmah who came before — from Janab Hunayn to Amir al-Muminin — and the thalathah sufarah (the three emissaries), namely Mawlana 'Abd al-Muttalib, Mawlana Abdullah, and Mawlana Abu Talib, did not have their inclusion through Amir al-Muminin. Rather, they remained in the barzakh (intermediate state) of the Janab-e-Qaim, and the Qaim absorbed them directly, not through Amir al-Muminin. They hold a very high status. Up to Janab Hashim, Amir al-Muminin gathered the hayakil (هَيَاكِل) (structures) of the sufarah-e-nayyirah (luminous emissaries). After that, he gathered the hayakil from Janab Hasan to Abul-Qaim, who became the Bab al-Abwab of Qaim. It has never happened that an Imam gathered the Aimmah who came after him; yet this is among the extraordinary miracles of Amir al-Muminin — that he gathered the hayakil of the Imams who came later as well. His nafs-e-hissiyah (sensory soul) was so powerful and spiritual that it surpassed the hayakil of others, for only one whose nafs-e-hissiyah surpasses another's haykal (هَيْكَلٌ) can gather it.

His hashr (gathering) was therefore with the Qaim. He will become the caliph of the 'Ashir, the Mudabbir. By Ashir here is meant not that Ashir, but the jussah (جسّة) of this kawr; yawman ma (one day), through a progression in the line that has been formed, he will become the khalifah (caliph) within it — the Caliph of the Ashir. "wa suuduhu ila rutbat al-Ashir," and his ascent to the rank of the Ashir: his ascent will be as the Ashir, and he will be the jussah-e-ibtidaiyyah in which he will come, yawman ma.

"sabbahathu al-malaikah al-ruhaniyyah bi-ala min dhalika al-shan... li-man fatana al-maani al-rabbaniyyah."

"The spiritual angels glorified him with a station higher than that, for one who comprehends the Lordly meanings."

They glorified him with a station higher than any he had held before — higher than what he had attained across the various recurring cycles. In the seventh dawr (period), in the seventh zuhur (ظُهُور) (manifestation), this manifestation of his was the seventh, higher than all previous manifestations. It was because it was his seventh manifestation that he performed those extraordinary miracles. The angels glorified him with a glorification surpassing all previous manifestations, for the one who reflects deeply upon the Lordly meanings.

"wa amma qawluka kam hatta tabaddala al-mashriq maghriban wal-shimal junuban, fa-yakunu dhalika 'inda tarakhi al-rubatat, wa takunu al-tabdilat wal-istihalat."

"As for your statement, how long until the east has changed into the west and the north into the south: that will be at the loosening of the ties, and the changes and transformations will occur."

The tarakhi al-rubatat is the end of the kawr. Very great changes will occur then, and major events will take place, such that things will be entirely transformed.

"wa amma 'iddat dhalika fa-qad dhakarahu Sayyidna Humam."

"As for the period of that, Sayyidna Humam has mentioned it."

The duration has been mentioned by Sayyidna Humam al-Din and Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan, and those matters are to be consulted with the permission of the Wali al-Amr.

"wa fi aqwal dhil-jaddayn ma yujli al-sada wal-rayn."

"And in the sayings of Dhul-Jaddayn is that which clears away the rust and the stain."

Dhul-Jaddayn is Sayyidna Husayn bin Ahmad, a figure of immense stature. He wrote the tafsir (exegesis) of the Quran in seventy days and nights, on the command of Sayyidna Ali Sahab, who gave that command because Sayyidna Husayn bin Ahmad was the son of his sister. He was thirty-three years old when the command was given. He also performed Hajj on behalf of Sayyidna Jafar. Sayyidna Jafar, despite his great status as the twenty-eighth Dai and the first among the shumukh duat (lofty duat), did not himself perform Hajj; he had once set out for it but had to turn back due to certain difficulties. Sayyidna Ali Sahab therefore commanded: "You perform the Hajj of Sayyidna Sahab as a qada (make-up) and complete it." He did so. And when Sayyidna Ali Sahab commanded, "Husayn, write the tafsir of the Quran," he wrote it in seventy days and nights, even while traveling throughout that period. When he had completed it, a very swift scribe from Yemen took eight months to copy it. It fills three large volumes.

This is a magnificent state that Sayyidna Husayn bin Ahmad achieved. Although he was not a munfarid (solitary) dai, he holds immense standing; the duat take tawassul (intercession) through him, and Sayyidna Ismail bin Hibatullah Sahab refers to him as "Sayyidna Usama" in several places within the Kawakeb al-Durriyah — that great and magnificent book. Help is therefore to be sought from the sayings of Dhul-Jaddayn, which remove the rust that has settled upon hearts.

## 15. Beneath the Kabah: Lowlands, Waters, and the Well of the Jinn

The fourth question: *al-sual al-rabi 'an al-bataih allati tahta al-Kabah, kam hiya* — "The fourth question, concerning the lowlands that are beneath the Kabah: how many are they?" — asks how many *bataih* (بِطَاحٌ) (lowlands) lie beneath the Kabah and how many they number. It continues with *wa min ayna abwabuhum* — "And from where are their gates?" — asking where their doors are; then *wa ma tasir wa yasiru ilayha* — "And what travels and goes toward it?" — what things go up to them; then *wa ma yadharu minha* — "And what appears from it?" — what things manifest from these *bataih*; and finally *wa Bir Dhat al-Alam alladhi qala al-shakhs al-azim* — "And the Well of Dhat al-Alam about which the great person spoke."

Bir Dhat al-Alam figures in the Battle of Bani Mustaliq. When the Prophet, salawatullahi 'alayhi, was returning from that expedition with his army, they came to a place whose people said: "We have a well in which there are shayatin who trouble us greatly, and all the people here are in distress." The Prophet commanded his army: "Who among you will go and fight them?" Two young men stepped forward. He said: "I give the guarantee of jannah to them" — that is, whoever fights these devils is guaranteed paradise. The two young men fought and were martyred. The Prophet then said to Ali (ع): "Qum ya Ali" — "Stand, O Ali" — that he should stand and fight them. Ali fought at Bir Dhat al-Alam, and when he emerged, blood was flowing from his sword. Rasulullah asked: "O Ali, how did you fight them?" He replied: "I fought them with my sword, and I fought them by reciting and blowing the verses of the Quran upon them." The verses of the Quran were so potent that he vanquished them through recitation and breath. He mentioned a third method as well, saying: "This is how I fought."

Bir Dhat al-Alam is thus the name of a well at which Amir al-Muminin fought. In one book, a question was put to a dai: were all the jinn whom Amir al-Muminin fought at Bir Dhat al-Alam destroyed? The dai answered: "No, they remain, but the coming Qaim will bring them to an end." The matter resembles that of *'adhab* (عَذَاب) (punishment) and *thawab* (reward) — the punishing of the enemies of the din — which the Aimmah do not finally determine. They leave that judgment to the Qaim, who is the Hashir and Nashir (Gatherer and Resurrector). The decision of whom to send to jannah and whom to send to the fire rests in part with Amir al-Muminin, who is Qasim al-Nar wal-Jannah (Distributor of the Fire and the Garden), and ultimately with the Qaim, who will pass the final judgment. He will cast the *azdad* (أَضْدَاد) (opposites) into Sakhr, and whatever decision he makes, he will make.

From the *'ulum* (knowledge), it can be understood that when the Qaim makes his *zuhur* (appearance) — at the occasion of Hajj — he will say to the Muminin: "On the yawm al-nahr, the day of Id, you kill the azdad." The Muminin will kill them, and this will continue for three days. After one or two days, such a stench will spread through the valley of Mina that the Muminin will find it difficult to breathe. They will plead to the Qaim: "We cannot endure this state, and yet we must remain in the valley of Mina until the thirteenth." The Imam will then command the winds, and winds will blow; the *manafidh* (مَنَافِذ) (outlets) of Sakhr — of which there are four — will draw those things away toward Sakhr. Sayyidna Ali Sahab has listed the names of those four *manafidh* in one of his books.

Asked where this well lies, he said: "I have not been able to determine where it is."

Among the miracles of Amir al-Muminin, from the perspective of the Qaim-e-Juzwi (partial Qaim), Bir Dhat al-Alam is likely counted as well.

The question continues: *Inni dhahabtu ila tahti mizab al-Kabah fi qissat qital al-jinn* — "I went under the water-spout of the Kabah in the story of the battle with the jinn." Amir al-Muminin said that he went under the *mizab* (water-spout) of the Kabah. Then *wal-mawaqif al-arba* — "and the four stations": the *mawaqif* (مَوَاقِفُ) are four. Then *wa min ayna manafidh al-bataih* — "And from where are the outlets of the lowlands?" — from where are the *manafidh* of the *bataih*, the windows. And finally *wa mannu bil-jawab fa-antum ahl al-sawab* — "So grant the favor of an answer, for you are the people of correctness": do the favor of giving the answer, for you are the people of correctness.

The reply opens: *al-jawab 'ala dhalika al-sual, naqulu bi-awn Allah al-Kabir al-Mutaal* — "The answer to that question: we say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Most High." The answer is offered with the help of Allah, the Great, the Most High, and with the grace of the Wali al-Asr wal-Zaman, alayhi salawat al-Rahim al-Rahman. And *wa tayid (تَأْيِيد) Mawlai al-Alam wa sirrihi al-ba' wal-muazzam* — "And with the support of my Master, the Most Learned, and his brilliant and magnificent secret" — that is, with the support of Sayyidna Hibatullah and his magnificent and luminous secret.

*anna al-bataih allati hawla al-qalb al-gharr hiya arba* — "that the lowlands which are around the brilliant heart are four." The *bataih* surrounding the luminous *qalb* (heart) are four, *kama sabaqa min al-qawl* — "as has been mentioned in the previous statement" — *li-man haqqaqa al-nazar* — "for one who has verified the observation." *wa qawluka innaha tahta al-bayt al-munir fa-laysa dhalika kadhalik bal hawlahu* — "And your statement that it is beneath the luminous House: it is not so; rather it is around it." The *bataih* do not lie beneath the Bayt al-Munir (Luminous House); they lie around it, on its peripheries, *'ala amrin jalilin khatir* — "upon a great and momentous affair." The *bataih* are on its peripheries, not beneath it.

*wa amma qawluka min ayna abwabuhum wa majariha* — "As for your statement, 'From where are their gates and their channels?'" — asking where their doors are and from where their channels flow. *wa amma ma yasadu minha wa yasiru ilayha* — "As for what ascends from it and what goes toward it" — things ascend and descend toward it. *fa-alam anna tahta al-qalb maun latif* — "So know that beneath the heart is a subtle water" — beneath the *qalb* there is a subtle water. *wa fi wasatihi* — "and in its center" — in its center too there is subtle water. *wa minhu yanbau ila tahti tilka al-bataih* — "And from it gushes forth to beneath those lowlands" — from those waters things ascend toward the *bataih*, *bi-majari* — "through channels" — channels that open, *wa manafidh* — "and outlets" — windows also being there, *'ala amrin sharif* — "upon a noble affair."

*wa hawla hadhihi al-bataih ahwiyah* — "And around these lowlands are atmospheres" — around these four *bataih* there are atmospheres: *al-sharifah al-shaffafah al-munirah* — "the noble, transparent, luminous ones" — transparent and luminous. *wa kadhalika hawla hadhihi al-bataih maun latif* — "And likewise, around these lowlands is a subtle water" — around the *bataih* there is also a very subtle water. *wa la yazal yasadu minha bukhar sharif* — "And a noble vapor does not cease to ascend from it" — noble vapors continuously ascend from these *bataih*. *wa huwa al-mizaj* — "And it is the temperament" — this is the *mizaj* (مِزَاجٌ) (temperament), *alladhi dhakarahu Sayyidna Hamid al-Din, alladhi takawwanat minhu nufus (نُفُوس) al-nuqa wal-aimmah al-mayamin* — "which Sayyidna Hamid al-Din mentioned, from which were formed the souls of the pure ones and the blessed Imams." This *mizaj*, rising from the subtle water and becoming vapor, was mentioned by Sayyidna Hamid al-Din al-Kirmani: from these vapors the *nufus* (souls) of the *nuqa* (نُقَاء) (pure ones) and the Aimmah al-Mayamin are formed.

*wa amma qawluka Bir Dhat al-Alam* — "As for your statement, 'the Well of Dhat al-Alam'" — that is, the question concerning Bir Dhat al-Alam — *wa qatluhu Amir al-Muminin al-muazzam al-jinn* — "and the killing of the jinn by the magnificent Commander of the Faithful" — *alladhina zaharu lamma daahum sallallahu 'alayhi fa-wallaw wa ankaru* — "who appeared when he, (ع), called them, but they turned away and denied." When Amir al-Muminin extended *dawat* (invitation) to them, they denied it and turned their backs. *fa-dhalika huwa al-qawl al-wadih al-sarih* — "So that is the clear, explicit statement" — the matter is true, clear, and explicit: Amir al-Muminin gave *dawat* to these jinn, and they denied it. In Surah al-Jinn it is stated that the jinn accepted faith; those were other jinn, whose states are described in that Surah.

*wal-hadith al-marwi al-sahih* — "And the authentic, transmitted hadith." *wa qatala minhum man zahara fi suwar al-haywaniyyah* — "And he killed from among them those who appeared in animal forms" — those who appeared in corporeal forms, he killed. *wa akhmada man tajarrada minhum fi surat al-zulmaniyyah* — "And he extinguished those among them who were divested in the form of darkness" — those in the *zulmani* (dark) form rather than the *haywani* (حَيَوَانِيّ) (animal) form, being *mujarrad* (مُجَرَّد) (abstract) among them, he destroyed and rendered motionless, *bil-hujaj al-wadihah* — "with the clear proofs" — *wal-barahin al-laihah* — "and the evident demonstrations" — for against those dark forms too the *hujaj* (حُجَج) (proofs) were established. *wa aslama minhum kathir* — "And many among them submitted" — many among them accepted faith and became Muslim (مُسْلِم). *wa la yunabbiuka mithlu khabir* — "And none can inform you like one who is well-acquainted" — no one else conveys these matters as they truly are.

*wa amma al-mawaqit fa-fiha asrar batinah wa zawahir kaminah* — "As for the miqats, in them are hidden secrets and latent manifestations." The *miqat* (مِيقَات) — the appointed place for entering ihram — holds hidden secrets and latent manifestations.

The miqats are five. The most excellent is Dhu al-Hulayfah, which is also called Bir Ali and Masjid al-Shajarah. It is the highest miqat, and the Hajj that the Prophet ﷺ performed — the single Hajj of the tenth year of Hijri — was commenced from this miqat. Great events are connected with it. The Prophet had taken Sayyidah Aishah with him, and she had her period at that time. She lamented greatly, and the Prophet said: "When Allah has made you weak and has made you deficient, then you must bear it," admonishing her firmly. He then moved forward, and many incidents occurred with his companions at the miqat.

All *riyat* and *fudulat* pass through the miqat. If a person does not enter ihram at the miqat and proceeds beyond it, he must turn back, return to the miqat, and make the *'aqd-e-niyyat* (firm intention) there. Such is the importance of the miqat: neither Hajj nor Umrah can take place without it, for the miqat is the *bab* (بَابٌ) (gate) to both.

The sixth miqat is a temporary one — Tanim, which is in fact Masjid Ali. It has become very grand; in earlier times it was a very small masjid. It is temporary in the sense that if a person arrives on the eighth of Dhu al-Hijjah with no time to reach the distant miqats such as al-Sail, he may make his *niyyat* (intention) at Tanim and join the pilgrimage. If he has still less time, he should proceed directly to 'Arafat, for standing at Arafat is what completes the Hajj. If a person does not reach Arafat, his Hajj is incomplete and has not been performed.

'Arafat is attributed to the Prophet. Three stations are visited: Mina, the main station, which belongs to Amir al-Muminin; Arafat, which belongs to Mawlana Rasulullah; and Muzdalifah, which belongs to Sayyidah Bibi Fatimah al-Zahra.

*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Allahumma ashraf al-fatiha hadiyyatan wa wasilatan saniyyatan zakiyyatan mardiyyatan ila arwah al-khamsat al-ashbah al-athar wa ila ruh Sayyidina Ali bin Hibatullah qaddas Allahu ruhahu wa anwara darihahu wa daafa 'alayna min barakatihi wa lazzatihi wa tayidatihi wa taqabbal minna ya Allah al-ifadah wal-istifadah bi-ahsani ma yakun bi-haqqi Sayyidina Muhammadin wa alihi ajmain wa sallim. Al-Fatihah.*

<!-- editorial:begin -->
> **Editorial note (tradition-grounded).** 
> A note for the reader: the detail that Amir al-Muminin fought the jinn of Bir Dhat al-Alam by
> reciting and breathing Quranic verses upon them gains depth from a related teaching preserved
> in this tradition, which derives the word Quran from a root meaning "connection," signifying
> that the Quran is bound to the People of the House (Ahl al-Bayt). On that reading, recitation
> in the hand of the Wasi is not an incantation borrowed from outside himself but the sounding of
> something already joined to him. The same tradition speaks of "luminous remembrances" (anwar
> dhikriyya) multiplying wherever emanation occurs — and the root of dhikr, ذ-ك-ر, carries one
> idea in two directions at once: to hold something in the mind and to speak it aloud. Recitation
> and inward awareness are thus a single act, which may help explain why breath and blade are
> named together as one manner of fighting.
<!-- editorial:end -->

## 16. Harun to Musa: The Successor Who Dies Before the Prophet

A questioner raised a matter concerning the saying of Mawlana Zayd, upon him be the blessings of Allah, in the supplication for Wednesday. The supplication reads: *wa bayanihi fi al-manzilah* (and his statement in the station), and speaks of those who were second, *wa irsalihi wa muqasimuhu fi kulli shan* (and his sending and his divider in every matter). The questioner asked what this means, since the phrase stands in a supplication and its explanation is sought.

The supplication continues: *wa hayat al-manqul fi hayatihi* (and the life of the one who is transferred in his lifetime) — that is, the passing of one who dies during the lifetime of another. *Manahu Harun* (its meaning is Harun). *Wa kana nuqlatuhu Harun qabla Musa* (and his transfer was Harun before Musa). The one "transferred" is Harun, for Harun passed away before Musa. And the Prophet (صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ وَسَلَّمَ) said:

> *'Aliyyun minni bi-manzilati Haruna min Musa*

Ali holds toward him the same station that Harun held toward Musa. *Fa-la shakka anna nur al-azim 'Aliyyun (عَلِيٌّ) intaqala fi hayati nabiyyihi al-karim* (so there is no doubt that the great light, Ali, passed in the life of his noble Prophet). It is therefore beyond doubt that the great light of Ali passed away during the life of the Prophet.

Sayyidna Ismail ibn Hibatullah also noted that the awsiya (legatees) pass during the lives of their anbiya (prophets). The pattern holds for the aimmah as well: the passing occurs within the life of the imam or prophet they serve. So the passing of Ali (ع) too occurred in the life of the Prophet. Outwardly it did not happen in this way, but in reality it did.

The questioner continues: *bi-tafaddul wa-tatawwul* (with favor and bounty) — asking for favor and kindness. *Fa-qad akthartu* (for I have asked much): he has been asking many questions. *Wa ana asaluka* (and I ask you), with the plea that the answer not be given in a single sentence, for otherwise doubt will remain in him. *Wa maqal al-summa* (and a deafening statement): a word given as a mere concise phrase will not satisfy him. *Wa man yaftair sail* (and who is it that asks but a beggar): the one who asks is a beggar. *Wa man saala dhalla* (and he who asks is humbled): the one who asks is humbled. *Wa anta al-mukhtar fi al-itai wa al-mana* (and you have the choice in giving and withholding): the choice to grant or to refuse belongs to the one being asked, who may answer or decline. *Wa bayyin li* (and clarify for me): explain the supplication of Mawlana Zayd — the supplication already mentioned, whose praise is confirmed by his actions, pertaining to the fourth circle of power. It has been written that aid was given in his actions within that fourth power. What, then, are these circles? *Hal hiya min al-uqul* (are they from the intellects?), and to what does this point? *Wa qawl al-natiq li-Kalilah, istaqbili* (and the saying of the Natiq to Kalilah: "Come forward"): the saying of the *Natiq* — that is, of Rasulullah — to Kalilah, meaning to the jackal: "Come to me." *Al-aql al-khamis, sahib al-nur al-azam al-anwar, mufid al-khayrat wa muti al-fadail* (the fifth intellect, the owner of the greatest and most luminous light, the bestower of good things and the giver of virtues) — the one who benefits with charities and gives graces and great boons. *Ila an qala... zaharat al-qudar al-Fatimiyyah* (until he said... the Fatimi (فَاطِمِيّ) powers appeared). *Wa minhu ashraqat badayi al-suwar al-musawwarah* (and from him the splendors of the pictured forms shone forth) — its new matters. *Wa qad kharajtu fi suali min mana fi mana* (and I have digressed in my question from one meaning to another). *Awjaba al-hal... kasrat al-sual* (the situation necessitated... the abundance of questioning): his state is such that he asks more and more. *Wa antum min ahl al-ijzal* (and you are among the people of generosity): among the people of great generosity. The writer is himself very accomplished.

*Al-jawab 'ala dhalika al-sual* (the answer to that question). Sayyidna Ali ibn Hibah says: here is the answer to the question that has been asked. *Wa naqulu bi-awnillahi al-kabir al-mutaal* (and we say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Exalted), *wa minnati wali al-asr wa al-zaman* (and with the grace of the Master of the Age and Time), *alayhi salawat al-rahim al-rahman* (upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate), *wa tayid mawlaya al-alam al-alam al-anwar al-sirr al-bahir* (and the support of my master, the most learned scholar, the most luminous, the manifest secret). With the support of Mawlana Hibatullah — most luminous, whose secret is a luminous secret — this answer is given. *Al-muwasil li-suwar* (the one who connects to the forms): such a secret as is obtained by the forms. *An qawli Mawlana Zayd... salamu alayhi, al-malik al-ashir* (regarding the saying of Mawlana Zayd, (ع), the tenth king). By *al-malik al-ashir* is meant the Sayyid Dai. *Fa-dhalika huwa al-sirr al-khafi* (for that is the hidden secret): the matter pertains to a great and hidden secret, *alladhi la yustar bil-aqlam* (which is not written with pens) — not committed to writing. That is the nature of the question. *Wa la yulqa illa min lisanin ila udhun* (and it is not imparted except from a tongue to an ear): it is transmitted only from tongue to ear, not set down in writing, *mimman ala lahum al-maqam* (from those whose station is high).

*Wa dhalika annahu ilahun al-mufaddal al-awwal al-sabiqah fi al-adwar* (and that is because he is the favored god, the preceding first one in the cycles). He is the most favored, the first, the preceding, who has appeared across the cycles of old. As the Prophet said, *ana ibn al-karrat* (I am the son of the recurring appearances): he kept appearing through those recurring appearances, and Ali was always with him, appearing alongside. *Istakhlafat al-mim wa muwazin laha fi khafi al-asrar* (the *mim* became the successor, and it is its equivalent in the hidden secrets). The *mim* assumed the successorship *fi khafi asrarihi* (in his hidden secrets). *Wa kadhalika al-ayn idh ma istakhlafat al-qubbat al-Muhammadiyyah* (and similarly the *'ayn* (عَيْن), when it became the successor of the Muhammadan dome). In the same way the *ayn*, the *azimah* (عَزِيمَة), became the successor of the Muhammadan dome. *Wa raqa ila ufuq al-ashir* (and he ascended to the tenth horizon): having assumed the successorship, he ascended to the horizon of the tenth. *Al-munfarid bil-ahadiyyah* (the one unique in his uniqueness): he became unique in his oneness, with none like him.

A clarification arises here: did the *'ayn* take the successorship of the *mim*? Yes, the *ayn* took the successorship of the *mim*. The word there does not refer to the physical entity. Sometimes it refers to the *hijab*, and sometimes when the *muhtajib* is spoken of, the *hijab* is meant. The *mim* took the successorship from the *ayn*.

For the *muhtajib* of Ali, whose name is *'ayn*, is superior (أَفْضَل) to the *muhtajib* of Muhammad the Arab (صلى الله عليه وآله وسلم). Yet among the veils (حِجَابَات), the veil (حِجَابٌ) of the Prophet is superior to the veil of Ali.

Mawlana says regarding the three generations (الْقُرُونُ الثَّلَاثَةُ) that exist: they are the Messenger of God and Mawlana Ali. He says that Ali gathered the aimmah — from Hunayd to Hakim, and from Hasan to the Qaim — gathering them all. This was a rare thing: before him, no Imam had gathered another Imam who would come after him. But this is the station of Ali, that he is the first and the last (الْأَوَّلُ وَالْآخِرُ). "I am the first, and I am the last." He gathered the first ones and also gathered those who would come after. The three envoys (ثَلَاثَةُ سُفَرَاء) he did not gather in this same manner. The Prophet gathered Ali, and Ali too was gathered; it is a matter between them. As for the successorship of the *'ayn-i azimah* (عَيْنِ عَظِيمَةٍ), the first to assume it was Mawlana Abd al-Muttalib, after him Abu Talib (ع), and with Abu Talib was Abdullah (ع), *indallah* (with God). After that, the third successorship was taken by Ali, or by the Messenger; the Messenger took it. These successorships continue in this way, like a conjunction (قِرَانٌ). Abdullah is said to be *indallah*: Abdullah's station is with Allah, meaning with Abu Talib.

*Wa ihtajab bi-hijabi mim al-Maryamah* (and he was veiled by the veil of the *mim* of Mary). He adopted absence (غَيْبَة) through the veil of *mim-i Maryamah* (مِيمِ مَرْيَمَة). That is, the apparent passing of the Messenger was not truly the passing of the Messenger; rather, it was the passing of Ali's veil that occurred. In the remaining life that followed, the veil of Ali was at work. After the apparent passing of the Messenger, the one who appeared as Prophet lived for a further thirty years, and there was a gap of thirty years between the Prophet (ص) and Ali. Those thirty years during which he remained alive belonged to the veil of the Prophet and not to the veil of Ali. This is a rare matter.

*Wa thabata al-mim bi-hijab al-ayn* (and the *mim* was established by the veil of the *'ayn*). The *mim* remained established through the veil of the *ayn*. This event occurred on the day of Ghadir.

Is there proof for this in the noble Quran? Yes. The noble Quran states that the wife of the Messenger of God cannot remarry; no one else may marry her, for she is *Umm al-Muminin* (Mother of the Believers). Yet the Prophet (ص) had given Ali the choice that after his passing, Ali might divorce 'Aishah if the situation were as it is. Can one say this is possible? The intellect cannot grasp such things — not while he is working as the soul of the Messenger (نَفْسُ الرَّسُولِ). For this reason, in the *Mubahalah* as well:

> *Taalaw abnaana wa abnaakum wa nisaana wa nisaakum wa anfusana wa anfusakum*

Come, let us call our sons and your sons, our women and your women, and ourselves and yourselves. For "our sons and your sons" the Messenger brought Hasan and Husayn. For "our women and your women" there were several wives, yet he brought not one of them; instead he brought Fatimah al-Zahra (ع) on his own accord. And for "ourselves and yourselves" — meaning "bring our own people," *anfus* signifying one's own self — he brought Ali. This made clear that Ali was the soul of the Messenger.

*Talbisan 'ala qasir al-suwar* (as a deception upon those of limited forms). This deception was created for those who were weak-minded — those of limited forms. *Wa-ghusnan ala fi midajihi qudur* (and a branch upon... in his disposition... powers). Those who had powerful dispositions did not understand what had occurred. These states could be understood only by the highest-ranking believers.

*Wa kama azhara Mawlana Jafar al-Sadiq al-ghaybah bi-hijab waladihi Ismail* (and just as Mawlana Jafar al-Sadiq manifested the absence through the veil of his son Ismail). This is a well-known matter. Ismail (ع) passed away, but in reality it was Mawlana Jafar al-Sadiq who passed away through his son Ismail. In the gathering where Ismail had passed away and his body lay there, Imam Jafar al-Sadiq (ع) said, "Who will become my ransom (فِدْيَة)?" All the *hudud* of the *dawah* present — very high-ranking *hudud* — came forward saying, "O Mawla, we will, we will." But Furat ibn Ahmad, a person of the *hudud* of the *dawah*, sat with his head bowed in humility, thinking: what is my station and greatness, that the Imam, addressing a gathering in which he too was present, should ask who would become his veil (حِجَابٌ)? This humility was not hidden from the Imam. So he said, *Qum ya Furat* (Arise, O Furat). At that moment he recited the verse of Surah al-Muddaththir:

> *Ya ayyuha al-muddaththir, qum fa-andhir. Wa rabbaka fa-kabbir. Wa thiyabaka fa-tahhir*

O you who are wrapped in garments, arise and warn. And glorify your Lord. And purify your garments.

As soon as this verse was recited, people began greeting him: "Ya Imam al-Zaman" — O Imam, upon you be peace. When the Imam recited a verse upon a *hudud* of the *dawah*, that person's external garb was transformed. What state, then, must have been created when Jibril (ع), on behalf of Allah, came and recited this very verse upon the Messenger. *Kulla yawmin huwa fi shan* (every day He is in a new matter). Every day brings a new station. The master also says in one place that he never spent a single day without an increase in his knowledge: the knowledge he had yesterday was increased today. This was the state of Rasulullah — a state of great magnificence.

Mawlana Jafar al-Sadiq said to Furat, at the end: "I am you, and you are me." Something similar occurred in another Imam's time. A Sindhi man had come for the *ziyarah* of the Imam and was sitting in his presence. The Imam asked the gathering who would become his ransom, and everyone presented themselves and stood. But the Sindhi sat quietly. The Imam addressed him: "Why did you not stand?" And then he said, *Anta ana wa ana anta* (you are me and I am you). At that moment the Sindhi brother became a veil.

In Sindh too, if the historical record is examined, many scholars have passed and remarkable things occurred. In the time of Sayyidna Daud ibn Ajabshah, a person came to Ahmedabad who was an Agha Khani in religion and a horse merchant by trade — a very wealthy man whose name was Lad bin Dhannu. The word *Dhannu* is commonly written in that region and means rich (غَنِيّ), *dhan-wala*, one with wealth. Lad bin Dhannu came to Ahmedabad in connection with his horse trade. He was, however, a man of knowledge. Sayyidna Daud ibn Ajabshah met him and was greatly impressed by his character and his learning. When the time for prayer came, Sayyidna Daud said, "You may pray your prayer separately, and we will pray ours separately." But Lad bin Dhannu said, "No, Mawlana, I will pray with you, in your congregation." He prayed in that congregation, and afterward he joined the Ahl al-Sharif. Not only did he join, but as he deepened his study he accomplished such a remarkable feat that it is said of him that he became the *dai* for the Jazira of Sindh. And it is also said of him that he was the جامع, the compiler and gatherer, of a great assembly of poets. These too are wondrous and remarkable states that have come to pass.

<!-- editorial:begin -->
> **Editorial note (tradition-grounded).** 
> *An aside for the reader.* The questioner here asks whether the "circles of power" belong to the
> intellects — a question this chapter answers only in part, since the matter is described as
> passing from tongue to ear rather than pen. A related teaching preserved in this tradition may
> help orient a modern reader: it recounts that when the seven intellects were established, the
> First Intellect manifested as radiant luminescence, and that all the intellects, witnessing that
> light, were astonished at His greatness and exaltation and acknowledged the First Intellect at
> that moment. Read alongside such an account, the language of a "fifth intellect" and of light
> bestowing good things belongs to an ordered spiritual hierarchy, not to isolated imagery.
<!-- editorial:end -->

## 17. The Fifth Intellect and the Verdict on the Caliphs

Maulana Ismail was seen in the veil of his father, and it became a mutual exchange — *fi sirrin jalilin*, in a great secret. *Wa lam yaqif 'ala ahl al-haqaiq tilka al-asrar*: the people of realities were not unaware of those secrets. *Wa kullu shayin indahu... inda rabbika bi-miqdar* — everything with the Lord is in a decreed measure. This supplication, *wa qawluhu fi hadha al-dua' al-madhkur fi shan Musa al-mashkur*, belongs to the station of the thankful Musa — that is, the supplication of Maulana Zayd — and it acts *bi-quwwat al-dairah al-rabiah*, by the power of the fourth circle, *al-mutashashiah anwaruha al-satiah*, whose brilliant and radiant lights spread in every direction.

The honorable Musa was the fourth among the *nutuqa* (نُطَقَاء), and the fourth is granted a special power. Among both the Imams and the *nutuqa*, the one who stands second and the one who stands fourth are each granted a particular strength. Among the Imams, Imam Husayn ibn Ali was granted a power by which he waged the greatest *jihad*. The fourth Imam, the honorable Baqir (ع), was granted a power by which he caused all the sayings of the honorable Prophet to be made manifest, expounding their interpretations and inner meanings. "O Baqir, split open knowledge," he was told — "O Muhammad, split open knowledge." That command became the source of his title. He conveyed this charge to Jabir ibn 'Abdullah al-Ansari, for the Prophet had said to Jabir: "My son's son — when you meet him, convey my peace to him." To convey peace is to convey the *tayid* and *mawaddah* that one carries; the bearer is the medium for it, and delivers it to its destination. Those were the circumstances.

Sayyidna Jabir ibn 'Abdullah al-Ansari was an elder of the very highest rank. He became a companion of the honorable Prophet and held great love for the honorable Ali. His deepest bond, however, was with the honorable Imam Baqir, and he lived an extraordinarily long life. On one occasion the Prophet said to him, "Go to that place." Jabir submitted, "O Mawla, I do not know that place; how should I go?" The Prophet gestured, pointing his finger, and from that finger a light emerged. "Follow this light," he said. "Wherever it is visible to you, go that way." By the guidance of that light Jabir set out on a long journey. He reached his destination, accomplished what he had been sent to do, delivered the Rasul's message, and completed his task. Afterward a thought came to him: long before, when he had once lost his way while carrying valuable goods, he had buried those goods beneath a bush somewhere nearby. He went to see whether that place still existed and whether the goods remained. He found the spot, dug the ground, and found the goods untouched. He took them with him, returned home, and then came before the Prophet. The Prophet said, "I sent you for one task, and you have returned having done two." Such was the honorable Jabir ibn Abdullah al-Ansari. His grave is in Alexandria, and a visitation was made there as well. He passed away in the time of the honorable Maulana Baqir, having lived a very long life, and he is visited to this day.

His son, 'Abdullah ibn Jabir, is in Iraq, in the mausoleum of Maulana Salman Farsi. Two are buried there. Maulana Tahir is also there — Tahir, the son of Maulana Baqir and brother of Imam Jafar al-Sadiq — and he too rests in Salman Pak, alongside Maulana Hudhayfah al-Yamani (حُذَيْفَةُ الْيَمَانِيُّ).

Hudhayfah al-Yamani holds a station of great significance. He was a companion of the Master and was present when, after the eighteenth — the day of Ghadir — Rasulullah undertook the journey toward Madinah. The hypocrites, the *munafiqin*, were burning with rage over the appointment of the honorable Ali, and they resolved to eliminate the Master himself. They formed a group, made their plan, and drew their daggers in the dark of night as the party traveled through a valley. By the power of God, Allah commanded the lightning to flash — and it flashed so continuously and so brightly that the Master was able to see every one of their faces. Hudhayfah al-Yamani stood with the honorable Prophet at that moment. The Prophet saw all their faces and said, "O Hudhayfah, did you see what I saw?" Hudhayfah replied, "O Rasulullah, what you saw, I saw." The Prophet said, "Keep this a secret; do not reveal it." And so Hudhayfah revealed it to no one. Yet the honorable 'Umar ibn al-Khattab would constantly press him: "Was I among that group that intended to kill the Prophet?" Hudhayfah held his silence — until finally Umar himself declared, *Wallahi, ana fihim*: "By God, I was among them." The question stands plain: men of that kind, who conspired to kill the Prophet, were they fit to become caliph?

A treatise by Nasir al-Din al-Tusi (نَصِيرُ الدِّينِ الطُّوسِيّ) addresses this question directly. In it, he dismantles the caliphs — and then Muawiyah and Yazid after them — with such precision that the work stands as essential reading. It is short, fifteen to twenty pages, yet the titles he applies to Abu Bakr are enough to make one's hair stand on end. The same holds for 'Umar and for Uthman.

As for whether Abu Bakr is counted among the *dais*, it is said that he was in the *dawah*. The reality with Abu Bakr is that from ancient times, from the earlier *dawah*, he had been a participant in the *dawah* of Abu Talib. He continued in that *dawah* and harbored a wish that he himself might be the awaited Prophet. When the Prophet ﷺ received prophethood and proclaimed the call of Islam, Abu Bakr accepted him — yet hypocrisy remained in his heart, and its expressions surfaced many times. One such expression was that the Master did not invite Abu Bakr along on the migration from Makkah to Madinah; the Prophet had intended to make the journey alone. Abu Bakr came along under the pretext of buying camels. He had learned of the plan because the Master, though he kept the matter of the migration secret, needed a fast camel and asked Abu Bakr for one. Abu Bakr agreed — but charged Rasulullah ten times the going rate for the camel.

Various such incidents are recorded. On one occasion Rasulullah was standing in the market and had struck a deal with a camel seller who had come from another city. The Prophet paid the agreed price and reached for the reins, saying, "Hand it over." The man said, "I will not hand it over. This camel is mine." A strange situation had arisen: the man had received his money but would not release the camel. At this point, according to the narrations, Abu Bakr and 'Umar were passing by. They asked, "O Rasulullah, what is the matter?" The Prophet explained: "This man came to sell a camel. I liked it, I paid the price, and now he will not hand it over." Abu Bakr and Umar then asked the Rasul, "Are you quite sure you paid the price?" At that the Prophet fell silent. By coincidence the honorable Ali also passed by and asked what was happening. The Master explained the same thing to Ali. Without another word Ali seized the man by the collar and said, "Return this camel." Afterward the Master asked Ali, "Why did you not do as the two Shaykhs did, and treat me the way they treated me?" Ali replied, "O Rasulullah, we have believed in you without seeing. We have not seen paradise, we have not seen hell, we have not seen the angels, we have not seen the hereafter, yet we have believed in all of it solely upon your word. Would we then not believe you in so small a matter as this?"

Many such incidents are recorded. One is the incident of the cave. Much is made of the fact that the honorable Rasul was with Abu Bakr in the cave, that Abu Bakr cradled the Prophet on his lap, and all manner of claims are advanced that do not hold up. The verse in Surah al-Tawbah says *idh huma fi al-ghar* — that they both were in the cave. This Allah acknowledges. And then: *fa-anzalallahu sakinatahu 'alayh* — Allah sent down His tranquility upon him. Two people were present, and tranquility descended upon one. No Muslim would dare to say that the tranquility — that special form of divine assistance — descended upon Abu Bakr while the honorable Prophet was left restless and helpless. Tranquility, then, descended upon the Rasul.

An objection might be raised: the Rasul is the Rasul and Abu Bakr is Abu Bakr; they are different, and of course tranquility descended upon the Prophet. But elsewhere — in Surah al-Fath and in Surah al-Tawbah itself — Allah says in another verse: *anzalallahu sakinatahu 'alayhi wa ala al-muminin* — Allah sent down His tranquility upon him and upon the believers. Tranquility descends upon the believers as well. Had Abu Bakr been among the believers, tranquility would have descended upon him too. A *dai* by the name of Shahriyar demonstrated this conclusively: since tranquility descends upon the believers, as the other verse makes plain, and no tranquility descended upon Abu Bakr in the cave, he was not even within the circle of faith. How, then, could such a person become caliph?

Then, when Rasulullah passed away, a gathering was convened at Saqifah Bani Saidah (سَاعِدَة) while the body of the Rasul lay unburied and without shroud. As Shaykh Sadi writes in his couplet: the dogs of the world, in their greed for worldly power, left the Prophet without his due.

There is also this point: as long as Muhammad ibn Abi Bakr was still in his father's loins, certain things were as they were. Abu Bakr's other son, 'Abd al-Rahman, and Muhammad ibn Abi Bakr — according to the narrations, both were absent in those moments. The narration specifically concerns Muhammad ibn Abi Bakr.

There is also the matter of the gap between the event of Ghadir and the Prophet's passing — two, three, perhaps four months — during which period history records that the Prophet dispatched Usamah (أُسَامَة) ibn Zayd on a military expedition, and these very men were placed under his command. They objected to being sent under the leadership of one so young; some said Usamah was seventeen, though the correct figure is twenty-eight and a half years old, the son of Harithah. They did not go. And Rasulullah, in his illness, kept asking by name: "Has Abu Bakr gone? Has 'Uthman gone? Has Umar ibn al-Khattab gone?"

The oath of allegiance was taken at Saqifah Bani Saidah — a place where thieves and robbers, after looting goods, would gather to divide their spoils among themselves. At that place the matter of the caliphate was decided. While the discussion was still underway, 'Umar ibn al-Khattab came forward at once and gave his oath of allegiance, declaring, "Whoever does not give the oath, my sword is on his head." That is how the oath was taken. God forbid. Today that place has been turned into a public bathhouse.

If this was a democracy, then in all three successions the principle of democracy was destroyed — in one case through nomination, in another through a council of six. It was all done in enmity toward Ali. The *dais* of high rank were not unaware of these matters. The ones the Prophet spoke of were indeed aware of all the sciences — Abu Bakr especially. But the greed of the world and the lust for rank led them to do all of this.

What happened to Muhsin bin Ali deserves reflection. He was so learned, so well read, that words fail to describe it. In the time of Sayyidna Husayn ibn Hasan, Sayyid 'Abdullah ibn Muhammad had been appointed, and Muhsin bin Ali stood behind Sayyidna Husayn ibn Hasan in prayer. Sayyid Abdullah ibn Muhammad would stand in such a way as to draw Muhsin close — half the space for himself and half for Muhsin. Such was their closeness. Sayyid Abdullah ibn Muhammad held such great friendship with Muhsin bin Ali that he always seated him at his side.

On Fridays, Sayyid Muhammad al-Jamali would come — he came only on Fridays, praying in his own mosques the rest of the time. Being senior, he would sit to the left of Sayyid Husayn bin Ismail, behind the Imam.

That was the time of Sayyidna Husayn. On a later occasion Muhsin was sitting behind him, and after Muhsin was Sayyid 'Abdullah. Once, when the summers began, the prayer was held in the courtyard. Sayyid Husayn bin Ismail led the Maghrib prayer. Then, in the interval between the two evening prayers, he sat awhile, rose, went into the prayer niche, and the congregation followed. He called Sayyid Abdullah Muhammad and said something to him, to which Sayyid Abdullah replied, "You pray your prayer, I will pray mine." At that point Muhsin led the prayer.

Affairs deteriorated considerably after that. Yet such had been the friendship between Sayyid 'Abdullah Muhammad and Muhsin. When the clarification came — after Sayyid sahib had tested Sayyid Abdullah Muhammad and Sayyid Ahmad al-Jamali for many years and then forgiven him — he performed the *nas* (appointment) and forgave him. So when Sayyid Abdullah ibn Muhammad became a *dai*, Muhsin came to him on the third day. He did not offer the full *musafahah* (مُصَافَحَة) (handshake of recognition); he merely shook hands and said, "You and I were at the same station." Sayyid Abdullah ibn Muhammad replied, "Yes, it is correct that you and I were at the same station. But from my station I have built a building. And you — you have remained on that same station." He had stayed where he was, while from that same station the *dai* had raised a great edifice of faith.

Abu Bakr was not among the confidants of the era of 'Isa. They are other individuals, outside the list of the righteous. Among the six names there are also common names. The teachers of the Rasul — and among them the confidants of the era of Isa, the six confidants who existed between the Rasul and Isa — were those who gathered the precious treasures of the teachings of Isa. Their joining occurred with the Rasul. Although they were the confidants of the era of Isa, they are also among the chosen ones of the *dawr* and hold a great station.

*Fa-hwa yushiru 'ala anna kulla aqlin min al-uqul al-sab' al-izam yanzuru ila man qabalahu min al-rutab al-nutuqa al-saba fi al-sarb wa al-nizam* — this indicates that every intellect among the seven great intellects looks toward the one opposite it among the seven ranks of the *nutuqa*, in their series and order.

Of the seven intellects: setting aside the First Intellect and the tenth, eight remain between them. From that count, the first emanated one — the one who became unique — is further excluded. Between the first emanated one and the tenth, then, stand seven intellects. Each of those seven gives material support to the one opposite it among the *nutuqa*. The third intellect supports the first among the *nutuqa*, that is, Adam (ع). The fourth intellect, the eternal one, supports the honorable Nuh. And so it proceeds.

*Wa amma qawl al-natiq li-Kalilah, thumma istaqbili al-aql al-khamis, dhu al-fadl wa al-fadilah, sahib al-nur al-azam al-anwar, mufid al-khayrat wa muti al-fadail, ila an qala 'alayhissalam wa al-salam minhu zaharat al-qudar al-Fatimiyyah* — this is the saying of the Natiq to Kalilah: "Then come forward to the fifth intellect, the possessor of grace and virtue, the possessor of the greatest and most luminous light, the bestower of good things and the giver of virtues," until he said (ع): "from him the Fatimi powers appeared."

*Fa-l-aql al-khamis 'arrafna fi hadha al-wajh wa al-mana huwa al-Husayn ibn Ali Amir al-Muminin, al-khamis min al-Nabi* — the fifth intellect, understood in this aspect and meaning, is al-Husayn ibn Ali Amir al-Muminin, the fifth from the Prophet. Within the *Panjatan-i Pak* (the Holy Five) — the honorable Prophet, the honorable Ali, the honorable Fatimah al-Zahra, Hasan, and Husayn — Husayn was the fifth. The reference is to this.

*Wa al-qudar al-Fatimiyyah, hum al-aimmah min awladihi* — the Fatimi powers are the *aimmah* from his progeny, that is, from the progeny of the honorable Imam Husayn. They are called the Fatimi powers.

*Wa awlad Maulana Fatim al-huda* — the children of our master Fatim, who guides.

*Wa minhum ashraqat badayi al-suwar al-musawwarat* — from them, that is from the *aimmah*, new and wondrous forms shone forth, and their advent radiated light.

*Wa bihim taharrakat al-mutaharrikat* — by them the movers moved. These are all spiritual matters and joinings to which the passage makes reference.

This passage is found in *Kanz al-Ulum*, the book written by Sayyidna Ibrahim ibn Muhammad al-Fad. There is also *Kanz al-Walad*, a different work. *Kanz al-Walad* was written by Sayyidna Ibrahim ibn Husayn al-Hamidi, the second *dai* after the first *dai* Sayyidna Dhuayb, and he wrote it for his son Sayyidna Hatim — to instruct a son who reaches the station of a *dai* in the things he must know. *Kanz al-Ulum*, by contrast, was written by Sayyidna Ibrahim ibn Muhammad al-Fad, who explains therein that the *aimmah* who appeared in succession from the progeny of the honorable Husayn ibn Ali are in one sense also called *Aimmah Fatimiyyin*, because they stand in awe and reverence of the station of the honorable Bibi Fatimah al-Zahra.

It is also said that whenever the honorable Fatimah came to the honorable Rasulullah, he would often rise and seat her in his own place. The reason was the gathering she carried with her — a gathering of such station that when the Master of the Universe perceived it, he would rise out of joy and to honor that gathering. That gathering was the gathering of the *muhtajibin* (مُحْتَجِبِينَ) of the *awsiya*, a most magnificent gathering. The honorable Fatimah had assembled the *muhtajibin* of all the *awsiya*, which caused the station of the *hijab* to increase. The states that belonged to Fatimah bint Muhammad were the states of the *hijab*, and when the station of the *muhtajib* increases, the station of the *hijab* increases accordingly.

Wherever the footsteps of the Rasul fall, there the footsteps of Fatimah will fall. The *khilafah* was established. Among the *duat*, there stands a question of awe and reverence: who should be appointed to the station of the *'ayn* — Bibi Fatimah al-Zahra or Ibrahim Khalilullah? Among them, who is the successor in the *khilafah* of the *ayn* after the Rasul? This matter has not been clarified. One of the *dain* (دَيْنٌ), Sayyidna Abdullah al-Ali, wrote a book in which — as the brother Mukarram Saifullah sahib had transcribed — he clarified that the successions of all the Imams until the Day of Judgment have been set out: who will become the successor of the *ayn-i azimah* after whom. A most magnificent book, in which every single point has been made clear.

*Wa bihim tajalla al-ayn al-azimah, 'ilmuhu, ulumuhu, wa biyutuhu, wa maqamatuhu al-fahimah* — through them, through the Imams of purity, the Great *Ayn* manifested: His knowledge, His sciences, His houses, and His exalted stations. The honorable Ali became the *muhtajib* for all the Imams, and they, the Imams, are His *hijab*. Such is the state, and such is the station of the Master of the Universe.

This matter — the station of the Master of the Universe and the station of the Imams — has been clarified in Surah al-Fatihah as follows. Sayyidna Hasan Khan bin 'Abd al-Malik observes that Allah began His word, after Bismillah, with *Al-hamdu lillahi rabb il-alamin* — All praise is due to Allah, the Lord of the worlds. *Al-hamd* is the *marifah* (definite) form; its *nakirah* (indefinite) form is *hamd*. The word *hamd* is composed of the letters *ha*, *mim*, and *dal*, with numerical values of eight, forty, and four respectively, giving a total of fifty-two. Fifty-two signifies the three — Muhammad, Ali, and Fatimah — together with the forty-nine Imams who extend to Abu al-Aimmah. After Abu al-Aimmah, the era of manifestation (دَوْرِ كَشْف) begins: the Mahdi of the end of time will come, and the manifestation will occur. The honorable Muhammad (ع), the honorable Ali, and the honorable Fatimah al-Zahra — these three, together with the forty-nine Imams — make fifty-two. In reciting *Al-hamdu lillahi rabb il-alamin*, they are recognized and affirmed.

God Almighty said: *Wa-bud rabbaka hatta yatiyak al-yaqin* — "and worship your Lord until there comes to you the certainty." Where the word *yaqin* (يَقِين) (certainty) is used, it also carries a reference to Amir al-Muminin. In the literal sense, *yaqin* means death: worship Allah until death. But it also means until the meeting with Ali, for after passing away the meeting will be with Ali, and he will ask questions.

As it is in the Quran, in Surah al-Zumar, the thirty-ninth surah: when the group is taken toward the heavens, they will be stopped and questioned. The commentators, both Sunni and Shia, hold that this question is the question of the love and *walayah* (وَلَايَة) of Ali — a position established by proof. In one's life, did one hold the correct *walayah* for Ali or not? That is the question that will be asked.

The Master said: if a person spends his entire life in worship in the blessed gap between the Rukn al-Asad and Maqam Ibrahim, such that his very joints become loose from the exertion, yet has no love for Ali in his heart, then Allah will cast him into hellfire headlong. For the essence of the matter is this: once, Rasulullah asked his companions, "What is the essence of faith?" Someone said prayer. The Rasul said, "No, prayer is not the essence of faith." Another said fasting, another said Hajj, another said jihad. For each the Rasul said, "Not this." Then the Rasul declared, "It is *walayah* and love," meaning the *walayah* for Ali is the essence of faith.

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. O Allah, convey the blessings of these holy offerings, a gift and a means, pure and pleasing, to the souls of the Holy Five, our masters Muhammad, Ali, Fatimah, Hasan, and Husayn. Accept from us this benefit and utilization. May Allah sanctify the soul of our master Ali ibn Hibatullah, and accept, O Allah, this benefit and utilization in the best way possible, by the right of our master Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, and bless and grant them peace. Al-Fatihah. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb il-alamin. Ar-rahman ar-rahim. Maliki yawm id-din. Iyyaka nabudu wa iyyaka nastain. Ihdina al-sirat al-mustaqim. Sirat alladhina anamta 'alayhim ghayril maghdubi alayhim wa la al-dallin. On behalf of Sayyidna, our master, the absolute Dai, the treasurer of the house of truth, Sharafuddin and Zeeshanuddin, Ghulam Husayn, son of Sayyidna Mansur the sanctified one, and for Walidna Nasrullah ibn Abdullah Hibatullah ibn Mansur the sanctified one, may Allah exalt his station, protect him and guard him, surround him and conceal him, suffice him and heal him from the evil of jinn and men, and from the evil of diseases, aches, pains, and calamities. From the evil of the treacherous workings of time and days, from the evil of the movements of hours and moments, and the glances of eyes, and the stumblings of feet, and the machinations of schemers. May He make good his end in goodness and well-being, protect him in the rest of his life, prolong his pure life, and guard him in the fortress of his lofty station. May He make his days pleasant in a good life and in the attainment of ranks and stations. O you who have been guided, may Allah grant us and you to be in the fortress of his dawah, and may He grant us and you the blessings of his stations. By the right of Sayyidna and our master, the Imam, the manifest one with magnificent authority, upon whom and upon his pure forefathers be blessings and peace. Al-Fatihah. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb il-alamin. Ar-rahman ar-rahim. Maliki yawm id-din. Iyyaka nabudu wa iyyaka nastain. Ihdina al-sirat al-mustaqim. Sirat alladhina anamta alayhim ghayril maghdubi alayhim wa la al-dallin.

## 18. Returning as You Left: The Doctrine of the Second Cycle

The sixth question was put in these words:

"I reflected within my soul that I fell short of drinking from the spring's source, that is, from the presence of the Dai. I was not in the presence of the Dai, and I did not acquire knowledge directly from him. I know that this was a sin and a failing on my part. And so I ask: will I return, in the fudla al-thaniyah, that is, after my passing, when my rajat (return) comes, in the second karrah (cycle), in the same state as those who are present today? Will I be as I am now? Be gracious, then, and inspire me with that which will remove this uncertainty from me, open up this understanding, and make it clear. Lift from me every concealed veil.

I hasten to seek forgiveness and to repent for my sins. Be gracious unto me with guidance and with knowledge. Cause me to be allotted a Hajj, an opportunity to perform a Hajj of one of the Hudud (ranks of the Dawah), so that my sins may be forgiven. Or let me perform some action that will release me from the hudud in which I am bound. Or let me intend to go to the Baytullah (House of God) and reside therein for years, in which I may suffice and abide by the command. And we seek forgiveness from Allah, for I ask about a matter of which I have no knowledge. Unless You forgive me and have mercy upon me, I shall be among the ignorant. I seek refuge in Your pleasure from Your wrath, and in Your forgiveness from Your anger."

The answer to that question proceeds with the help of Allah the Great, the Most High, and with the grace of the Wali al-Asr wa al-Zaman (Guardian of the Age and Time), may the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate, be upon him, and through the support of the master — most learned, most luminous, and most radiant, whose secret flows into the subtleties of forms.

On the question of the fudla al-thaniyah — whether a person returns in the same condition as he held in past cycles — the answer is yes. As a man stands in this karrah at the moment of his death, so shall he be born in the next. He returns as he was at the beginning. Each rank a soul held is the rank to which it returns:

- If he was a Bab — that is, Bab al-Abwab — in the previous karrah, he will be born as a Bab in this karrah as well, for the people of the milla.
- If he was a Hujjah, he will hold the rank of Hujjah here too.
- If he was a Dai, he will be born as a Dai. He allots the Hujjahs and allots the guided ones, and this clarifies the principle.
- If he was a Madhun, he will come as a Madhun — though progressing in rank after being born at that station is a separate matter, for it is the work of Qudrat (Divine Power) to place one at the rank of Madhuniyyat.
- If he was a Mukasir (مُكَاسِر), he will be a Mukasir in this karrah too, upon this foundation.
- If he passed away as a Mumin, it is incumbent upon Qudrat that when he is born in the second karrah, he will be brought to the level of a Mumin.

Those whose rajat occurs quickly die in childhood, and that is a different situation. Such souls, it has been said, did not have the opportunity to take the 'ahd-e sharif (sacred covenant), or their lifespan did not permit it. When they come in future karrahs, they will be born in the state of iman.

This rank does not shift. A soul does not move from one rank to another — born at one station and arriving at a different one. It does not happen that way. There will be neither increase nor decrease in the pattern. The vehicle keeps moving upon this very design. A soul is also born in the same locality or town from which it originated — whichever city, whichever land it was born in and grew up in. This is the course set by the word of Allah the Exalted, and birth in this karrah will be in that same place. As Allah Taala says:

"From it We created you, and into it We will return you, and from it We will bring you forth another time."

This verse of the Holy Quran is recited when the deceased is lowered into the grave and soil is placed upon him. "From it We created you" — We created you from this very soil. "And into it We will return you" — you return into this soil, into this grave. "And from it We will bring you forth another time" — We will bring you out from this very place.

Once, someone asked Amir al-Muminin (ع): "O Mawla, in the state of prayer — the two prostrations, the two sajdahs, that we perform — what is the reason for them?" He replied: "Have you read this verse of the Holy Quran?" And he repeated it: "From it We created you, and into it We will return you, and from it We will bring you forth another time." He explained that when Allah Taala said, "From it We created you" — meaning, "I created you from this soil" — that is an indication toward the first prostration. And "Into it We will return you" — meaning, "I will merge you back into this soil" — points to the second prostration.

A man will be born in the era of whichever Imam al-Zaman's time it was in the previous karrah, and in the time of the Dai of that era — the Dai in whose time he was born. They will not respond except to the one who called them. They will not have iman, and will not respond, except through the very one who called them in the past. They will not obey anyone except the one who guided them.

There followed an exchange upon this point.

It was asked: regarding increase or decrease — given that the same Imam al-Zaman and the same Dai in whose time one is born set the frame, will the nashist (era, sitting) that is to come align precisely with the ages?

The answer given: the Imam has progressed in rank, but his niyabat (representation) remains established. Whoever becomes his khalifah (successor) and his naib (deputy) is the Imam. That is the meaning.

It was said: that part is understood. But the era and the nashist that will come, according to age, will not align exactly. The era and the age differ for each individual.

The reply: some died at a young age.

It was said: young age, old age — take, for example, the Imam's age, the Dai's age, the Mumin's age, or the age of the other Hudud-e Kiram. The nashist spoken of as sitting according to time will not align precisely with the period. For instance, if the rajat happened after a thousand years, the Imam at that time would be different, and the answer is that it is through his niyabat.

The reply: yes, in his niyabat. Since it has not been clarified further, it is possible that after a thousand years, whichever Imam one appears with, that one would be the khalifah of that Imam — or it lies within the khilafat (خِلَافَة) established among the Imams.

It was said: a statement came earlier that the thousand-year period of returning is a general principle, and that as the ranks increase, the period of their rajat increases — one coming in a thousand years, or two thousand, or five thousand.

The reply: no, that is fixed, for the Mumin as well as for the non-Mumin. That thousand-year period is fixed: after a thousand years, one must come again a second time. That is established. There is no change in it.

It was asked whether the Ahl al-Nass are separate — the duat, whose rajat is delayed.

The reply: no, for if the rajat is delayed, then what of the Muminin? The Muminin will be born in the era of the Imam or Dai with whom they had their 'ahd (عَهْد) or wilayat (allegiance) — in that very era, with whoever they had iman in the previous era, upon his hand.

It was asked: for the duat, whose ranks increase very quickly and whose time is short, is their rajat also after a thousand years?

The reply: yes, for the duat, though their khalasi (liberation) happens very quickly. As has been said, the Imam (ع) will not remain more than three days — his rajat being within three days and no longer. Similarly, the duat remain for thirty years, their period in the grave being thirty years, after which they return. For the Muminin it is three hundred years. That is the situation. But that concerns their progress. As for the rajat itself, not all things of the duat progress completely; some things are left behind, and Qudrat then brings them again for purification. It is the same for the Mumin.

So too with poverty and honor: whether in poverty or in a state of honor, in disgrace or lowliness rather than exaltation. Some live life with great intelligence and cleverness and become eloquent and articulate; others find their eloquence diminished, their intellect weak and light, and the sharpness of mind absent. All of this occurs according to the measure of what was present in a person in the fudla al-ula (first form). His actions cast their reflection here as well — what his hands wrought and sent forth. According to the measure of the righteous deeds he has performed in obedience to the Hudud, his reward is from the One Worshipped, from Allah Taala. And according to the measure of the good deeds he has done with his Mumin brother — meeting him with cheerfulness and welcome, with deep humility, teaching him whatever he possesses, guiding him, and striving to elevate him to the highest of ranks.

And according to the standing and the advice offered with sincere intentions, and the manner in which a person stands and leads his life with sincere intentions. And according to the service rendered in the Dawahs — that is, in the Dawat-e Hadiyah. And according to the measure of what a man does for the people of the ranks — the people of high ranks who are worthy, the Hudud of the Dawah — with whom he performs good deeds, according to the time and the moment, the pressing need, and the situations that arise. And likewise the land-tax and the ransoms: in this way he gives his fiqaqat (ransoms), through which salvation comes. Through these fiqaqat his salvation happens, and success, and arrival at the permanent abode of felicities. He succeeds, and he reaches the abode of permanent felicities — the destination.

The fiqaq is also of great importance, because what happens through fiqaq is that the defects and impurities within the nafs namiyah (نَفْسٌ نَامِيَة) (vegetative soul), nafs hissiyah (نَفْسٌ حِسِّيَّة) (sensory soul), and nafs natiqah (نَفْسٌ نَاطِقَةٌ) (rational soul) — the flaws within them — are removed. Just as zakat purifies wealth and purifies souls, so fiqaq too purifies these souls — namiyah, hissiyah, and natiqah.

It was asked whether the ahl-o-ayal (family) also stay together — whether their rajat occurs along with everyone else's, the family and the children coming together.

The reply: that is not mentioned here. But the matter will be the same. The same ones who had come with the Imam or Dai will also come. The word itself has not been used, yet for one who is a mumin and is upon the haqq (truth) — and for all those who are upon the haqq — the matters that happen with the people of haqq are to be understood in this way: his track becomes completely different. Then the question arises of how his accounts will be settled, when he will come, and when he will come again.

What has been said regarding this is as follows. Take a person who is blind and a kafir (non-believer). A mumin brother sees this kafir crossing the road and, considering it a work of neki (good deed), helps him across — without asking, "What is your name? What is your religion?" He performed a good deed from a purely human perspective. In this way, various works keep happening in the world, and such nekiyan (good deeds) keep accumulating. The question then is how a person's full hisab kitab (reckoning) will be settled for such good deeds, since the reward — the jaza — of good work is to be given to the mumin. It has been said that for those who are ghair-e-iman (without faith), in the second realm, in the other chamber, their good deeds are dealt with in this manner: sometimes it also happens that they had performed good deeds for the mumin, and so from the good deeds of such ghair-e-momineen, water is made, and after being made water, it is brought into use — into the use of wudu (ablution) and other purposes — and in this way the accounts between them are settled. This too has been said. So the matters that happen at the hands of the Imams, and how many such matters happen, the accounts are fully settled.

But that is the positive side — he was helped. There are also negative ones. Qisas (retribution) also happens. Of this Allah Taala says:

"And for you in legal retribution there is life, O you of understanding."

That is: in qisas is life, O people of understanding. The circle of qisas is vast, and life runs on a very small track — a small line — within it.

Imam Ali Zayn al-Abidin is famous for having said, "I am afraid of Allah that I may not get caught in small sins." And he gave this example: "I used to watch my mother light a fire using small pieces of wood, from which the fire would ignite. In that same way, I am afraid that I might be caught by small, small sins." Although the Imam is masum (infallible) and has no mistakes or sins, this becomes a seerah — an exemplary way of life — and thus the seerah of the Imams is formed.

## 19. Offerings, Trials, and the Making of a Dai

Another matter concerns the wife of an Imam named Aziza Bu. Her grave stands in the rawdah (رَوْضَةٌ) (tomb) of Maulana Shahid Saheb Muqaddas. She was Aziza Bu bint Qasim Khan, the zawjah (wife) of Muhammad Ali bin Swaleh Badri — the first mansoob (high-ranking official), who is buried in Ghash. Her wealth was extraordinary: she held trade in seven cities. From the time of Sayyidna Hibatullah, she sent her fitrah (religious dues) each year — not merely the prescribed amount, but a thousand times it. Whatever sum the Dai had set as fitrah for that era, she sent a thousand times that figure. Year after year, for many years, this is what she sent to Sayyidna Saheb. The mansoob of that time was instructed to seek her counsel and keep her in his consultations. Shaykh Yasin, who held the position of mansoob in that era, was told that no affairs of the dawat (mission) were to be conducted without the counsel of Sayyidati Aziza Bu. Because he did not heed his wife's counsel and went astray, he was ultimately removed from the dawat. Removed. Such was the station she held.

On the subject of those who go astray, Amir al-Momineen has a saying: do not look at how those who went astray did so, but see how those who were saved were saved. This is his saying, and in a poem he expressed the same thought: see how those who were saved were saved, and give attention to that.

Many such incidents are recorded. Dawood bin Fee was a scholar of great standing, but his flaw was this: he said, "I am greater in knowledge than Sayyidna Hibatullah Saheb." The nass (نصّ) for Sayyidna Hibatullah Saheb had been performed by Sayyidna Muhammad bin Ismail, whose uncle Sayyidna Hibatullah Saheb was. The lineage runs: Sayyidna Muhammad bin Ismail bin Muhammad bin Ibrahim bin Muhammad bin Fahd. Sayyidna Ibrahim's son was Ismail, and nass had been performed upon him, but Sayyidna Ismail passed away during his father's lifetime. Nass was therefore performed upon his grandson, Muhammad bin Ismail, who holds a very great station. Among the fifty-four or fifty-five Duat (plural of Dai), he is the only one who led prayers outside the Arabian Peninsula (kharij-e-jazirah), for Sayyidna Muhammad bin Ismail led prayers in Habasha (Abyssinia) by the Imam's command. He then designated his uncle, Sayyidna Hibatullah bin Ibrahim, performing nass upon him.

At that time Dawood bin Fee was in Najran, and his claim was that he surpassed Sayyidna Hibatullah in knowledge. Sayyidna Hibatullah Saheb had written a letter to be read out to the ikhwan (إِخْوَان), but Dawood bin Fee did not make it public — he did not read it out. Later, some of the ikhwan traveled to Sayyidna Saheb's side, and Arabs came as well, for in the time of Sayyidna Hibatullah there was much coming and going, many migrants passing through. Through this it came to light that the letter had never been read at all. Censure (عِتَاب) was then sent down. Later he repented — Dawood bin Fee repented — and Sayyidna Saheb forgave him, keeping him within the order of the faithful, among the momineen. He was given leave to return, but on his way he passed away at a place called Wasit. He died on the road.

Abdul Majid, Dawood Qutub, and others went astray in the time of the Imams. These were people of great station, and yet they fell away. The one who progressed and was saved was Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd. Sayyidna Sulayman Saheb had performed nass-e-sharif (noble designation) upon Sayyidna Jafar, but had left the name of the mustawda (trustee) blank, saying he would clarify it later. When he performed the second nass-e-sharif, he wrote the name of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd. The hallmark of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd was that he took charge of the dawat and held firm; his feet did not waver at all. For this he is called Ghadab fil Dawat (Zeal in the Mission). The jaza given to him was this: because of the way he had cared for Sayyidna Jafar and safeguarded the dawat — Sayyidna Jafar had been only eleven years old at the death of Sayyidna Sulayman Saheb, and Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd had taught him, educated him, and made him capable — the reward given was that Duat would continue in his progeny until Qiyamah (قِيَامَة) (the Day of Resurrection). This jaza was given.

The same applies to Sayyidna Ali bin Muhammad bin Walid, who is the fifth Dai. They were very great elders. The jaza given to him was this: Sayyidna Ali bin Hatim studied with him. That era had many wars, particularly with the Zaydis. In those wars, Sayyidna Hatim, qaddasallahu rawahahu, performed nass-e-sharif on Sayyidna Ali bin Muhammad Walid. Sayyidna Ali bin Muhammad Walid said to him, "Maulana, please reconsider. Why not perform nass on your own son?" Sayyidna Hatim replied, "Brother, you know that we did not get a chance to prepare our son for this position, because all our time was spent in wars and their concerns; in that preparation the time passed." He said, "You should at least test your son." The Dai then administered a very difficult test. Sayyidna Ali bin Hatim was asked questions of the highest level, and it was seen that he possessed great substance (جَوْهَرٌ). He gave all the answers. Sayyidna Hatim bin Ibrahim was left speechless. He asked Sayyidna Ali bin Muhammad Walid, "How did he acquire this knowledge?" He said, "Maulana, during the wars that used to happen, we were occupied by day — but there was not a single night, not a single night, that he slept without my teaching him and his studying." A Dai had been prepared on the battlefield. In light of this, Sayyidna Hatim then performed nass. And then Sayyidna Ali bin Hatim performed nass upon his own teacher. The goodwill shown, with sincerity of intention, had this jaza: from his progeny the Duat of Bani Anf continued — seventeen Duat in all. Today's Dai is approximately the nineteenth or twentieth in descent from Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd. Sayyidna Ahmed is also from that progeny, from the progeny of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd. Sayyidna Ali bin Hasim is also from his progeny — the twentieth or twenty-first Dai. Sayyidna Taher Saifuddin would often recite this verse for them:

> *Antum al-anf wa ghayrukum al-dhanab*

"You are the nose, and others are the tail." He would often recite it for them.

The name Anf (nose) carries its own story. Anf means nose. In the time of Maulana Maadd al-Mustansir, the eighteenth Imam, a man from Yemen used to come who enjoyed great closeness to the Imam. He would stay with the Imam for a few days, then return to Yemen. The ministers there — men of a kingdom, close to the Imam themselves — were astonished: "We are so close to the Imam, but who is this person who becomes even closer to the Imam than we are? The Imam honors him so much." One day they asked the Imam, "Who is this person whom you honor so highly and show such regard?" The Imam said:

> *Hadha anf min Yemen*
