"This is a nose from Yemen." His name was Ali bin Ibrahim, but the name Anf came from the Imam's own words. His progeny thereafter were called Bani Anf. That was in the time of the eighteenth Imam, and many centuries later these became the Bani Anf.

Their story also connects to a moment from the time of the Rasulullah. During a jihad (battle) on a high place — on a mountain — with his companions, the Rasulullah looked into the valley below and saw the army of the kuffar (unbelievers) gathering. He told his companions: "Look, these kuffar are coming to confront us. Among them, the one who is visible on a red camel" — and he mentioned his name, perhaps it was Walid — "that person is a very base and vile person who has come to confront the Rasul of the time. But in his progeny, very righteous and pious people will be born." Such was the vision of the Rasulullah, and this was recorded in history. An investigation was then made to determine whose progeny that was, and it was found that Ali bin Ibrahim descended from him — and from Ali bin Ibrahim descended the Duat of Bani Anf. Such is the manner in which God grants jaza.

None from that progeny remain in the dawat today. Two houses in Najran belong to Bani Anf; all the rest became Zaydi. A family from which seventeen Duat came, and all their descendants went astray and became Zaydi. So it stands: Allahu yahdi man yasha wa yudillu — Allah guides whom He wills and leads astray whom He wills. This is absolute truth, and so it came to pass. Two houses in Najran belong to Bani Anf; the rest all became Zaydi.

During the time of Sayyidna Yusuf, when he was appointed as Dai, someone from Bani Anf sought to become the Dai — something of that nature occurred. In his time there was Sayyidna Muhammad bin Husayn, during Yusuf's time, who is the last Dai of Bani Anf — the twenty-fourth Dai, the last Dai of that line.

There is further a Dai from Yemen whose family are bankers. His surname appears among the Duat; it is a very fine name, and he is very well known — among the owners of NCB, the National Commercial Bank. That too is in Mahfuz. His name is Ali bin Hanzala al-Mahfuz. He is a Dai of very high rank, and the risalah (treatise) he wrote is named Simt al-Haqaiq. It is the only such risalah in existence — the only one that records the punishments to be visited upon the kuffar (unbelievers), the mushrikin (polytheists), and the enemies of the Awliya Allah (friends of God). When Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan would perform the ratiqah, he would recite the names of all the Duat from Sayyidna Dhuayb bin Musa all the way to Sayyidna Jamali Ali bin Husayn al-Makhrami, taking their names with their alqab (honorific titles). The ratiqahs were therefore very long — half an hour at a stretch. When the name of Sayyidna Ali bin Hanzala came, he would say it thus: Sayyidna Ali bin Hanzala, Sahib Rutbat al-Fadl (The Possessor of the Rank of Grace). That was the laqab he used.

In the matter of fiqah, there is the case of Sayyidna Ibrahim, `alayh al-salam`, Muqaddas. When he went there — to what was then called Badr, not yet Najran — and reached the dai, the dai said a few days later that he had to pay his fiqah (religious offering). He was very, very poor. He said, "Mawlana, I have not paid the fiqah yet." The dai said, "You have to pay the fiqah." He had only two sets of clothes: one for ordinary days, and the other kept for Jumuah (Friday) and special occasions. He presented that good set of clothes as fiqah to the dai. Fine clothing was offered as fiqah to the dai; only one set of clothes remained, which he would wash and wear. In such hardship he lived and pressed forward. He fell ill in the end — epidemics were also present at that time.

Similarly recorded is the account of Sayyidna Ghulam Husayn Muqaddas. When present with Sayyidna Ali bin Muhsin Shawam, his eyes would be watering, tears flowing from them constantly, and they would be red. Sayyidna would ask, "Is your health all right?" He would reply, "Yes, it is all right," saying that he had a cold. This continued for several days. Later the dai sahib came to know that below the residence where he lodged lived Udayni migrants who cooked their food on wood, sending smoke upward that burned his eyes. He had not told Sayyidna sahib that the Udaynis' cooking fire was troubling him. For this reason, the laqab given to him in the Nass al-Sharif (sacred text of designation) reads: `al-walad al-adib dhi al-khulq`, `al-adab al-allaf al-arib dhi al-khulq al-ajib` — the most well-mannered, intelligent one of wondrous character; a brother of great, great qualities, possessed of most wondrous qualities, the owner of noble morals. He said `dhi al-khulq al-ajib`.

`wa al-fawz wa al-falah fi daim al-saadah. Wa amma al-hajj ila Bayt Allah al-Haram, fa kafa bi qawlihi taala 'alayh`: and triumph and success in everlasting bliss; as for the Hajj to the Sacred House of Allah, the saying of the Exalted suffices concerning it. The saying of the Messenger, `alayh al-salam`, likewise suffices: `man arada khayr al-dunya wa al-akhirah` — whoever desires the good of this world and the Hereafter, whoever intends the good and well-being of both worlds; `fa l-yaumma hadha al-bayt` — let him intend to visit this House, the House of Allah; `aman bada am` — year after year, one year after the other; `wa al-istighfar bi khalis al-niyyat` — and seeking forgiveness with pure intentions; `wa yamhu al-dhunub` — and it erases the sins, for that seeking forgiveness with pure intentions erases them completely and wipes them away; `wa al-sayyiat` — and the evil deeds likewise. `Waffaqana Allah wa iyyaka ayyuha al-akh li mardatih`: O brother, may Allah grant us and you success in attaining His pleasure. `wa nazamana fi silk majami' al-anwar`: and may He place us within the assemblies of lights — the assemblies of the Imams of the religion, their illumined gatherings. `wa duatih`: and of their callers. `wa la yuakhidhna bi mubighat al-dhunub`: and may Allah the Exalted not hold us accountable for our major sins. `wa satara 'anna jami' al-uyub`: and may He conceal all our faults. `wa waffaqana wa saddadna fi jami al-afal wa al-aqwal`: and may He grant us success and uprightness in all our deeds and words. `bi haqqi Muhammad wa alih khayr al-al`: for the sake of Muhammad and his family — `khayr al-al` — those who are the best of kin.

A question was then raised. In the salawat that is recited it is said:

> `Allahumma salli 'ala Sayyidina Muhammadin wa ala ali Sayyidina Muhammadin kama sallayta wa sallamta wa barakta wa rahimta wa tarahhamta ala Ibrahima wa ala ali Ibrahim`

O Allah, send prayers upon our master Muhammad and upon the family of our master Muhammad, just as You sent prayers and peace and blessings and mercy and compassion upon Ibrahim and upon the family of Ibrahim. What manner of salawat was sent upon Janab Ibrahim, `alayh al-salam` — the one to which the phrase `kama sallayta wa sallamta 'ala Ibrahim` refers? Is that defined anywhere? The answer is that for any Imam, no specific formula of salawat has been transmitted. That distinction was not there for any Imam. But it happened for Janab Ibrahim.

There is also this. The words `Muhammadun wa 'Aliyun khayr al-bashar` — Muhammad and Ali are the best of creation — and `wa itratuhuma khayr al-itar` — and their progeny are the best of progenies — are said twice. What Sayyidna Ali bin Sulayman explains regarding this is that the first utterance refers to the Imams who passed in ancient times up to Sayyidna Hunayd, and declares that the Muhammad and Ali of the present time are the best of creation among all those past Imams up until kashf (كَشْف) (unveiling), until Janab Hunayd. The second utterance — `Muhammadun wa Aliyun khayr al-bashar wa itratuhuma khayr al-itar` — then declares that the Muhammad and Ali who have now appeared, and their progeny, are better than the Imams from Janab Hunayd up to Janab Hashim. It thus refers to the Imam of the time. The phrase is said twice for this reason.

This applies to the seventh Imam, Muhammad bin Ismail, as well. Upon him rests the same `Muhammadun wa 'Aliyun khayr al-bashar, wa itratuhuma khayr al-itar`. What is mawsul (مَوْصُول) upon Sayyidna Muhammad bin Ismail, salawat Allah alayh, is that when the shahadah (testimony of faith) is given — `ashhadu anna Muhammadan rasul Allah`, I bear witness that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah — that is one testimony. A second testimony is given for Mawlana Muhammad bin Ismail, who is the Sahib al-din al-tahir (Master of the Pure Religion). He is the Sabir, the seventh, the Sahib al-din al-tahir. He abrogated the shariah of the Prophet Isa, possessing the authority to do so, and in the blink of an eye (chashm-e-zadan) he abrogated it and then reinstated that same shariah. Sayyidna Muhammad bin Ismail holds a great station, by virtue of being the seventh.

In this regard, Surah Qul Huwa Allahu Ahad has four verses — five in one narration, since `Lam yalid wa lam yulad` is there counted as two verses — but four verses. Within those four verses, corresponding to the dawr al-nabawi (دَوْرُ النَّبَوِيّ), the dawr al-tayyibi (دَوْرُ الطَّيِّبِيّ), and the dawr al-qiyamah, four Imams stand as the very essence, each attributed to one of the verses:

1. `Qul huwa Allahu ahad` — Say, He is Allah, the One — indicates Mawlana Muhammad bin Ismail.
2. `Allahu al-samad` — Allah, the Eternal Refuge — is Sayyidna al-Tayyib, Mawlana 'Ali bin al-Tayyib.
3. `Lam yalid wa lam yulad` — He neither begets nor is born — is the 35th Imam, Sayyidna Ahmad, who is the waqih Imam of Sayyidna Sulayman Sahib.
4. `wa lam yakun lahu kufuwan ahad` — Nor is there to Him any equivalent — indicates the 49th Imam, the Imam of today, whose name has not been revealed, who is called Abu al-Qaim, the Qaim of the Imams.

These four Imams are thus attributed to the four verses of Qul Huwa Allahu Ahad.

The Maghrib ki tatawwa also originates from him. For the Maghrib tatawwa there is the saying of Amir al-Muminin:

> `man lam yatatawwa fa laysa minna`

Whoever does not perform the supererogatory prayer is not from us. Whoever leaves the Maghrib tatawwa is not from us — a saying that establishes its great importance. These four rakahs of the Maghrib tatawwa are attributed to Sayyidna Mawlana Muhammad bin Ismail.

In the progeny of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Ismail there were three who were muhtajibin (veiled) and were also wasis. Such is the station. His son Yada became one, and Ali became one. They all became muhtajibin. Their hujjats were then appointed: Abd Allah, Ahmad, and Husayn.

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Sharf al-Fatihah to the souls: Sayyidna Mawlana 'Alim bin Hibatullah, qaddasa Allahu ruhahu wa nawwara zarihahu wa jaalana min barakatihi wa aazatihi wa taidatihi wa taqabbal minna al-ifadah wa al-istifadah bi ahsani ma yakun bi jah al-mursalin wa Sayyid al-awwalin wa al-akhirin wa al-Quran al-Azim wa Ahl al-Bayt al-Tayyibin al-Tahirin salawat Allah alayhim ajmain. Al-Fatihah.

> *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb al-alamin. Al-rahmanirrahim. Maliki yawmid-din. Iyyaka nabudu wa iyyaka nastain. Ihdinas-siratal-mustaqim. Siratal-ladhina anamta 'alayhim ghayril-maghdubi alayhim wa lad-dallin.*

In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful. Praise belongs to Allah, Lord of the worlds, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful, Master of the Day of Judgment. You alone we worship, and from You alone we seek help. Guide us upon the straight path, the path of those upon whom You have bestowed favor, not of those who have earned anger, nor of those who have gone astray.

## 20. The Cold Edges of the World: Where the Opponents Are Cast

The day was the twenty-first of Rabi al-Awwal. The twenty-first of any month is among those seven dates in which inauspiciousness (نُحُوسَةٌ) is gathered. Yet the dua appointed for it is a rare (نَادِرٌ) one. Imam Jafar al-Sadiq composed such duas with great subtlety, and the tone of this one is unmistakable: fulfill my needs, forgive my sins, and I will not rise from this spot until You do this for me. The tone is strange and wondrous — like a child insistent with his father, saying, "Get me this thing," and bringing his father to agree one way or another. Do this for me, do that for me, and until You do it I will not rise from this spot. That is the tone of the dua recited on the twenty-first. The day also marks the passing of the *awsiya*.

Several errors commonly enter the recitation of Ayat al-Kursi:

- At the beginning there is *bi-urwatillahi al-wuthqa* (by the firmest handle of Allah), and mistakes gather there.
- The second is *li wa lakum wa li-walidayya* (for me and for you and for my parents), often recited as *li wa-likum*; the correct form is *wa-lakum*, with a *zabar*, not *wa-likum*.

Then there is the passage:

> *shahida Allahu annahu la ilaha illa Huwa wa al-malaikatu wa ulu al-ilm... la ilaha illa Huwa al-aziz al-hakim. Inna al-dina 'inda Allahi al-islam... wa man yamal... suan*

Allah witnesses that there is no god but He, and the angels and those of knowledge; there is no god but He, the Almighty, the Wise. Indeed, the religion in the sight of Allah is Islam, and whoever does evil.

The correct reading here is *wa-man yamal*, though many say *wa-min yamal*. The Quran Majid reads *wa-man yamal*. The phrase appears in two places in the Quran, written differently in each: in the shorter form it differs, and in the longer form it differs as well. In the Quran Karim it is *wa-man yamal*, while the one that comes later is *fa-man yamal*. The first belongs to the dua of the *wazifa* (وَظِيفَة), not to the Quran Majid itself; the one that comes later is the Quranic verse.

Likewise, *wa yastaghfirullah* is incorrect; the correct form is *yastaghfirillah*. In both instances further down, the form is *yastaghfirun*.

The gravest error concerns *'Aliyyun al-Azim*, where the reading should be *Aliyya al-Azim*. The word appears in three forms in the Quran — *Aliyyun al-Azim* (عَلِيٌّ الْعَظِيمُ), *Aliyya al-Azim*, and *Aliyyi al-Azim* (عَلِيِّ الْعَظِيمِ), with *zabar*, *zer* (زِير), and *pesh* (پِیْش) — each in a different context and place. At the end of Ayat al-Kursi, when *istighfar* (اِسْتِغْفَار) is performed seven times, the correct reading is *al-Aliyya al-Azim*, with a *zabar*. In the *adiyah*, *Aliyyan* (عَلِيًّا) is recited at the beginning.

The sevenfold recitation at the close — *astaghfirullah al-aliyya al-azim* (I seek forgiveness from Allah, the Most High, the Most Great) — carries several reasons behind it. Among them: in the seven *dawr al-sitr* (دَوْرُ السِّتْر) (cycles of concealment), this present existence is the seventh existence, the seventh *zuhur* (manifestation). *Istighfar* is therefore performed for the six manifestations that have passed, and then for the seventh as *al-aliyya al-azim*. These are the seven acts of *istighfar*.

In a previous *majlis* it was said that the name of Mawla Ali stands within the Kalima Sharif. It appears in many places: *Aliyyan waliyyu Allah* (Ali is the friend of Allah). It was said that *Aliyyun* occurs in Surah Maryam, where the words *ala siraatin mustaqeema* (upon a straight path) are recited as *Aliyyan mustaqeema*. The *zer* and *zabar* were changed, but the intention was directed toward Janab Ali. The verse is:

> *Inna hadha siraatu 'aliyyin mustaqeem*

Verily, this is the path of 'Ali, a straight one.

On that point it was said that this is Mawla Ali's own testimony that he was *sadiq* (truthful). Yet the word *sadiq* is not used there. The other verse cited is:

> *Wajal li lisana sidqin 'aliyya*

And grant me a tongue of truthfulness, an exalted one.

That is a different verse, and the point was made upon *sidqin 'aliyya*. The first remains *inna hadha siraatu aliyyin mustaqeem*.

### The Seventh Question

*Al-Sual al-Sabi.* *Qala badu al-mufidina, jazahum Allahu 'anna afdhala al-jaza'* (Some of the benefactors said, may Allah reward them with the best of rewards on our behalf), *wa kadhalika fi kitab Kalilah wa Dimnah* (and likewise in the book Kalilah wa Dimnah). Kalilah wa Dimnah became widely famous. It is an Arabic rendering drawn from the Panchatantra, composed by Ibn al-Muqaffa. Ibn al-Muqaffa was a great scholar who wrote several books, among them *Al-Adab al-Kabir* and *Al-Adab al-Saghir*, and this one as well. Since he lived after the time of the Prophet, he also wrote certain matters concerning the Hudud.

The report is that *yawma saala al-natiq* (on the day the Natiq asked), whose mention comes in what follows, the north (الشِّمَالُ) is the path (مَسِيرٌ) and destination (مَعَاد) for the opponents (أَضْدَاد) of the Aimmah, and their followers are in the west (فِي الْمَغْرِبِ). The north here means the peripheries. There is the northern pole and the southern pole — those parts of the earth buried under extreme snow. At the extreme north and the extreme south lies the path and place of return for the opponents of the Aimmah (أَضْدَادُ الْأَئِمَّة) and for the followers of those opponents. Their destination is there, at the pole. *Wa fi al-maghrib wa azdad al-anbiya fi al-junub* (and in the west, and the opponents of the prophets in the south): the followers of the opponents of the Aimmah have their destination in the west, and the opponents of the Anbiya are in the south (الْجَنُوبُ). *Wa al-tabiina bihim fi al-mashriq* (and their followers in the east): the followers of the opponents of the Anbiya are in the east (الْمَشْرِقُ).

There is also another explanation. The opponents — those who oppose the Aimmah, the Hujjat, and the Hijabs of the Aimmah — are gathered in *ra's wa dhanab* (رَأْسٌ وَذَنَبٌ) (head and tail). The *dhanab* (tail) is a more evil place than the *ra's* (head). These are *wahmi* (وَهْمِي) (imaginal) realities, lying beyond the celestial sphere (نِطَاقُ الْقَمَرِ). *Ra's wa dhanab* are also called Rahu and Ketu. In the *dhanab*, the tail, lies the destination of the opponents of the Aimmah and their Hijabs; in the *ra's*, the head, lies that of the opponents of the Anbiya and the *Duat*. That is their destination. This is what is said, and it is why in the polar regions there is no namaz.

The *mashriq* and *maghrib* intended here are places, but places of severe cold. The north and south are bitterly cold, and the extreme east and extreme west are in a similar state, with snow there as well. They fall within the map of the world. Within the world, then, the north, the south, the east, and the west — taken as the extreme boundary points — are all described as bad. A widespread misconception reads this otherwise and supposes it does not concern one's own region, since broadly speaking every land falls within east and west. But that east and that west mean the end corners: places such as Tibet and Alaska, one at the far edge and the other far up beyond. The intent is the extreme, faraway places where the east ends, or the west, or the north, or the south — places always covered in snow, where there are six months of day and six months of night, a condition found in the far north and south. As for the extreme east and west, there are many islands in the Pacific around the dateline, on both sides — on the Japan side and the America side. Those are extreme positions. The volcanic islands of Hawaii and others lie there, all in the middle of the Pacific Ocean.

The questioner then asks: *Fa-kayfa yunsakhu ma yunsakhu ila hadhihi al-mawadhi al-nufus wal-ajsad?* (So how is that which is transmigrated transmigrated to these places, the souls and the bodies?). How do the transmigrating things reach those places, and is it the souls (نُفُوس) of the opponents of the Aimmah or of the Anbiya that arrive there, or their bodies (أَجْسَاد)? Sayyidna Abdullah Sahab always said that one should rely on the answer, not on the question.

The question continues. *Audhu bika min masiri hadhihi al-hussad* (I seek refuge in You from the destiny of these envious ones): refuge is sought in Allah from the destination and place of these envious ones. *Wa atasimu bika min fili ahl al-fasad* (And I take refuge in You from the actions of the people of corruption): *'ismah*, protection, is sought that one may remain free of the deeds of these people of corruption. *Wa ala kam mudadin yulhaquna bi-tilka al-diyar?* (And for how long a period are they attached to those lands?): for how long do the opponents of the Aimmah and the opponents of the Anbiya go and remain there? *Wa hal al-riyah yuwassiluhum bi-shayin wa al-riyah shimal wa junub wa dabur (دَبور) wa saba (صَبَا)?* (And do the winds connect them with something, and the winds are north, south, west, and east?): do winds blow that reach them, and are the winds named north, south, *dabur*, and *saba* available to them? *Fa-tawallaw alayya* (So they turned away from me): have mercy upon me. *Wa audhu bika wa bi-Sayyidina Muhammad ibn Ismail, wa bi-waladika Sayyidina Ali ibn Hibatullah min amrin taqulu anni atasawwaru ma laysa yudhanu li* (And I seek refuge in You and in our master Muhammad ibn Ismail, and in your son our master Ali ibn Hibatullah, from a matter about which you would say of me that I imagine what is not permitted for me): that no such conception be formed for which permission has not been given. *Wa asaluka bi-man yalu alayka ila babika al-azim wa khalifatika al-karim, an tasturani wa tamhunani ridaka wa sitraka wa awnaka wa safhaka* (And I ask You by the one who is exalted before You to Your great door and Your noble successor, that You conceal me and grant me Your pleasure, Your covering, Your help, and Your forgiveness): that faults be kept concealed, that the honor of His pleasure be attained, that affairs be kept covered, that His help accompany, and that He forgive. *Wa anta al-mukhyyar fi al-atai aw al-man'* (And You are the one with choice in giving or withholding): the choice to grant or to refuse the answer belongs to Him.

### The Answer

*Al-jawab 'ala dhalika al-sual, naqulu bi-awnillahi al-kabir al-mutaal wa minnati waliyyi al-asri wal-zaman* (The answer to that question: we say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Exalted, and the grace of the Master of the era and time), *alayhi salawat al-rahim al-rahman, wa taidi mawlaya al-alam al-anwar wa sirrihi al-sari ila lataif al-suwar* (Upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate, and the support of my master, the luminous banner, and his secret which flows to the subtleties of the forms). Upon him be the blessings of the *Rahim* and the *Rahman*, and with the support of the master who is the banner — Hibatullah Sahab, most luminous — whose secret flows toward the subtleties of the forms.

The answer is this: *inna intiqaala azdad al-aimmati wa al-nutaqa, wa al-tabiina bihim min al-awam, awan al-ayyam, ila tilka al-atraf al-mashumah wa al-jihat al-malumah, tarmihim ilayha al-inayat al-ilahiyyah* (Verily, the transmigration of the opponents of the Imams and the Natiqs, and their followers from among the common folk, at the time of the days, to those inauspicious peripheries and the cursed directions: the divine providence throws them there). The opponents of the Aimmah and the Nutaqa, who are themselves very great, and likewise their followers among the lowly, are carried in their transmigration to those peripheral places which are utterly bad and those directions which are cursed. The divine providence (عِنَايَة إِلَهِيَّة) — meaning the divine arrangements through him who has become the viceroy of the Tenth upon the earth, that is, the *hudud siddiqiyyah* (حُدُودٌ صِدِّيقِيَّة) — casts them to these places. *Wa al-qudrat al-rabbaniyyah* (and the lordly power): the lordly power carries out this act. *Fa-yakununa fiha ala mithali hayawan wal-jibal* (So they are in it in the likeness of animals and mountains): there they exist in the likeness of animals and mountains. As stated in another book, there are vast chunks of ice, and beneath such a chunk lies an immense animal that the ice has wholly covered. Or there are great mountains within which their filthy refuse accumulates.

*Wa al-hissu mawjudun fihim* (And sense perception is present in them): in those upon whom these things fall, sense perception is present; they feel it. So too it has been said that the person who does not take the *'ahd sharif* (noble covenant) has his *nafs al-hissiyyah* (النَّفْسُ الْحِسِّيَّةُ) (sensory soul) pervading his body, on account of which he remains in torment after death and continues to feel that torment. This same *nafs al-hissiyyah* is at work in *dhabihah* (ذَبِيحَة) (slaughter): with *Allahu Akbar* the *nafs al-hissiyyah* of the goat is separated from it, and by that separation it is elevated, and one day it will come into human form. Because of that separation its meat becomes lawful (حَلَال). It has also been said that the meat should not be eaten raw even after the *nafs al-hissiyyah* has been separated; it should be cooked. And even after cooking, the Rasoolullah says that when the steam rises it should be allowed to escape and the meat not eaten while hot, because whatever remainder of the *nafs al-hissiyyah* is left departs with the steam.

The text continues: *fihim, bila intiqal (اِنْتِقَال), muqabbalina bil-salasil* (in them, without transmigration, shackled with chains), *wal-aghlal* (and shackles) — fetters upon them and handcuffs as well — *muadhdhabina bil-adhab al-hail al-muhin* (tormented with the terrifying, humiliating punishment), a punishment vast, dreadful, and horrifying, *bil-harri wal-bard* (with heat and cold), with extreme heat and extreme cold. It is said likewise that those who reach *saqar* (سَقَرُ) (hellfire) are given the light of the moon by day as punishment and the light of the sun by night.

*Al-mulim al-nakil. Wa amma qawluka wa hal hiya ajsadun am al-nufus al-marmiyyah fi al-bilad wa al-bu's* (the painful, exemplary punishment. As for your statement: "Are they bodies or the souls cast into the lands and misery?") — the question being whether it is their bodies or their souls that reach these places, cast there amid calamity and hardship.

*Falam, fa-inna-ma yufariqu ahl hadhihi al-tabaqat illa al-tasawwur al-khabith al-qaim min nasb li-awliya Allah wal-adawat* (So know, for nothing separates the people of these classes except the vile conception arising from enmity toward the friends of Allah and the enmities). What separates them from these classes is a vile conception — the conception that takes shape in one who harbors enmity toward the friends of Allah. Such a person sends that vile conception to those places. *Wa amm al-nufus fa-hiya tashrif fi tilka al-ajsad al-radiah* (As for the souls, they are ennobled in those base bodies): the souls pervade their bodies, which are base. *Wa yakunu minha fiha al-wayl wa al-thubur* (And from it, in it, is woe and destruction): from this comes ruination, destruction, and affliction. *Wa takunu akthafa min al-ajsad li-adhami ma ataw* (And they become denser than the bodies because of the greatness of what they were given). The souls grow denser than the bodies because of what the Qudrat had bestowed — a gift that, over time, would have drawn the soul up and beyond the body. Yet in his own foolishness, the man forced his soul back into his body and refused the favor the Qudrat had given. As it is said:

> *al-shirku zulmun 'azim*

Associating partners with God is a great injustice.

Allah taala will forgive every sin, from one magnitude to another, except this. As it is said:

> *inna Allaha yaghfiru al-dhunuba jamian*

Verily, Allah forgives all sins.

Allah has the power to forgive every sin, but He will not forgive the ascribing of partners to Him.

The state there is *la yahya wa la yamut* (neither living nor dead). On entering that punishment, neither life nor death comes to them. So too does Surah al-Ala declare:

> *la yamutu fiha wa la yahya*

He will neither die therein nor live.

Of those in this state it has been said that they are killed a thousand times each day and revived a thousand times. Such is their condition, and immense is the torment and pain within it.

The scribe of this text was not a great scholar, and many errors stand in it. The correct reading is *wa al-wayl wa al-thubur*, yet the word *shol* appears here. Many such errors have entered through the work of the copyists.

*Wa amma qawluka 'ala kam mudad yulhaquna...* (As for your statement, for how long a period are they attached...) — the word written here is *kam madad*, but the correct word is *mudadd*, the plural of *muddat*; *madad* is something else entirely. The question is: for how many ages are they held at those peripheries, and when do they arrive there? *Fa-bada an yaktatifu adhraw al-silsilah fi kulli nawmin minha sabina qamisan bila khilaf* (So after they have snatched the arms of the chain, in each sleep of it, seventy shirts, without disagreement). What is known of this is recorded in the books of the *awliya' Allah* and the *hudat* (هُدَاة), upon them be the salutations of their Imam, and blessings be upon them.

As for the statement concerning the winds, they differ across all their states. Among them are ascending, noble winds. *Ittasalat bi-kulli latif* (connected with every subtle thing): these meet with certain subtleties. *Wa ma kana min al-habitin ittasala bi-ma yulaimuhu min al-zalimin* (And what was of the descending ones connected with what suits it from among the oppressors): among the winds are descending ones as well, and their connection is with the oppressors. *Wa qad yattasilu al-salihu bil-talih* (And the righteous may connect with the corrupt) — the meaning of this is not disclosed. *Wa yakhussu bil-qawl al-wadih li-mujibat qadhat bada asbabin* (and he specifies with the clear statement the requirements that some causes have decreed): as for why this occurs, Allah knows best. *Min ahl al-jahl wa al-iktisab. Fa-khudh dhalika bil-wajiz min al-qawl* (from the people of ignorance and acquisition. So take that with the concise statement). *Wa lillahi subhanahu al-quwwah wa al-hawl* (And to Allah, the Glorified, belongs the power and the might). To Allah alone belong the power and the might.

### There Is No Power and No Strength

The words *la hawla wa la quwwata illa billahi al-aliyyi al-azim* (there is no power and no strength except in Allah, the Most High, the Most Great) are not a verse of the Quran Majid. The verse of the Quran Majid — which appears perhaps in Surah Bani Israil — is *la quwwata illa ma sha Allah* (there is no power except what Allah wills). But when the Master of the Universe began to use *la hawla wa la quwwata illa billahi al-aliyyi al-azim* in his duas, the saying grew so renowned, and Allah taala loved it so deeply, that He made it common in duas. It appears very often in duas, especially at their conclusion.

The realities explained of it are these. One meaning of *la hawla wa la quwwata* is that a man has no strength to refrain from evil and to do good except what Allah taala grants — this is the outward meaning. The other meaning is that there is no power and no might except through Allah, *al-Aliyy al-Azim*. Within that, it has further been said that there is no power in the external realm (بَابِ ظَاهِر), nor in the internal realm (بَابِ بَاطِن), by which a *haykal* (spiritual form) might be formed — that is, the *suwarat al-nayyirah* (luminous forms) do not reach the Imam.

The form of the deceased, one day, gathers through the *hudud daniyyah* (حُدُودٌ دَانِيَّة) and the *hudud 'aliyyah* (حُدُودٌ عَلِيَّة) with the *dai*, and rises upward until it reaches the *Bab al-Abwab* (Gate of Gates) — who is the *bahr al-zakhar* (overflowing ocean), also called *fasl al-khitab* (فَصْلُ الْخِطَاب) (the decisive speech). There all the forms of the believers gather with the *Bab al-Abwab*. It is a vast gathering. Yet within that gathering, for all its immensity, there is not the form of a *haykal*. A *haykal* is that which is in human form. When this gathering is prepared, the present Imam (ع) receives it and grants it to his son. The son, who until that moment was Imam *bil-quwwah* (potentially), becomes through this gathering's connection to him Imam *bil-fil* (actually). The present Imam then passes away, and his *Bab al-Abwab* also passes away. The *qalb al-haykal* (قَلْبُ الْهَيْكَلِ) (heart of the *haykal*) of the son — who has now become the Imam — is formed by the *Bab al-Abwab*, who rendered a great service, being the greatest servant of the Imam by gathering so immense an assembly through his arrangements.

What is said here, then, is *la hawla wa la quwwata illa billah* (لَا حَوْلَ وَلَا قُوَّةَ إِلَّا بِاللهِ). There is no power and no strength except in Allah, by His grace. And who is meant by Allah? He is the Imam of the era and time. And who is *'Aliyy al-Azim*? The *ayn al-aimmah* (عَيْنُ الْأَئِمَّة) (essence of the Imams), who is the manifestation of Ali (ع). All the Aimmah are therefore the *hijabs* (veils) of *Aliyy al-Azim* (عَلِيٍّ الْعَظِيمِ) — the *hijabs* of the *ayn al-aimmah*. In this the entire picture emerges, from the believer up to the *ayn al-aimmah*, revealing the process that is unfolding.

*Ya Allahumma, ashrah al-fatihah hadiyyatan wasilatan suniyyatan zakiyyatan mardiyyatan ila ruhi Sayyidina 'Ali ibn Hibatullah, qaddasa Allahu ruhahu wa anwara darihahu wa daafa alayna min barakatihi wa taidatihi wa inayatihi wa taqabbal minna al-ifadah wa al-istifadah bi-haqqi Sayyidina Muhammadin wa alihi wa barik wa sallim. Al-Fatihah.*

## 21. Rubies, Elephants, and Believers: The Summit of Each Kind

*Audhu billahi minash shaitanir rajim. Bi fadli Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Wa bihi nastain wa sallallahu 'ala rasulihi Sayyidna Muhammadin wa ala ali Muhammadin wa barik wa sallim.*

*Al-sual al-thamin 'an al-jawahir wal-yawaqit* — the eighth question, concerning jewels and rubies, the stone, and what is related to that.

Every thing has a *ghayah* (غَايَة) — an ultimate point, meaning its limit and its highest expression. There is thus *ghayat al-ahjار*, the ultimate point of stones, and *ghayat al-madan*, the ultimate point of minerals. Among all minerals found inside the earth, and among all stones, the *ghayah* — the most noble — is the ruby, *yaqut*. Among all jewels, minerals, and stones (أَحْجَار), their *ghayah* is the ruby.

In the same way there is *ghayat al-haywan*, the ultimate point of animals. Among all animals, their *ghayah* is the elephant, *fil*. The reason given is the elephant's intelligence: it is the most intelligent among animals, and by virtue of that intelligence it becomes the *ghayah*. Its physical strength is also immense, yet it is its length, its breadth, and its shrewdness that earn it this honor. The horse too holds its own place, but the elephant possesses a greater shrewdness than the horse.

Likewise, *ghayat al-insan* — the ultimate point of man — is the *mumin*, the believer. The station of the *mumin* is lofty: Allah Taala has used this single word for the believers, including them in the rank of those honored with *Ahl al-Jalis* (أَهْلُ الْجَلِيسِ). And wherever in the Glorious Quran the phrase *Ya ayyuhalladhina amanu* — O you who believe — appears, its primary intention is Amir al-Muminin and those who lead the believers, the *amir*.

Allah Taala has also called Himself *Mumin*. Among His names is *al-Mumin*. Such are the ranks, and this is the nature of the matter.

There is also *awwalu man amana* — the first who believed. The final two verses of Surat al-Baqarah are exceedingly noble, and the Rasul said that they should be recited daily before sleeping. When the Rasulullah, sallallahu 'alayhi wa alihi wasallam, journeyed on the Miraj, Allah bestowed two things upon him — things that, as he himself said, had not been bestowed upon any prophet before him. The first was Surat al-Fatiha, and the second was the last two verses of Surat al-Baqarah. Although the recitation in *sadaqallah* takes in the last three verses, the specific mention is of the last two. Within those two, the phrase *awwalu man amana* appears — the first who believed. The numerical value derived from *awwalu man amana* equals the numerical value of *Ali ibn Abi Talib*. Among all the interpretations pointing to Janab Ali (ع), this stands out: the two numerical values are equal.

Concerning the jewels, *jawahir*, and the rubies, *yawaqit*, a question was asked: *hal hiya tashtamiu ila mahallin maruf wa makanin mawsuf* — do they gather in a known place and a described location? That is, does their gathering occur at a special, famous, described place? *Wa tanhallu wa tanqasimu 'alal-kawakib* — and do they dissolve and get distributed upon the planets? That is, are they ground up and distributed among the *kawakib* (كَوَاكِبُ)? *Wa idha kana yashtamiu fa-ala kam muddatuha* — and if they gather, for what duration? If their gathering occurs in the *kawakib*, how long does it persist? *Wa kayfa intiqauha* — and how is the selection carried out within the *kawakib*? *Hal hiya taudu ila mawdiihal-qadim* — do they return to their ancient place? After being ground and entering the *kawakib*, will these jewels and rubies gather again in their original state? This is what the questioner asked. *Tafaddalu alayya* — please favor me, for you are worthy of it. *Fal-abdu yasalu malikahu min fadlihi* — for the servant asks of his master from his bounty.

*Al-jawab 'ala dhalika's-sual* — the answer to that question. *Naqulu bi-awnillahil-Kabiril-Mutaal* — the answer is given with the help of Allah, the Great, the Most High. *Wa mannat Waliyyil-Asri waz-Zaman alayhi salawatur-Rahimir-Rahman* — and by the grace, the gift, of the one who is the *Wali al-Asr wal-Zaman*, may the blessings of Al-Rahim and Al-Rahman be upon him. And by the *taid* (تَأْيِيد), the aid, of *Mawlaya al-Alam, Alam al-Huda* — by the aids of Sayyidna Hibatullah Saheb, which are most luminous. *Wa sirruhul-bahir al-sari ila lataifi's-suwar* — and his manifest secret, flowing to the subtleties of the forms, streaming toward them.

The answer is *annal-yawaqit wal-jawahir al-hisan tuhfazu fi khazainil-muluk* — that the fine rubies and jewels are preserved in the treasuries of kings. *Wa tusan* — and they are protected there, their complete protection maintained. *Thumma tanshau mata shaal-mudabbir* — then they arise whenever the Disposer wills. They remain for a period, set in crowns and placed at positions of honor, and then, when the Disposer wills, they begin to be ground up and enter a state of dissolution. *Wa yasiru fil-amakinish-sharifah* — and they travel to noble and exalted places of distinction. *Wa mata abalal-aflak wal-kawakib sarat hadhihi li-taghliba fil-khidmah* — and when the celestial spheres and planets wore out, these traveled to prevail in service.

When the *aflak* and *kawakib* fall, each must ascend toward the spiritual realm, and every ascending thing must touch the earth before its ascent — except the *Falak al-Muhit*, the encompassing sphere, which, from where it already stands, becomes the Universal Intellect, *Aql al-Kulli* (عَقْلُ الْكُلِّي), and enters the spiritual realm directly. All other things — whether the moon, the sun, or the *aflak* — must touch the earth, and only thereafter does their progress occur. Meteorites likewise fall from above to the earth and become extremely valuable, consistent with the same principle.

When the *aflak* and *kawakib* fall, they ascend. What takes their place is the jewels and rubies, which assume their *khilafat* — their vicegerency — in service. *Bi-hikmatin muharrikil-awالim* — by the wisdom of the Mover of the worlds, a wisdom belonging to the Mover of all things. *Wa baqiyat muddatal-kawnil-adami* — and it remained for the duration of the Adamite existence; for the duration of a *kawr* of Adam, they remain. Thus *khilafat*s are established: when the *aflak* and *kawakib* progress, the jewels and rubies take their *khilafat*, and for one *kawr* of Adam their duration persists. *Ila an tawaffa ma 'alayha min al-khidam* — until the service appointed to them is fulfilled. *Wa yajril-amru ala hadhihil-khutiyyah hatta yasadal-alamu bil-kulliyyah* — and the matter proceeds along this path until the world ascends in its entirety, which happens at the end of a *kawr*.

*Li-qawli asdaq al-qailin* — as the most truthful of speakers said, for Allah Taala says: *kama badana awwala khalqin nuiduhu* — as We began the first creation, We will repeat it; *wadan alayna* — a promise upon Us; *inna kunna failin* — indeed We are the doers of it, and We will do it again. This coming and going of the *kawr*, all these things will keep unfolding.

The verse from Surah Ya-Sin also speaks to this: *Awa laysalladhi khalaqa's-samawati wal-arda bi-qadirin 'ala an yakhluqa mithlahum, bala wa huwal-khallaقul-alim* — is not He who created the heavens and the earth able to create the like of them? Yes, and He is the knowing Creator. He is the *Khallaq al-Alim*. Allah created the heavens and the earth, and He is able to create them again. The same system will be recreated. After each *kawr*, a new system comes.

## 22. Visiting the Graves: Traces, Intercession, and Free Will

The ninth question, *al-sual al-tasi 'an ziyarat al-qubur*, concerns the visitation of graves — *kayfa dhalika*, how it works — and *wa ma yahdurul-qasidina lil-ajsman*, what attends those who visit the interred bodies. When visitors present themselves at a grave, *kayfa shuurul-maqsud* — how does the one intended sense it? Do the dead perceive that a visitor has come?

The question also concerns Sayyidna 'Abdullah ibn Thamir (عَبْدُ اللهِ بْنُ ثَامِرٍ), a very high-ranking elder and *mubashshir al-nabi* — one who gave glad tidings of the prophet. He lived before the Prophet ﷺ, his birth and death both preceding him, and he gave tidings of the coming of the Huzur. He died in Najran, at the site of *Ukhdud* (أُخْدُود), mentioned in Surah al-Buruj. His grave was seen in that era left in very poor condition — nothing more than a mound of earth, with no care given to it. Later, in pictures, it appeared beautifully restored. When journeys for *dawat*s were made in the time of Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan and the road passed through *Ukhdud*, Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan would always recite an *iradah* (إِرَادَة) — a devotional prayer — for him, and would recite a *dua*.

> *wa qad sara lahu hadhihil-muddah al-madidah*

A long period has passed for him, the questioner notes. The questioner adds that he has begun asking many questions and has exceeded his means, but his state is one of deep trouble, and so he continues. The one addressed is from *ahl al-fadl wal-nawal* — the people of favor and bounty, generous givers of gifts.

### The Answer

*Al-jawab 'ala dhalika's-sual* — the answer to that question.

> *naqulu bi-awnillahil-Kabiril-Mutaal*

We say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Most High.

> *Wa mannat Waliyyil-Asri waz-Zaman, 'alayhi salawatur-Rahimir-Rahman*

And by the grace of the Master of the Era and the Time, upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate.

> *Wa taidi Mawlayi al-Alam*

And by the aid of my Master, the distinguished one, Mawlana Sayyidna Hibatullah.

> *Wa sirruhul-bahir bi-nurin-nazm, anna ziyarat al-qubur sirrun mastur, wa huwal-muttasil bi-dhalikal-haddi 'alal-ghurur*

And his manifest secret with the light of the order: the visitation of graves is a concealed secret, connected to that rank over deception.

The concealed secret of grave visitation is this: the visitor receives emanations — *fudul* — from the rank of the one whose grave is visited, a high rank indeed.

> *Li-yashfaa lahu 'inda jamii's-suwar*

So that he may intercede for him at the gathering of the forms.

The one visited intercedes for the visitor in those gatherings of *suwar* where he is present. The higher the rank, the greater the spiritual powers. In life, the *hudud* of the Dawah were angels *bil-quwwah* — in potential. After death, they become angels *bil-fil* — in actuality. It has also been stated elsewhere that when a visitor comes, the one visited senses the visit. The luminous form — *surat nayyirah* (صُورَةٌ نَيِّرَة) — of the one visited is always turned toward his *fudala* (فُضَلَاءُ), his spiritual particle. A person, wherever he may be sitting, keeps his thoughts on his home and everything within it; the mind stays with what is most precious even across distance. In the same way, the one who has passed away keeps his luminous form oriented toward what is most precious to him: his *fudala*, which is advancing. Such a one desires that this *fudala* progress as swiftly as possible and ascend into the *aflak*. Through the luminous form — which resides within the *hiyakil* (هَيَاكِل), the spiritual structures, or accompanies the *hudud 'aliyah*, the higher ranks, on its way toward the *Bab al-Abwab*, the Gate of Gates — an *ishraq* (إِشْرَاقٌ), an illumination, continuously emanates. When a believer enters the circle of that *ishraq*, he too receives benefit, and the one visited intercedes for him.

As the Master of the Universe stated: when children visit the graves of their parents, the parents sense their arrival and are as joyful as they were when those children were small and would come and sit in their laps. That same joy is felt by the mother and father. This is the saying of the Rasul — that parents become extremely happy upon their children's visitation and feel great pleasure. And when the children pray for them, recite Yasin, or perform *nazr* (نَذْر) and *niyaz*, the parents perceive all of it. This connects to the verse in Surah Bani Israil:

> *Rabbirhamhuma kama rabbayani saghira*

My Lord, have mercy upon them as they brought me up when I was small.

People often mispronounce it as *rabbiyani*, but the correct form is *rabbayani*. The meaning is: O Allah, have mercy on them just as they showed mercy to me when I was small. This *dua* should be recited both during a parent's lifetime and after their death — mercy is not reserved for after death alone. While parents are still living, children should pray for them with this Quranic supplication: O Allah, have mercy on them as they had mercy on me in my childhood.

If the visitor is of a higher rank than the one visited, the matter is reversed. Aid and help then flow from the higher visitor down to the lower one, just as water always flows from a higher surface to a lower one.

For the grave of a great elder or a prophet, there are holy traces. The *fudala* itself is no longer there, but the traces — the *athar* (آثَارٌ) — remain, and a trace still signifies something.

Yes, traces remain. Even without *fudala*, the *athar* endure. It is on account of these *athar* that the blessings of the grave persist. A bottle of *'itr* — perfume — after the perfume has been emptied out still retains the fragrance. In the same way, even after the *fudala'* has progressed onward, its *athar* remain at the grave. Indeed, the very site of the grave will become the place of its future *futteh*.

Yes, yes.

### Intercession and the Means

> *Wa li-yashfaa lahu 'inda majamii's-suwar, wa li-yakshifa anhu ma arahu min fununil-masaib*

So that he may intercede for him at the gathering of the forms, and so that he may remove from him the various types of calamities that have afflicted him.

To that extent, the various calamities befalling the visitor are removed by the one visited. This connects to the practice of going to the graves of the *awliya Allah* — the saints of Allah — and saying: you are the *wasilah*, the means, through which Allah Taala may hear our prayers and help us; Allah alone is the one who helps, but you have become the intermediary. This *wasilah* is itself a profound matter, as it appears in the Quran:

> *Wabtaghu ilayhil-wasilah*

And seek the means of approach to Him.

Among the common people, *'awam al-nas*, the misguided generally interpret this to mean: reach Allah through the Quran. That is not its meaning. It means to reach Allah through the means — that is, through the Rasul and the Ahl al-Bayt, or through their followers: the *duat*, the *hujjat*, and the *imam*. Through them one reaches Allah. The *marifat* (مَعْرِفَة) — the gnosis — of the Worshipped, the *mabud*, is contained within the *marifat* of the *hudud*, the ranks of the Dawah.

> *Wa ma 'alima bihi min jamiil-maaqib*

And what he knew of all the consequences —

that is, whatever affliction is befalling the visitor from various circumstances.

> *li-kawnihi yushrifu 'ala fudalatihi*

Because he is overseeing his *fudala*.

The one whose grave is visited has his *surat sharif* (صُورَة شَرِيف) exerting *ishraq* upon his *fudala*.

> *Wa yahummu 'alayha*

And he cares for it. He is deeply solicitous toward his *fudala*.

> *Wa yudabbiruha*

And he manages it — directing how to advance the *fudala* as swiftly as possible.

> *Wa yahdurul-qasidina ilayha*

And the visitors attend to it. The visitors present themselves in relation to this *fudala*.

These blessings are then obtained by the visitors, because the *ishraq* of the visited is directed toward his *fudala*, and within that circle the visitors arrive and benefit.

### The Degrees of Illumination

The lowest *ishraq* occurring — beyond which higher *ishraq*s also operate — is the *ishraq* of a believer's own *surat sharif* upon his body. Although the *surat sharif* is not yet fully formed at this stage, remaining at the level of *nafs namiyah* (the vegetative soul), *nafs hissiyah* (the sensory soul), and *nafs natiqah* (the rational soul), the *nafs natiqah* is nonetheless a *surat sharif* *bil-quwwah* — in potential — and its *ishraq* is already operating upon the body. This is the lowest *ishraq*. Above it come the higher *ishraq*s of the Imam and others.

The believer has been placed within Surat al-Fatiha. Surat al-Fatiha has seven verses, but its words — *kalimat* — number twenty-five. Those twenty-five words correspond to the twenty-five prophets mentioned in the glorious Quran. Although there are 124,000 prophets, Allah Taala has mentioned only twenty-five in the Quran, and Surat al-Fatiha likewise contains exactly twenty-five words. These twenty-five words indicate:

- Twelve *hudud* of the *jazair* — the islands — that is, the *duat* of the *jazair*, who are twelve.
- Twelve *hudud* of the *hadarat* — the presences — that is, the *duat* of the *hadarat*, who are twelve.

Their leader, the head of these twenty-four, is the Imam of the Time — making twenty-five in all.

The twelve *hudud* of the *jazair* each carry out their *dawat* at their own places, in their own languages, individually. The *duat* of the *hadarat*, however, operate where the manifestation of the Imam of the Time is present, and so their *dawat* is collective, not individual. This distinction is reflected in the *tahajjud* (تَهَجُّد) prayer, where two-*rakat* sets are offered in six *salam*s, because the *duat* of the *jazair* are connected in pairs — each *dai* has a companion. The *duat* of the *hadarat*, by contrast, carry out the *dawat* collectively, and their example is reflected in *salat al-layl* (صَلَاة اللَّيْل), whose twelve *rakat*s are prayed consecutively, all at once. After the *salat al-layl* there is a *dua*.

There is, there is.

Yes, a very long *dua*.

Yes, the *dua* is very long.

After no other prayer is there such a *dua*.

*Salat al-layl* is the final prayer before *shafa* (شَفْعٌ), and it indicates the *duat* of the *hadarat*. They are of very high rank, for it is from these very *duat* — from their *fudala* and their *riyad* — that the *Bab al-Salsal* (بَابُ السَّلْسَال) and the *Bab al-Anbiya* (بَابُ الْأَنْبِيَاءِ) are formed. These are people of the highest rank.

### Conscience and Choice

Conscience — *zamir*. A man's conscience does not allow him to do wrong; he feels it speaking to him. That is the guidance of the *natiqah*. The *natiqah* always seeks to keep its *namiyah* and *hissiyah* with it, and strives toward that end. The stronger the *natiqah*, the more powerfully it exerts *ishraq* upon its *namiyah*, its *hissiyah*, and upon the body, seeking to elevate them. This is why some *hudud* of the Dawah have been such that not only the *namiyah*, *hissiyah*, and *natiqah* join together after death to form the *surat sharif*, but even their internal organs and lower bodily parts ascend with them — on account of their nobility and the life of piety and purity they lived.

Conscience, however, is universal: everyone has one. And everyone has taken an oath. Some are bound by it; some are not. A man may say consciously, to his own conscience: "No, I am going to do this wrong thing." He then performs the act — whether right or wrong — with full intention. It is not that merely belonging to the Ahl al-Silsilah guarantees one walks the straight path. Free will — *ikhtiyar* (اِخْتِيَار) — exists.

Free will exists, and it is a very great thing that nature has given to man alone. Angels have not been given free will, for angels cannot sin, as the Quranic verse states:

> *wala yasun*

And they do not disobey.

They do not sin. Jannah and Dozakh — paradise and hell — have been made for humans alone. Among Allah's creations there are many: the moon, the sun, the *aflak*, the earth, animals, minerals, and countless other things. But Jannah and Dozakh are for humans alone, because man is the only creature with the choice — *ikhtiyar* — to do good and to do evil. Goodness is clear to him and evil is clear to him; yet knowing goodness, he may incline toward evil, and knowing evil, he may incline toward goodness. He has complete freedom of choice. Because of this choice, if he does good he draws near to Jannah, and if he does evil he draws near to Dozakh. This condition applies to man alone, not to others.

For the one endowed with *natiqah*, with *juzur*.

Yes. Among all of Allah's creation, it is man alone for whom Jannah and Dozakh have been created. There is a *dua* whose author urges that one should ask for Jannah five times daily and five times seek refuge from Dozakh:

> *Allahumma inni asalukal-jannah*

O Allah, I ask You for paradise.

> *Asalullahal-jannah*

I ask Allah for paradise.

> *Wa audhu billahi min al-nar*

And I seek refuge in Allah from the Fire — asking Allah for protection from Dozakh. This is prescribed to be done five times.

A slightly different tone is used for the *dua* after the *adhan*. *Rabbana atina* is in its own place. Here the asking is directly for Allah to grant Jannah, and the tone is different. That *dua* is in its own place — it is, in fact, the *dua* of Amir al-Muminin, what is recited after the *adhan*. Five times: some recite it five times at different times. And this one — five times together: five times to ask, and five times to seek refuge.

The *dua* after the *adhan* is apt because the interval between the *adhan* and the *iqamah* (إِقَامَة) is one of the five times when *dua*s are accepted. During that interval, supplications are answered. The prayer is offered, as Amir al-Muminin prescribed:

> *Allahumma inni asalukal-jannah... wa an tuzawwajani min al-huril-in*

O Allah, I ask You for paradise, and that You unite me with the maidens of paradise.

The *hur 'in* are in truth the *hudud aliyah* (حُدُودٌ عَالِيَة). The prayer is that unification — union — may be granted with the *hudud aliyah*.

### The Traces That Remain

> *Wa 'an Sayyidna Abdullah ibn Thamir*

And concerning Sayyidna 'Abdullah ibn Thamir — the question that has been asked.

> *Wa annahu lahu hadhihil-muddah*

And that for him has been this duration. A period has passed for him.

> *Wa anna qad zada 'alal-alfi fil-iddah*

More than a thousand years have passed.

> *Wa in kana dhalika wa qad saidal-fudala*

If that is so, the *fudala* has indeed ascended. After a thousand years, all of it — *jamiuha* — has progressed: its flesh (لَحْمُهَا), its bones (عَظْمُهَا), and its nerves (أَعْصَابُهَا).

> *Fa-adal-athar*

The traces returned. Those *athar* remain in that grave.

> *Wa't-turbatush-sharifah allatijtajanat biha*

The noble soil in which the *fudala* was enveloped also remains: the honored earth in which it was mixed preserves its nobility still.

> *Wa ishraqu's-suwar fi tilkal-amakini't-taharah*

The *ishraq* that continues to occur moves toward the *fudala*, toward its *athar*, and toward the soil in which the body was laid. In that soil too, a nobility was created.

> *Wa ala qadriz-zair wal-mazur tuqabiluhuz-zubaduz-zahirah*

According to the rank of both the visitor and the one visited, the manifest quintessences meet him.

### The Twenty-Eight Stages

The *zubad* (زُبَد) are these. After the *fudala* has progressed, it must travel to Baytullah. The great grave — as distinct from the small grave — is Baytullah; the small grave is the one in which a person is placed after death. To journey to Baytullah, there are twenty-eight stages, *manazil* (مَنَازِل), to cross. From the place where a person died, the *fudala* moves gradually:

- First it comes to the spot in the masjid where he used to pray. One who prays habitually makes a particular place his own, and that place becomes the gathering-point for his *fudala*.
- From there it progresses to the *mihrab* — the prayer niche.
- From the *mihrab* it advances to the shrine of Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan.

The progression continues. From Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan's shrine it moves onward through various noble places — *amakin sharifah* — until it reaches the *mawaqit*, the appointed stations: the five stations from which pilgrims bind their intention for *umrah*. It is said that *fudalat* and *riyad* gather at those stations. Progressing further, from Anderganj Charkhan the *fudala* arrives at the precincts of Baytullah, reaching the various *rukn* (رُكْنٌ)s — the corners — and ultimately arriving at the *rukn al-aswad*, the Black Stone corner. This process is ongoing and unceasing. From there it ascends.

The state of ascension is called *zubad*. While it rises, it remains in the state of *fudala*. When the *fudala* arrives at the pillars of Baytullah, it is called *zakhair* — treasures. When it ascends to the *aflak*, the celestial spheres, it is called *zubad*. The *zubad* is a very exalted state of being.

The *zubad sharifah* thus meet both the visitor and the one whose grave is visited, and they aid them.

> *Wa aydan fa-inna kullu qabrin damma hadhihil-hudud*

Every grave that contains these *hudud* is connected to these *hudud muazzamah* (حُدُودٌ مُعَظَّمَة).

> *Fa-tahtahu sirrun 'azim min al-khamairil-kaminah*

Beneath it lies a great secret from the latent leavens.

> *Fi tilkal-biqail-mukarramah*

Those are honored spots — their noble places.

> *Fa-li-dhalika wajabat ziyaratuha*

Their visitation therefore becomes obligatory. The hidden leavens are present there; the *suwar* cast their *ishraq* upon the grave; things have progressed while *athar* remain. For all these reasons visitation becomes obligatory. These places are exceedingly pure, and they benefit the believer and the visitor alike.

### Amanullah Saheb

A story of this kind occurred in the time of Wali al-Muqaddas. A man named Amanullah Saheb, who served as a *Mulla Adib* in the village of Taliwala, contracted cancer in his final days. The cancer was in his jaw, and his condition was grave. Various doctors examined him, yet the cancer continued to worsen. He was a man of firm faith with deep trust in Huzur. Taliwala lies very close to Khankhah Sharif — the nearest village to it. At Khankhah Sharif are two of the elders: Sayyidi Yusuf bin Ali Khan and Sayyidi Sharif Muhammad Saheb Sindhi. Amanullah Saheb would go there and sit as if in *itikaf* — in seclusion. He would take soil from that grave and apply it to the afflicted place. This became his daily routine, maintained for months. When he later returned to the doctors, the cancer had vanished entirely, and he was completely well.

This too reflects a principle at work. The masters of the graves, who lived noble lives, were *bil-quwwah* — in potentiality — during their lives, and have now become *bil-fil*, in actuality. By virtue of becoming *bil-fil*, they have the capacity to benefit their followers and those who hold faith in them. Benefit is received in this way.

Amanullah Saheb was the father of Salim, who recently built the hospital.

### Seeking Help from the People of the Graves

> *Allati la hadatah*

Without limit.

Those noble *suwar* meet both the visitor and the one whose grave is visited. The *suwar* are ever oriented toward their *fudala*, and because of this the *ishraq* continues uninterrupted — the *ishraq* of the *surah* toward its *fudala*. The person who comes to visit, dwelling within that circle, also receives benefit.

As has been mentioned, the soil of Imam Husayn's grave is indeed regarded as *khak-e-shifa* — soil of healing. There is also a saying of the Master of the Universe:

> *wa idha daqati's-sudur fastainu bi-ahlil-qubur*

When the chests become constricted, seek help from the people of the graves.

When the chests become constricted — when no way out can be found, when the distress is such that a person cannot see how to remove his trouble — help is to be sought from the people of the graves. The saying does not say to seek help from Allah directly. Allah is the one who ultimately helps. But these are the people of Allah, and because they are the people of Allah, Allah helps through them, and through them help is received.

As it is in the Quran, in the verses revealed concerning Sayyidna Isa ('ع):

> *man ansari ilallah*

Who are my helpers for Allah?

> *Qalal-hawariyyuna nahnu ansarullah*

The disciples said, "We are the helpers of Allah."

Does Allah need help? This is a Quranic verse. For the sake of Allah, who will be my helper? The disciples answered that they were the helpers of Allah.

> *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Allahumma ashrik fil-fatihah, hadiyyatan wasilatan suniyyatan zakiyyatan nadiyyah ila ruh Sayyidna Ali bin Hibatullah, qaddasallahu ruhahu wa nawwara darihah, wa daafa 'alayna min barakatihi wa lahdhatihi wa taidatihi, wa taqabbal minna al-ifadah wal-istifadah fi azam ma yakunu bi-haqqi Sayyidna Muhammadin wa alihi ajmain wa sallim al-Fatihah.*

> *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi Rabbil-alamin, al-Rahmanir-Rahim, Maliki yawmi'd-din, iyyaka nabudu wa iyyaka nastain, ihdina's-siratal-mustaqim, siratalladhina anamta 'alayhim ghayril-maghdubi alayhim wa la'd-dallin.*

> *Al-Fatihah.*

> *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi Rabbil-alamin, al-Rahmanir-Rahim, Maliki yawmi'd-din, iyyaka nabudu wa iyyaka nastain, ihdina's-siratal-mustaqim, siratalladhina anamta 'alayhim ghayril-maghdubi alayhim wa la'd-dallin.*

## 23. The Knife and the Name: Sacrifice at Mina

The tenth question from *Kunooz al-Hikmah*, *as-sual al-ashir*, concerns *Dhibh al-hadi fi Mina* — the slaughter of the *hadi* (هَدْي) at Mina — and asks: *ma haqiqatuhu*, what is its reality?

One meaning of *hadiyyah* (هَدِيَّة) is a gift, and *al-hadi* is the name given to the sacrificial animal, that which is offered up. The place is called both *Mina* and *Muna* (مُنًى), and *Muna* is the more evocative form, since *muna* means the ultimate desire, the deepest aspiration. The questioner asks concerning the animal slaughtered at Mina by a pilgrim performing *hajj*: what is its true reality? *Wa amma al-mathal wal-mawsul* — as for the metaphor and its referent — *faqad 'arraftuhu min tafaddulukum*, these the questioner has already come to know through the favor shown him. So too with what is at Mount Arafat, the highest point of that plain: *faqad araftu al-mathal*, the apparent sense of it he has grasped. What he now asks for is *al-jawab al-shafi wal-manwal al-kafi* — the healing answer and the sufficient provision, a full and complete exposition.

*Al-jawab 'ala dhalika as-sual, naqulu bi-awnillah al-kabir al-mutaal.* The answer is given with the help of Allah, the Great, the Exalted, *wa minnat wali al-asr wal-zaman*, and by the favor of the *Wali al-Asr wal-Zaman*, *alayhi as-salawat al-rahim al-rahman*, *wa tayid mawlaya al-alam wa sirruhu al-bahir al-muazzam*, from the support of Mawlaya al-Alam, namely Hibatullah Saheb, and from the effulgence of his magnificent secret.

The whole plain of 'Arafat is a *mawquf*, a place of standing. The Sarwar-e-Kainat ascended the hill there and offered *duas*, and Muslims generally make their way to that hill as well.

The meaning of the sacrifice is this. When the *Mudabbir al-Hakim*, the Wise Planner, intends *min irqaiha wa isadiha 'ala an-nasl al-qawim* — its elevation and its ascension upon the upright lineage — so that the flesh of the sacrifice may rise to the pure lineage, the slaughter is performed. As for its soul, that which is called the *nafs hissiyyah* (نَفْسٌ حِسِّيَّةٌ), the sensory soul, it is punished through the act of slaughter. By the slaughtering of the animal, its *nafs hissiyyah* is expelled from it.

If an animal dies a natural death, its *nafs hissiyyah* retreats inward into the body, and that is a bad form of death, for the sensory soul has turned inward. But when the slaughter is performed with the invocation of the name of Allah, the *nafs hissiyyah* separates from the animal, and for that reason its flesh may be eaten and used. The flesh is elevated and made to ascend, while the *ruh* — that is, the *nafs hissiyyah* — is punished and driven out.

There is the question of whether the slaughter is valid when something other than *Allahu Akbar* is pronounced. The Jews who perform slaughter do not say *Allahu Akbar* but invoke in their own tongue, and their slaughter is nonetheless permissible (جَائِزٌ). The slaughter of a Jew and the slaughter of a Christian are both *halal*. A Muslim will say *Allahu Akbar*; in the case of others, whatever they say in their own language for that purpose is accepted.

As for what is done in Saudi Arabia, a butcher who performed slaughter there explained that a tape recording of *Allahu Akbar* is played in the slaughterhouse while the machine operates. That is a practice about which one may have reservations. Their contention is a practical one: how many times can the invocation be repeated over a mechanical process?

Immense are the blessings that flow from saying *Allahu Akbar*. There is a hadith of Rasulullah, Sarwar-e-Kainat:

> *as-salatu qurbanu kulli taqiyyin*

Prayer is the sacrifice of every God-fearing person.

The word *qurban* (قُرْبَان), sacrifice, is used here deliberately. At a slaughter, *Allahu Akbar* is said, and the animal becomes *halal* so that its flesh may be eaten. In prayer, after *wudu* and after coming to the masjid in a state of worship, the worshipper says *Allahu Akbar* over himself. His own *nafs hissiyyah* is thereby being sacrificed, struck and expelled continuously. In a two-*rakat* prayer alone, *Allahu Akbar* is uttered eleven times. If a single utterance renders an animal *halal*, what a magnificent difference it must make when a believer in a state of *iman* pronounces it over himself. The *nafs natiqah*, the rational soul, of a believer is so deeply veiled in its inner workings that it does not disclose to the *nafs hissiyyah* — the soul nearest to it — the secrets, the blessings, and the divine effulgence it receives from *'Ali Illiyyin* (عَلِيِّ عِلِّيِّينَ). It informs neither the *nafs namiyyah* (نَفْس نَامِيَّة), the vegetative soul, nor the *nafs hissiyyah* in any way. Such is the nature of the *nafs natiqah*.

The Messenger named the most excellent *wazifah* (وَظِيفَة), the finest invocation, as four phrases:

> *Subhanallah, wal-hamdu lillah, wa la ilaha illallah, wallahu Akbar*

Glory be to Allah, and all praise is for Allah, and there is no god but Allah, and Allah is the greatest.

*Allahu Akbar* is among them. A fifth phrase is also included:

> *Wa la hawla wa la quwwata illa billahil-aliyyil-azim*

And there is no might and no power except with Allah, the Most High, the Most Great.

A narration relates that the Prophet counseled a person to recite these four phrases before sleeping, for doing so brings the reward of one *Hajj*. Such are the blessings contained within them.

In the discussion of the *tasbih*, the correspondences between these phrases and the souls were established: *Allahu Akbar* corresponds to the *nafs namiyyah*, *al-hamdu lillah* to the *nafs hissiyyah*, and *Subhanallah* to the *nafs natiqah*. The final phrase, *La ilaha illallah*, pertains in its form to the *nayyirah* (نَيِّرَةٌ), the luminous form. At the time of death the three souls unite, and they are transformed into the noble form and the form of the *nayyirah*.

A question was raised about the *nafs hissiyyah* escaping the *jism*, the body: does escaping mean that it ascends? It does not ascend. The *nafs natiqah* absorbs the *nafs hissiyyah*, and it absorbs the *nafs namiyyah* as well. The sensory soul spreading into the body is a very bad death, a bad state; the sensory soul being absorbed into the rational soul is a very noble thing. The *nafs hissiyyah* becomes part of the *nafs natiqah*.

Nor does this belong to the hour of death alone. Throughout life the process continues, the *nafs natiqah* drawing the *nafs hissiyyah* into itself, and the more obedient the sensory soul is, the more completely it is absorbed.

This is why it is said that when the *awliya Allah* pray, their state changes — the person he was before is not the person he is after. The Messenger, on the night of *Miraj*, went to meet Allah and obtained those excellences and that nearness to God; on his return, he was not the same person who had departed. It is very far from reason that the one whom the Lord of the worlds had summoned for a meeting would be sent back in the same state.

There is also the case of Sayyidna Ibrahim bin Muhammad bin Fahd. Sayyidna 'Ali bin Sulayman (عَلِيّ بِن سُلَيْمَان) said of him: "Had it not been decided in pre-eternity that I would precede Ibrahim, I could never have preceded him." A momentous statement. Sayyidna Ibrahim bin Muhammad bin Fahd remained in Yemen, for there is a principle of the *dawah* that a *Dai* cannot leave the island of Yemen unless his successor is present there. At that time the Zaidi Imam who held power imprisoned him, and he remained in jail for fourteen years. During those fourteen years, Sayyidna Ali bin Sulayman performed approximately eleven *nusus* (نُصُوصٌ), appointments, upon him. One of the *hudud* of the *dawah* asked him: "Master, you have already performed *nass* on Sayyidna Ibrahim; why was there a need to perform it again?" He replied: "The Ibrahim upon whom I had performed *nass* has changed. Ibrahim is no longer Ibrahim." How great a statement that is. That Ibrahim is not the same Ibrahim; he has changed, and the *nass* was performed upon a different Ibrahim.

Ibrahim was the son of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd, and their mother was the same, Khadija bint Nuh. He was perhaps born after the passing of Sayyidna Sulayman, so the difference in their ages may not have been great.

*Wal-qatlu fi jamii halatiha wa la taqtassu laha al-inayat al-ilahiyyah bi's-suud*: the divine providence did not take *qisas*, retribution, from the *suud*, the ascension, of the *dhabihah*, *li-kawnihim min azdad al-hudud*, because it was from the opposites of the ranks. What is meant is its *nafs hissiyyah*. The meat of the slaughtered animal did ascend and entered into the food of the believers; but its sensory soul, being from the opposites of the ranks, was not elevated through retribution. *Wa yufariquhu bi-wasitat at-tasmiyat al-muazzamah 'inda arbab al-huda*: it separates from it by means of the naming of God, magnificent in the view of the masters of guidance. The *nafs hissiyyah* separates by means of the *tasmiyah* (تَسْمِيَة), that is, by the saying of *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim*.

Where it goes is to desolate deserts. The *nafs hissiyyah* goes to *Ra's al-Zanab* (رَأْسُ الذَّنَبِ), which is its abode. The sensory souls that gather there — whether they are *azdad*, opposites, or those of the slaughtered — being evil, cause harm to the believers. The *azdad* of the *awliya Allah* in particular are given a respite of forty days during which they roam between the heavens and the earth, harming the believers. After forty days they are drawn into *Ra's al-Zanab*, their permanent abode. Their powers are also greatly increased during that time, for the opponent of the *dawah* has performed good deeds in his life; the reward for those deeds will not come to him in the hereafter, but in this very world he is given free rein, and that is the reward he receives.

As for the *dhabihah*, its meat is at the level of *yawm an-na*, ascending in human form. Yet many eat of it — fifty people separately. How nature keeps the account of what belongs to a single unit, say the goat of a particular individual, when it has been divided among fifty, only God knows. But it has entered the state of ascending, and though divided among many, it remains in that state.

Great is the importance of *Bismillah*. The Prophet says in one of his prayers:

> *Bismillahilladhi la yadurru maa ismihi shayun fil-ardi wa la fi's-samai wa huwa as-samiul-alim*

In the name of Allah, with whose name nothing in the earth or the heavens can cause harm, and He is the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing.

The *Bismillah* carries such power and strength that nothing can harm the one who says it, neither in the earth nor in the sky. This prayer is well established and known even among the general people. Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd has also mentioned it in his collection of prayers, with mention of reciting it three times in the morning and three times at night, and it is a promise from Allah conveyed through the tongue of the Messenger that no harm will come to the one who does so.

The matter of the *dhabihah*, that which is made *halal*, is thus understood. As for the animals that are not made *halal*, their matter goes into the *darakat* (دَرَكَات), the lower levels — absolutely. How subtle a matter this is in the world. The greatness of making something *halal* is a great virtue: one utterance of *Allahu Akbar* makes a creature *halal*. Those who eat meat that comes from outside ought to exercise great caution regarding how it has been prepared.

The meat of the *dhabihah* of Eid al-Adha should not be given to a non-Muslim, and generally it is not given; nor to those who are paid wages. The meat of the religious offering is a different matter. As for people who take their Eid al-Adha *dhabihah* home and distribute it themselves, what they do is their own responsibility. But the meat of the *dhabihah* of Eid, and likewise the meat of the *'aqiqah* (عَقِيقَةٌ), should not be given to a non-Muslim. And the mother whose child's *aqiqah* (عَقِيقَة) has been performed should not eat that meat either, since the child is still a nursing infant. If the mother eats it, its nutrition will enter her body, form her strength, and form her milk. The child will drink that milk, and for him that milk is not *halal*. This is generally not known to people. She should not eat it, not even the soup made from it.

How many dimensions there are within what is *halal*. If two animals are brought and one remains alive while the second shivers from the cold and dies, the dead one is simply a carcass to be discarded, while the living one is slaughtered with *Allahu Akbar*. An animal that dies its own death dies a bad death, with the exception of the pigeons of the Baitullah. If the pigeons of the Baitullah die their own death, that death is *halal*; they are not to be slaughtered, nor eaten by way of slaughter, but their natural death is *halal*. This question was put to the *duat*: since there are pigeons at the Baitullah and also pigeons in Madinah al-Munawwarah, are the pigeons of Madinah like those of the Baitullah? The *Dai* Sayyidna Ismail bin Hibatullah replied: "No, the pigeons of Madinah are not like the pigeons of Baitullah, because the station of the pigeons of Baitullah is very high."

The *riyat*, the subtle essence, of a buried believer rises within three days, ascends to Jupiter, then to the moon, and at the level of *yawm an-na* descends as completely pure and transparent matter to form the body of the Imam. The *fudala*, the remains, go into the *buruj* (بُروج), the constellations, to be refined. Many things — the hard bones, the teeth, the skull — take a very long time to be cleansed, while the soft tissue ascends more readily. After refinement, these remaining things emerge in three forms.

1. They become *jawahir*, jewels. For those who are the *fudala* of the *hudud al-dawah* who performed great service in their lives, even their remains are honored — as *fallahu khayrun hafizan wa huwa arhamur-rahimin*, for Allah is the best of guardians and He is the most merciful of the merciful — and they become jewels set in the crowns of kings or preserved in their treasuries.

2. They become pigeons, those very pigeons whose natural death is *halal* and who need not be slaughtered. Remarkably, they do not perform *bawl-o-baraz*, urination and defecation. The animal that ordinarily produces the most *bawl-o-baraz* is the pigeon, a nuisance in any house it inhabits. Yet the pigeons of the Baitullah do not make a mess, nor do they fly over the Baitullah itself, and the pigeons of Madinah also do not make a mess. This nobility is theirs because they are formed from the *fudala* of the *hudud al-dawah*.

3. They become the salt of which it is said:

> *al-milhu qabla kulli taamin wa badahu yadfau sabina daan ahwanuha al-judham wal-junun*

Salt before and after every meal wards off seventy diseases, the least of which are leprosy and insanity.

Such is the nature of that salt. It is not the ordinary salt used for cooking and other purposes, but the salt that nature refines and brings to the table, tasted at the beginning and the end of a meal. It carries such blessing that it wards off diseases, and it is formed from the bones of the *hudud al-dawah*.

As for wild animals that die their own death, they are not slaughtered; they simply die, and they remain in a fallen form. Yet in *yawm an-na* their resurrection must also occur. As for the disbelievers and the polytheists, because *shirk* is a great wrong, progress will not happen for them until they recite the *kalimah* (كَلِمَةٌ) of *La ilaha illallah*.

The *dawah* is presented to them at every death, and when they change bodies (قَالِبٌ), Islam is presented to them as well. This continues until they fall so far as to enter the body of tar (دَمَّار). At that point the *dawah* is no longer presented to them. There is a prayer in the *munajat* (مُنَاجَاتٌ): *Wa libas al-qatiran la tulinni* — do not make me wear the garment of tar — meaning, may I never fall so far as to reach that state. In present times that garment is worn in the form of nylon and whatever else is now in use, for nylon is made from petroleum, and the final residue of petroleum is tar.

There is a thing made of thread and a thing made of air, of which there is neither emptiness nor fullness. In the air no thing is empty; rather, all things are filled. It is the eye that lacks the capacity to see this. This is why it is said that one should not become naked, for the things present in the air exert their influence upon a person.

*Wa lidhalika mathaltu 'ala qatl al-mukhalifina inda qiyam (قِيَام) qayam yawm ad-din*: and for this reason, when the *Qayam* (قَائِم) rises on the Day of the Hereafter, these opponents will be killed. The opponents meant are the *azdad*, the opposites. And the *nafs hissiyyah* was itself from the opposites of the ranks.
