Those opposites of the ranks will be given a human garment and will come forward near the time of *Qiyamah*. They will appear dressed in human form because this *Qayam* who is coming occupies a very high station, the station of the seventh *kashf* and the seventh *sidr* (سِدْر). For such a *Qayam*, what kind of assembly will there be? From ancient times, those who were the opposites — who had died and been destroyed — will be presented before the *Qayam* in physical form. Yazid, Muawiyah, Marwan bin Hakam, Nimrud, Firawn — all of them will be brought in human form to the plain of Mina. Nature will drag them, as a flock is driven, and they will be brought to that plain. The *Qayam ala dhikrihi as-salam* (قَائِمٌ عَلَى ذِكْرِهِ السَّلَامُ) will command the believers to kill them. They will do so, and the plain of Mina will be entirely covered with their corpses, until such a stench arises that the believers declare they cannot remain in that condition. They will complain to the Imam, who will command the wind, and that wind will blow and carry the corpses toward *safrah* (صَفْرَاء). There are four doors to that *safrah*, one of which is in Hadramaut. The mention of all four doors is recorded in a book by Sayyidna Ali bin Sulayman. The corpses will be taken inside, and their duration there is one *qawr-e-azam*, one great epoch — *illa ma rahima rabbi*, except for those upon whom my Lord has shown mercy. Those who had performed some good deeds will, on account of those deeds, be released before the full *qawr-e-azam* has elapsed. That release comes through volcanoes: they emerge as lava.

The example is 'Umar bin Abd al-Aziz, a caliph of the Muslims from the line of Muawiyah, from the Banu Umayyah, who did two things of consequence. The *Bagh-e-Fadak*, which had been usurped by Umar ibn al-Khattab and thereafter remained in the possession of successive governments, he returned to the Ahl al-Bayt. That was one. The second was that from seventy thousand pulpits the cursing and reviling of Sayyidna Ali and his progeny had been carried out, and even the keeping of the name Ali had been forbidden; he put a stop to that practice, declaring it an injustice upon the Ahl al-Bayt. Because of these two deeds his duration in that state was shortened, and he was released through a volcano before the full *qawr-e-azam*. A gate in that courtyard bears the name Abd al-Aziz, though the Abd al-Aziz of that name is theirs, from the Saudis.

The lava that comes out is a good form for them, for by it they emerge. That lava then becomes soil, and from that soil vegetation grows, and from the vegetation comes food, and so it ascends and is refined.

And then there is *Jabal 'Arafat*.

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. O Allah, convey this Fatihah as a gift, a means, and a noble and pleasing sacrifice to the soul of Sayyidna 'Ali bin Hibatullah, by the right of the master of the messengers, Sayyidna Muhammad al-Mustafa, and the great *Qaim*, and his pure and holy Ahl al-Bayt (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ); and by the intention of the master of the Husayni *dawah* from the Sindhi lineage, Sayyidna Husayn bin Sayyidna Muhammad, Muqaddas, and his father, Sayyidna Muhammad bin Hibatullah, Muqaddas. Al-Fatihah.

<!-- editorial:begin -->
> **Editorial note (tradition-grounded).** 
> A related teaching preserved in this tradition puts the logic of sacrifice plainly: the idea
> behind it is to bring a lower form of life to an end so that a new journey toward a higher form
> of life may begin — for when the meat of the sacrificed animal is eaten, the body digests it, it
> is absorbed into the bloodstream, and it is converted into energy. That may help a modern reader
> see why elevation upon the upright lineage, rather than mere ritual killing, is the point. The
> wider tradition also joins prayer and ablution as a working pair that delivers a person from the
> mothers of sins — pride, greed, and envy — the root of every spiritual impurity, from which all
> other impurities are born. Read alongside the hadith that prayer is the sacrifice of every
> God-fearing person, the two images illuminate one another: the knife outward, the purification
> inward.
<!-- editorial:end -->

## 24. Standing at 'Arafat

The noble Mount 'Arafat carries beneath it a subtle secret. On the Day of Arafah, forms shine in that place such as have never crossed any human heart. The passage reads: *Wa amma Jabal Arafat al-sharif, fa-tahtahu sirrun latifun (سِرٌّ لَطِيفٌ), wa fi Yawm Arafah tashruqu fi dhalika al-mahall, min al-suwar, ma la yakhturu ala qalbi basharin*.

Jabal 'Arafat is the *maqam* (مَقَامٌ) (station) of Arafat, the *maydan* (plain) of Arafat, and it is obligatory for the *haji* (pilgrim) to set foot there. Among the six days of Hajj, Arafat is the *ruh* (spirit) of all of them. A person who goes for Hajj without enough time to perform the Umrah of *Tamattu'*, yet reaches 'Arafat on the ninth day of the month, has completed his Hajj. Even without reaching Mina, reaching Arafat alone suffices. Such is the *azmah* (greatness) of Arafat.

The three *maqamat* (مَقَامَات) (stations) — Mina, 'Arafat, and Muzdalifah — are each associated with one of three great souls. Arafat is associated with Mawla-i Kainat, Mawla Rasulullah. The *maqam* of Mina is associated with Janab-i Ali (ع). Muzdalifah is associated with Janab-i Bibi Fatimah. In the *Dawr al-Satr* (Period of Concealment), these three are personalities without equal — the *lubab* (essence), the *lubb al-lubab* (very essence) of the *Dawr al-Satr*.

Regarding 'Arafat: arrival must come before *ghurub al-shams* (sunset). The *haji* must set foot there before sunset, for with sunset the next day begins — the tenth. On the ninth, one must reach Arafat and touch the ground before the sun goes down. Jabal Arafat holds its own *sharafah* (nobility) for all who reach it, for there the *khuddam* (خُدَّامٌ) appeared. It is the most noble of places.

*Wa li-hadha, fa-tahtahu sirrun latifun*: beneath it lies a *sirrun latifun*, a subtle secret. On Yawm 'Arafah, *wahi* (وَحْي) (revelation) descended upon Rasulullah without interruption. What must his state have been as he drew near the plain of Arafat? The revelation came so heavily, and so many times, that his she-camel would lower herself under its weight. It is related that the she-camel would repeatedly sit down from that weight, and people would try to raise her, but Rasulullah would say, "Do not lift her; she sits down from the weight of the revelation." The purity and luminosity of Rasulullah's blessed heart is measured by this: an animal could not bear what descended upon it. The Quran and its *anwar* (lights) descended upon that heart.

On Yawm 'Arafah, *ishraq* (illumination) occurs in that place from *suwar* (forms) such that *ma la yakhturu ala qalbi basharin* — what has never crossed the heart of any human. Such *suwar-i nayyirah* (luminous forms) manifest and shine there. The most intense *ishraq* of that day was connected to Ghadeer-i Khumm, by reason of that imminent arrival. Rasulullah was heading toward Ghadeer afterward. One reason for this intensity was the exalted station itself and its association with Rasulullah; another was that the noble Kalam was nearly complete — only the final *surah* relating to the succession remained. All the *wahi* descending, all the *anwar* and *ahkam* (commands) coming from Allah's side, bore upon this, and it was on account of this that the she-camel was in that condition.

Immediately after Hajj, Rasulullah set out for Medina. On the thirteenth of the month he was in Makkah, and there he informed Janab-i 'Aishah that Allah had commanded him to appoint Ali as his *wasi* (successor) and *khalifah* (caliph), asking her to keep the matter secret. She went and told her father Abu Bakr. Abu Bakr then told Umar, and their scheming began.

When Rasulullah reached the approach to Ghadeer-i Khumm, the company was passing through an extremely dark valley at night. Certain *munafiqun* (hypocrites) drew their daggers with the intention of attacking him. But by Allah's power, lightning flashed — so brightly, so forcefully, and for so long — that Rasulullah saw all of their faces clearly. Among those who witnessed this was Hudhayfah al-Yamani. Rasulullah said, "Hudhayfah, did you see what I saw?" He replied, "Yes, O Rasulullah, I also saw." Rasulullah then said, "I am instructing you to keep this a secret and not tell anyone." Hudhayfah al-Yamani kept that trust for as long as Rasulullah lived, telling no one. Afterward, 'Umar ibn al-Khattab would persistently ask Hudhayfah al-Yamani, "Was I among those who attacked the Prophet?" Hudhayfah would reply, "Rasulullah made me swear not to say this to anyone." But Umar would say, "By Allah, I was among them." These are great spectacles of history. As for the route: Rasulullah came to Makkah, and from Makkah went directly to Ghadeer-i Khumm; he did not return to Medina first.

*Wa li-hadha tunfara fihi al-dhunub*: for this reason, sins are dispersed there. Through the shining of these *suwar*, sins are forgiven, faults are pardoned, and *mawaiz* and *balaya* (trials and tribulations) are removed and finished. *Wa fi al-mawaqifi qumairun tahirah wa asrarun zahirah*: in the stopping places are pure hidden treasures and manifest secrets. In the *mawaqif* — Mina, Muzdalifah, and 'Arafat — at these stations of standing, there are *khamair* (خَمَائِرُ) (hidden essences) of great purity and secrets that are manifest. Among them are things such as *tayy al-ard* (طَيُّ الْأَرْض) (the folding of the earth).

*Wa li-hadha hurrima 'ala al-anam qatl al-ashjar*: for this reason it has been forbidden for mankind to cut down the trees and shrubs there. *Fi tilka al-jiha al-lati nahat fiha al-asrar*: in those regions in which the secrets are contained. *Fa-hiya mujawiratun li-tilka al-khamair al-kaminah*: for the trees are neighbors to those hidden *khamair*, mingled with the *dhubat al-batinah* (hidden essences).

*Wa hadhihi qatratun min bihar 'ilm Mawla-i al-Alam*: and this is a drop from the oceans of the knowledge of the Master of the World. *Wa sadaqatun min jumlati sadaqatihi al-mursalah ala abdihi al-mawjud al-mujid lahu min al-adam*: and a charity from his many charities, sent upon his servant whom He brought into existence from nothingness. The servant was nothing at all, and from nothingness He made him, and made him capable of giving answers and clarifying realities. This is said about his *walid* (father).

*Jazahu Allahu 'an imami wa sayyidi afdala ma jaza al-duat*: may Allah reward him on behalf of my Imam and my master with the best of what the *duat* (supplicants and propagators) are rewarded. *Wa razaqna minhu maqbul al-shafaat*: and grant us sustenance from his accepted intercessions. *Wa sunna an al-bayni al-ladhi ulqiya alayka bi-haqqi mawlaya*: and may we be protected from that veil which has been cast upon you, by the right of my Mawla, which is established upon you. *Wa man adhaa shayan min hadhihi al-asrar fa-qad tabawwaa maqadahu min al-nar*: and whoever broadcasts anything of these secrets has certainly prepared his seat in the Fire. *Wa bariun min al-Aziz al-Ghaffar*: and he is disavowed by the Mighty, the Forgiving. Disavowed means disassociated — Allah will be free of him. So too in Surah al-Baraah, *baraatun min Allahi*: displeasure is declared, that the displeasure of Allah and His Messenger rests upon the polytheists. That is the meaning of it: to become independent of, to be free from.

<!-- editorial:begin -->
> **Editorial note (tradition-grounded).** 
> A note on a word: the phrase quoted here — *ma la yakhturu ala qalbi basharin*, what has never
> crossed the heart of any *bashar* — turns on a term whose root repays attention. The wider
> tradition traces b-sh-r first to the outer skin, and from it *bashar* comes to mean humankind:
> mortals defined by that skin, creatures of surface and limit. The same root branches into
> *bushra*, glad tidings, because good news is precisely what visibly brightens the skin of the
> face. So the expression carries a quiet double edge for a modern reader: the luminous forms said
> to shine on the Day of 'Arafah exceed what any skin-bound heart could conceive, yet the very
> word for that creature is kin to the word for the joy that shows itself openly on a face.
<!-- editorial:end -->

## 25. Prayer Outside the Island: The Eleventh Question

The eleventh question comes from Sayyidna Amin al-Mutaman (أَمِينُ الْمُؤْتَمَن) Sulayman ibn Hasan. The title Amin al-Mutaman was conferred upon him by Sayyidna Daud ibn 'Ajabshah, who had two *nussus* (نُصُوص) (texts of appointment) written for him.

The first *nass* is very long, written by the hand of Daud ibn Qutb. At its conclusion he wrote *ana turabu nalayhi* — that he, Daud ibn Qutb, was the dust beneath the feet of Sayyidna Sulayman ibn Hasan. The second *nass* was dictated to Sayyidi Raj three days before his passing, in the year 970 Hijri.

In the first *nass*, Sayyidna Daud ibn Ajabshah employed twenty-eight distinct titles. Sayyidna Muhammad ibn Fa'd states that these twenty-eight titles point to the *darajat* (دَرَجَات) (ranks) of Sayyidna Sulayman — that he was the possessor of twenty-eight exalted titles corresponding to the twenty-eight *huruf* (letters) of the *Kalimah al-Shahadah* (كَلِمَةُ الشَّهَادَةِ). After this *nass*, whoever recites "La ilaha illa Allah, Muhammadun Rasulullah, Aliyyun Waliyullah" is required to bear an additional *shahadah*, a testimony for Sulayman ibn Hasan; otherwise he does not remain within the circle of Imamah. This was established by Sayyidna Muhammad ibn Fa'd al-Alawi. When Sayyidna Daud passed away, his two sons Ibrahim and Ahmad were present with him, along with his wife.

Sayyidna Muhammad ibn Fa'd offered magnificent praise for Sayyidna Sulayman. In one passage he states that Sayyidna Sulayman ibn Hasan stood, in relation to the Imam of his time, at the *maqam-i nasut* (station of humanity), while in relation to his *abna-i jins* (fellow believers) — the circle of believers beneath him — he stood at the *maqam-i lahut* (station of divinity). This is a most profound declaration.

*Wa-iqamatuhu li-waladihi fi ardhi birayn*: and his establishing of his son in the land of the two wells. Sayyidna Sulayman ibn Hasan established his son Jafar at the place of two wells while he was traveling. The precise details of this incident are not fully known, since some accounts were abbreviated in transmission.

Then the matter of Sayyidna Muhammad ibn Ismail, *qaddasa Allahu ruhahu*, the one who appointed Sayyidna Hibatullah as Dai and served as his support. *Fa-qad samitu annahu salla jamaatan fi tarfi bilad al-habashah*: it is reported that he led a congregation in prayer in the lands of Habashah. *Fa-la-amri la-qad faqa man mada*: by Allah's oath, he surpassed all who had come before him in this regard, for the *duat* had never previously led prayer outside their designated three islands. The questioner then says: *fa-haqqiq li-abdika al-faqir fi hadhihi al-masalah, bi-haqqi fulan, bi-haqqi fulan* — so investigate this matter for your humble servant, by the right of such-and-such, by the right of such-and-such. *Wa-lakin akhafu an tatlaa 'ala dafi aqli*: but I fear that you will perceive the weakness of my intellect. *Wa-qad udtu bi-dhambi wa-dhilli wa-dhalili fa-ya harrun*: I come burdened with my sins, my abasement, and my lowliness, so O my protector.

As for the three islands: the Dai of Iran would lead prayer within Iran, but the Dai of Yemen was appointed over three specific islands and would not lead prayer outside them. Habashah and other regions each had their own Dais. The island of Sham, for instance, is a distinct island, and no Dai of Yemen prays there. The Dai of Yemen is superior in rank to the *duat* of all other islands, yet even so he confines himself to the three islands — Hind, Sindh, and Yemen. When Sayyidna 'Ali ibn Husayn al-Makrami traveled to London, he did not lead prayer there, as it lay outside his island. Likewise, no Dai may lead prayer outside his own island or within another's island. The *mansub* (appointee) stationed in Najran holds a *mansub* superior to all other *manasib* (appointments), even if appointed at a later time, because his nobility and station are elevated: he is, *bi-l-quwwah* (by potential), the successor of the Dai, possessing the eligibility to become the Dai's khalifah regardless of how many other *manasib* there may be.

*Wa-qad ahattat bi dhambi wa-yuhallu bi qarbi*: my sin has encompassed me, yet it is resolved by my closeness to you. Pardon my sins and resolve the debt that weighs upon me. *Wa-lakin aqulu ya Allahu ya man azhara al-jamila wa-satara al-qabiha*: but I say — O Allah, O You who make the beautiful manifest and conceal the ugly. This is in fact the supplication of Husayn ibn 'Ali. *Fa-mnun alayya bi-ihsanika wa-fadlika*: so favor me with Your grace and Your bounty. *Fa-kayfa akhafu wa-ihsanuka awsau wa-fadluka indi ajmau*: how then should I fear, when Your favor is most vast and Your grace is most complete with me, and blessings have been continuously bestowed upon me? *Wa-la ziltu fi hifzi al-dai wa-husni riayatihi*: and I have never ceased to be in the protection and good care of the Dai — meaning in Allah's protection. By the right of Sayyidna Muhammad and his pure and noble family.

The answer to that question follows, with the help of Allah the Great and Exalted, by the bounty of the Wali of the Age — may the blessings of the Merciful be upon him — and with the support of Mawla, the Most Luminous Secret and the Great Ocean, and with the support of Mawla Hibatullah and his shining proof.

When *ifadah* (إِفَاضَة) and *istifadah* (imparting and receiving benefit) take place — when the one who imparts knowledge and the one who receives it sit together — the *suwar* (souls) of believers who have passed away, who carried within them a longing for knowledge, find that longing still alive, though they can no longer act upon it. They have no body, and thus cannot pray, fast, perform Hajj, engage in Jihad, or carry out any deed pertaining to bodily existence. Yet the quest for knowledge persists even after death. When such *majalis* (gatherings) are held — gatherings in which the words of the Awliya Allah are spoken and discussed, in which *ifadah* and *istifadah* take place — those souls gather there. And when a questioner puts forth a question, in reality it is not the questioner alone who asks; through his tongue, those souls ask their question. It is as though the *hadd-i 'ali* (حَدِّ عَالِي) (high-ranking representative) causes the question to be asked through him.

An incident bears upon this. Dhul-Maali, in the old Ziyarah Muqaddasah, had been giving *asbaq* (lessons) in his house rather than in the mosque after the Fajr prayer, on account of pain in his leg. Then a matter arose: a spirit possessed the mother of Hadrat Ziyarah Sahib Muqaddas, casting a shadow over her. When Hadrat Ziyarah Sahib Muqaddas questioned the spirit through his knowledge, it emerged that she was the soul of a Bohra (بُهْرَة) woman. He rebuked her, but she said she had come with no ill intention — that she wished to enter the noble community. He then administered the *'ahd al-sharif* (noble covenant) to her, and she departed.

After some time she returned. He rebuked her sharply, reminding her of her promise to leave and not come back. She replied that she had not come of her own accord but had been sent — sent by his Mawla and hers, Fardali Sahib Muqaddas, who had already passed away. She had been sent with three messages:

1. The *asbaq* being conducted at the Ziyah Manzil should be moved to the mosque, because the *suwarat* (souls) who attend have a longing to hear them and gather there for that purpose.
2. Jafar Ali Sahib — the forefather of Maqsood bhai and Safdar bhai now in Hyderabad — was building a house and was sitting beneath a wall for shade while supervising the workers; he should be warned that the wall was about to fall and he must not sit there.
3. A third matter was also conveyed, though its precise details have not been retained in memory.

Such is the manner in which these things come about, and the manner in which the spiritual dimension enters into worldly affairs. Similarly, it is recounted that Sayyidna Sulayman ibn Hasan was once delivering a sermon in a *majlis* with the *ikhwan* present, when Sayyidna 'Ali ibn Sulayman stood up in the midst of it and said, "Mawla, you have graced my *majlis* with your presence, and it is a great honor that you have come. Please pray for me." Then he sat down. The gathering was astonished. This happened on several occasions, and when people asked what was occurring, he said that Sayyidna Sulayman ibn Hasan had come — that he had perceived his presence there.

When the Daudis filed a case in the time of Sayyidna 'Ali, Sayyidna Ali and Hasan Khan ibn Abd al-Malik were required to appear in court. The night before their appearance, Sayyidna Jafar would come to Sayyidna Ali Sahib in a dream and say: "Tomorrow the judge will ask you this question. Give this answer." The next day they would appear, the judge would ask the very question foretold, and they would give the prescribed answer. On another occasion, when they were greatly troubled by a government matter, a person appeared before both Sayyidna Ali ibn Sulayman and Sayyidna Hasan Khan ibn Abd al-Malik and recited a verse of the Quran to them:

> *La takhafa, inna ana maakuma*

Do not fear, for I am with you both. With this they were consoled and their hearts steadied.

Another incident: Sayyidna Husayn ibn Ahmad had been placed under a form of house arrest in Taif (الطَّائِف). From there he would come to Baitullah, and during his stays in Makkah he would perform seven *tawaf* every day without fail. On one occasion, while performing *tawaf*, he raised both hands and cried out, "*Wa shauqah, wa shauqah*" — O my longing, O my longing. The companions and *hudud* who were with him had never witnessed such a state in him, and afterward they asked him about it. He said that he had been seeing three *suwar* — the souls of Sayyidi Fardali Sahib Muqaddas, Sayyidi Ziyali Sahib Muqaddas, and Sayyidna Ghulam Husayn Sahib Muqaddas — performing *tawaf* before him. They were circling with great speed, but he, encumbered by his body, could not match their pace. The sight of them intensified his longing, his *shauq*, because he could not perform *tawaf* as they did.

On yet another occasion, a person in Najran recounted that he was sitting beside Sayyidna Husayn ibn Ismail, both of them reciting — for Sayyidna Husayn ibn Ismail was known to recite the Quran abundantly and with great speed. As they were reciting, a figure appeared and extended his hand toward Sayyidna Husayn ibn Ismail. The moment Sayyidna Husayn ibn Ismail saw him, he kissed the figure's hand and immediately rose to his feet, and at that moment the figure vanished. It is possible that this was Imam 'Ali (ع) himself.

With the support of Mawla-i 'Alam and his magnificent secret, the affair of Sayyidna Sulayman ibn Hasan is taken up — Sulayman-i Humam (سُلَيْمَانِ هُمَام), Sulayman al-Imam. The account of Sulayman-i Humam is that in the *jazirah* (island) he is Sulayman, and one of his forms is established in Hadramawt, whose name is Humam. This has not occurred with anyone else. The Imam of the time is the thirty-fifth Imam, and he is the seventh (*sabi'*) of his cycle (هَفْتَه). Nor is that all: he is included among those four Imams — Sayyidna Ahmad ibn Muhammad among them — whose greatness and station are very high. Two cycles are thus combined in him; he is the possessor of fourteen Imams. On account of these circumstances his station is very high, and when the Imam's station is high, the Dai's station also becomes high.

*Wa-iqamatuhu li-waladihi Sayyidna Jafar*: and his establishing of his son, Sayyidna Jafar — he established his son in Birayn, among the people. *Wa-dhalika kama faala asluhu al-azim fi al-dawr al-qadim*: and that is as his great forefather did in the ancient period. This matter came about just as it did in the ancient period, when Sulayman ibn Hasan and Jafar ibn Sulayman appeared — it was done then, and so it has been done again now. What that incident was is not known. *Allahu yahdi man yashau ila siratin mustaqim*: Allah guides whom He wills to a straight path. *Wa-aydhan lamma ittasalat maddatu imamatihi ilayhi*: and also, when the substance of his Imamah connected to him — that is, when the Imam's confirmations reached him — *dhakara qurbanahu wa-mathubatahu*: he mentioned his sacrifice and his offering, that the sacrifice should be presented.

*Wa-amma sallatu Sayyidna Badruddin*, and as for the prayer of Sayyidna Badruddin, *'ala qudsuhu fi lina kama dhakarta fi ghayri hadhihi al-jazirah al-yamaniyyah*: may his soul be sanctified, in a land as mentioned, in other than this Yemeni island. The prayer led by Sayyidna Badruddin — that is, Sayyidna Muhammad ibn Ismail — his leading of the prayer, as the questioner mentioned, in other than the island of Yemen: that island was not Yemen's. The prayer he led in Habashah was thus *fa-dhalika li-izzati rutbatihi al-bariah*: on account of his sublime and great rank. That matter came to pass because his rank is very sublime and great among the people. *Wa-li-ihtijabi imamihi anhu bi-suratihi al-muazzamah*: and because his Imam became veiled from him with his magnificent form — the Imam becoming veiled from him with his magnificent form in the island. *Wal-idhnu lahu muadhdhinan lahu bi-l-salah fi ghayri jazirah al-mukkaramah*: and the permission for him to lead the prayer in other than the honored island — the Imam gave him permission to lead the prayer outside the honored island, that is, outside the island of Yemen. *Wa-lahu shanun azimun*: and his is a great station. *Wa-khatbun jasimun*: and a weighty matter. *Wa-ma hajara min ardhihi wa-baladihi illa li-adhami shanihi*: and he did not migrate from his land and his country except for the greatness of his station. He did not migrate from his land and his country but on account of his great and magnificent state. He was in Safan, in Yemen, but he migrated to Najran and went to Habuna, and did so because of that greatness and magnificence. *Wa-quwwatihi al-ruhaniyyah wa-li-ihyai marasim al-dawah* (مَرَاسِمُ الدَّعْوَةِ): and for the strength of his spiritual power and to revive the rituals of the Dawah — that is, on account of his spiritual powers and in order to revive the rituals and secrets of the Dawah.

Was there a personal meeting with the Imam? No. The Imam's luminous form became veiled from him. How that occurs is not known. There is a treatise by Kamaluddin — Sayyidna Husayn ibn Ahmad himself — *Al-Hijab wal-Muhtajib*, The Veil and the Veiled. It is a small treatise, yet very difficult; what he intends to say is not easily grasped. These states lie beyond comprehension — how a veiled one is veiled by a veil, because the veil is in reality...

The lesson was brought to a close at this point, and the Fatihah was presented as a gift and a connection, pure and pleasing, to the soul of Sayyidna 'Ali ibn Hibatullah; may Allah sanctify his soul and illuminate his resting place, and may his blessings, lights, and supports be multiplied, and may He accept this imparting and receiving of benefit in the best possible way. By the right of Sayyidna Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon them.

> Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi Rabb al-alamin, al-Rahman al-Rahim, Maliki Yawm al-Din. Iyyaka nabudu wa iyyaka nastain. Ihdina al-sirat al-mustaqim. Sirat al-ladhina anamta 'alayhim, ghayr al-maghdubi alayhim wa-la al-dallin. Amin.

And with the intention:

> *Wa-bi-niyyati Sayyidi Mawlaya al-Mansub al-Mutlaq fi al-Jazirah al-Hindiyyah al-Sharifah ila al-Faqih al-Nabil, al-Alim al-Jalil al-Hajj al-Sharafi Usamauddin Ghulam Husayn ibn Sayyid al-Mansub al-Muqaddas Nasrullah ibn Hibatullah ibn al-Mansub al-Muqaddas Fathullah. Tawwala wa-abqaahu Allahu Taala, yahfazuhu wa-yahmihi wa-yubqihi bi-tayyibi 'ayshihi min sharri jami' al-amrad wal-asqam, min sharri al-awja wal-alam, min sharri al-ghilal wal-hijam, min sharri al-nufus al-khabisat wal-fusufat, min al-taqdidat wal-tathrisat, wa-biati al-munazarat, wa-yudda wa-yazid fi nasrihi wa-yahfazuhu fi mauni 'amrihi min aybi al-nawafis wal-nuqsan, wa-yahmihi fi al-hifazat al-rabbaniyyah al-shahaniyyah, wa-tawala Allahu fi tuli hayatihi, wa-afali wa-kalamihi wa-darajatihi, wa-yashkurana iyyakum fi saati daawatihi, wa-yadhkuruna iyyakum fi anfai hadaratihi, bi-jahi Sayyid al-Mursalin, Sayyidina Mawlana al-Mubayyin wa-Ahl Baytihi al-Tayyibin al-Tahirin, salawatullahi wa-salamuhu alayhim ajmain. Al-Fatihah.*

The lesson continues tomorrow, InshaAllah.

<!-- editorial:begin -->
> **Editorial note (tradition-grounded).** 
> **A note for the reader.** Because this chapter counts the letters of the *Kalimah al-Shahadah*,
> it may help to recall how the wider tradition reads that same formula. A related teaching
> preserved in this tradition holds that the First Intellect established the *tawhid* of the
> Almighty through a dual path of negation and affirmation contained in the utterance of sincere
> devotion: "*La ilaha illa 'Allah*" carries only these two movements — the denial and the
> confirmation — with no third way between them. The same tradition dwells on the definite article
> in Arabic, noting that "a book" (*kitab*) may be any book while "the book" (*al-kitab*) marks
> out one particular book, and reading the three letters of *al-ilah* — alif, lam, and ha — as
> themselves signifying degrees of meaning. Readers weighing the significance assigned here to the
> letters of the testimony may find that background useful.
<!-- editorial:end -->

## 26. Seven Ranks Within Every Rank

The discussion had turned to prayer in the *ghayr jazirah*, and Habashah came into the conversation. A *dawah* exists there, but its people do not give allegiance to the Dai. The matter continued up to the time of Sayyidna Husayn bin Ismail and then Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan. Ali bin Ali related that Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan had sent him to Habashah to establish a *qiyam* there, and that the *qiyam* was indeed established upon his return. A Sajjad also related that Syedi sahib had said that his own father, in the last phase of his life, would serve as the Dai of Habashah. The Prophet had not given permission for that in the *ghayr jazirah*, and permission for prayer was granted in the *ghayr jazirah* of Makkah al-Mukarramah.

The text before them read: *wa lahu shanun 'adhim wa maqamun jasim* — he has a great station and a lofty rank. His station is very great and his rank very high. Then followed: *wa ma hajar min ardihi wa baldatihi illa li-adhm shanihi* — he did not migrate from his land and his city except on account of the greatness of his station. The *hijrah* from the land of his birth, from that land and from his city, was undertaken for no other reason than his greatness and his station. The people of Satan were what they were, and the *hijrah* was performed because of that great station.

Long afterward, Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan traveled to the very places where Sayyidna Mahmud bin Ismail had performed *hijrah*, and remained there for several months. He built a *masjid* there, and he built a *Dar al-Hijrah* (دَارُ الْهِجْرَة) there as well. Later, in the present age, Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan built a magnificent *Dar al-Hijrah* in that place, together with a very splendid *masjid*.

Of a visit to that place the account runs in the speaker's own words: "So then when we went there, we were sitting in the *masjid*. I said, 'Malik, if you permit, I will recite about Sayyidna Sulayman sahib.' He said, 'Yes.' I recited the *qasidah*. It was a very long *majlis*, and Sayyidna sahib kept listening with concentration."

The text continued: *wa li-ihyai marasim al-dawah* — for the revival of the ceremonies of the Dawah. The *marasim al-dawah* are those written documents and the *nusus* that had existed; he reissued them and brought them again to notice. Everything he did within the Dawah, he did. *Wa fiiluhu bi-al-wail* — and his action by the earlier ones, who were lying in the abyss until he brought them into action, making people aware that these things had been written in the past, that these documents had been made, and that the *duat* had ordained these writings apart from the books. All of that he manifested.

Then: *wa li-istikhraj al-sawa al-kamil min bilad al-Yaman* — for extracting the complete secrets from the lands of Yemen. The things that lay hidden in the lands of Yemen he brought to light. *Min al-sadis*, from the sixth — *shurur al-sharifah*, the noble secrets — he brought to light.

Then: *wa law tulina al-kalam* — and if the speech were prolonged. To speak fully on this subject would carry it very far. The ranks of the *duat* are *rutab al-aliyah* (رُتَبٌ الْعَالِيَةُ), high ranks, and those ranks, along with the other ranks besides them, differ among themselves *fi 'uluww al-darajat* — in the loftiness of degrees. Rank is not of one type, for the degrees themselves are lofty.

*Li-anna kull al-martabah fi dhatiha sab rutab* — because every rank in itself has seven ranks — *yu-khafi min al-asrar al-thaniyah*, hidden from the second secrets. Every rank divides into seven ranks according to the high secrets that belong to it. If there is a *dai*, that one *dai* is divided into seven ranks, and among them is one who stands in the highest of the seven. Sometimes a *dai* stands in that highest rank.

*Inda dhawi al-adhan al-saqiyah* — with those of piercing intellect. *Wa minhum man yakunu fi awsatihi* — and among them is one who is in its middle. Some *duat* stand in the middle of these ranks, *tilka al-rutab al-madhkurah* — those mentioned ranks, whose mention has been made. And among them is one who is in the lowest rank; some *duat* stand at its lowest.

Asked who decides which rank a man occupies, he answered that it is the Imam of the time who decides which *dai* is in which rank, and that the declaration comes later. Succeeding *duat* say that so-and-so was in such a rank, that his rank was this. The matter is recorded in the books, and some *duat* have also expressed their ranks, saying that this one was in such-and-such a rank and that one in such-and-such a rank.

Among the *duat*, there are also those who, on account of their past *sawabit* (ثَوَابِت) and their *hasanat* — the good deeds that were theirs — come *ila adna rutab al-hujjiyah*, to the lowest rank of the *rutbah* (رُتْبَةٌ) of *hujjiyah* (حُجِّيَّة). They leave the rank of *dai* and enter the lowest rank of *hujjat*. *Ma fawqaha min rutab al-aliyah* — what lies above it from the high ranks — they may ascend to a rank even higher than that, since the rank of *hujjat* likewise contains several ranks, and they enter into those as well.

A *hujjat* is of two types:

- The rank above the *duat*, which is the rank of *hujjat*.
- The son of the Imam, who is also called a *hujjat*.

Asked about the *dai*s of the present day, he answered that all the *duat* who came after Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan, until the *Qiyamah*, were established in the rank of *hujjat*. Their ranks were elevated and they were established in the rank of *hujjat*. This is their *shan*. These are the *qaimiyyat* (قَائِمِيَّة) — the *duat* of the era of the *qaim* — for there are three eras:

1. The *dawr al-nabawi*
2. The *dawr al-tayyibi*
3. The *dawr al-qaimi* (دَوْرُ الْقَائِم)

The *dawr al-nabawi* lasted until Mawlana Ali al-Tayyib. After Mawlana Ali al-Tayyib came the *dawr al-tayyibi*, which lasted until Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan, after whom the *dawr al-qaimi* began. When one thousand years were completed, the *dawr al-qaimi* began.

*Wa minhum man yakunu mustawdaan* — and among them is one who is a temporary trustee. Among those *duat* there is this further distinction: some hold the rank of *mustawda*. *Wa minhum man yakunu mustaqarran fi al-istida* — and some are *mustaqarr* (مُسْتَقَرٌّ), permanent, within the *istida* (اسْتِعْدَاد). The *istida* is the rank of *duat*, and it is a *mustawda* rank, while the rank of the *aimmah* is *mustaqarr*. So within the *istida*, some are *mustawdain* (مُسْتَوْدَعِين) and some *duat* become *mustaqarrin* (مُسْتَقِرِّين).

Asked to point to an instance, he said that Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan — despite his learning and despite the greatness he possessed, of which an example had just been set before them — was in the rank of *mustawdain*, while Sayyidna Jafar bin Sulayman was *mustaqarr*. Other *duat* came after him. Not all the *duat* have been mentioned, but some *duat* became *mustaqarr*, and among them the first was Sayyidna Jafar bin Sulayman — who held the rank of *mustaqarr*, meaning that within the *istidaiyyat* he became *mustaqarr*. All are *mustawdain*, but within the *istidaiyyat* he became *mustaqarr*. So the rank of Sayyidna Jafar rose higher still, and he reached the station of *hujjiyah*.

When it was compared to the station of the Nabi, he said that the station of the Nabi is something else entirely. When asked for the definition of *mustaqarr* — and whether it meant the succession of son after father — he answered that it is not like that. That belongs only to the Imams; the lineage of son from father is among the Imams. But *mustawda* and *mustaqarr* mean something different from that, and their full explanation has not come down: their rank moves from *istida* toward *istiqrar* (اِسْتِقْرَار), permanence, and it is possible that they become masters of special *majami* (مَجَامِع). Allah knows best. When it was put to him that from *mustawda* to *mustaqarr* is higher, and that this is what the passage yields, he agreed, and said it could be that they become masters of *majami*, special ones, and that special support is obtained by them from the Imam.

Just so did Sayyidna Jafar receive it. When Sayyidna Jafar traveled to Janad — a place in Yemen — and entered the *masjid* there, he was on a journey. With him were Sayyidna Ibrahim, his brother and the son of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fawz; Sayyidna Husayn bin Ahmad, his sister's son; and a Sindhi brother traveling in their company. Right at the time of *Maghrib*, when its hour was very near, Sayyidna Jafar said, "Look, at this moment the *tajalli* (تَجَلِّي) of the Imam is upon me. And at this moment, whatever *dua* you make, it will be accepted." So Sayyidna Ibrahim bin Muhammad bin Fawz made this *dua*: that his son should be a Dai and that the lineage of *duat* should continue in his progeny until the *Qiyamah*. Sayyidna Husayn bin Ahmad asked that he obtain such knowledge as neither the first ones had received nor the last ones. He then wrote such magnificent books — *Badiat al-Bayan*, and the *Hashiyah* on the *Anwar*, and others — that the *duat* are astonished and perplexed by them. The *duat mustaqarrin* (دُعَاةٌ مُسْتَقَرِّينَ) are astonished and perplexed by them. When they mention his name they call him Sayyidna Husam, though he was not *mustaqarr* and remained *ghayr munfarid* (غَيْرُ مُنْفَرِد); yet they mention his name with great respect and call him Sayyidna Husam. The Sindhi brother who was there had very poor handwriting, so he asked that his handwriting become very good. As the capacity of each was, so were the supplications, and his handwriting also became very good.

This narration used to be read in the *hijrat*, in the time of Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan. Between *Maghrib* and *'Isha* the book of *Sirah* of the *duat* was read, and incidents from the *sirah* of the *duat* would come up. One time this incident came up, and Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan looked at Ghulam and said, "Ghulam, you people are always worried about your handwriting." At that time Syedi Abdullah had arrived from Hindustan — Hadrat Abdullah sahib had arrived. And Sayyidna sahib said, "Mawlana, he was not a Hindi, he was a Sindhi."

Asked whether the man spoken of was Syedna Husayn bin Ahmad Dhul-Jaddayn, he said yes, Dhul-Jaddayn. The one in Baroda, however, is not Dhul-Jaddayn himself but Ali bin Husayn bin Ahmad bin Husayn bin Dhul-Jaddayn. Sayyidna Husayn bin Ahmad, whose story is told alongside Sayyidna Jafar, is his grandfather, and the one in Baroda is his grandson. He was sent in the time of Sayyidna Hibatullah, Ali bin Husayn. It is therefore a misunderstanding to say that the son of Dhul-Jaddayn lies there; the one buried there is not his son but his grandson. He passed away at forty-five years of age, in Baroda, and he was a great scholar — a very great scholar.

It was said that this is why Sayyidna Sulayman sahib always referred to Sayyidna Jafar as "my mawla." He answered: *Al-Mawla*. "Mawla Jafar, Mawla Jafar." How great a station it must be, that the father — in the *abyat* that Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan wrote describing the *shan* of Sayyidna Jafar bin Sulayman — used the words "Mawla Jafar, Mawla Jafar." Then Sayyidna Jafar, *qaddas Allahu ruhahu*, after his father's passing, transcribed those verses. His handwriting was very strange and unusual, completely unique. He transcribed them, and he removed the word "Mawla." When Sayyidna Sulayman sahib passed away, Sayyidna Jafar was eleven years old. Had he been much older when he was called "Mawla," the matter would be understandable; but at such a young age it was said, "my Mawla Jafar."

As for Ali bin Husayn just mentioned, his *rawdah* is the one called the *rawdah* of Dhul-Jaddayn. In it Hadrat Shakir sahib Muqaddas is also buried, and 'Azizah Bu as well — on one side Hadrat Shakir sahib Muqaddas, and on the other side Azizah Bu. It was observed that the day before had been his day, and he confirmed it.

When Sayyidna Ali bin Husayn al-Makrami came to Hindustan, Hadrat Shakir sahib Muqaddas was still alive. In Baroda, Hadrat Shakir sahib made this request: "Mawlana, my age has advanced, and *kullu nafsin dhaiqat al-mawt* — every soul will taste death — so I want to select the place of my burial, where I will be buried." Syedi sahib looked at all four sides and then said, "No, your place should be here," meaning where he was standing. There are four corners; in one corner he is buried, and in one corner is 'Azizah Bu, who was already there. He said, "This is your place." Asked whether Hadrat Shakir sahib had chosen another spot, he said yes, Hadrat Shakir sahib had thought of being near Dhul-Jaddayn, and Syedi sahib said, "This is the place."

Concerning Sayyidna Sulayman sahib and Sayyidna Jafar, it was asked whether what happened at the birth was not an indication that a Dai greater than himself had been born — for at that time Sayyidna Sulayman sahib had not yet become the Dai. He said yes. It was then the era of Sayyidna Daud bin 'Ajab Shah, who passed away in 999 Hijri. The matter belongs to a time before that: when Khadijah bint Nuh was pregnant and Sayyidna Jafar bin Sulayman was still in the womb, Sayyidna Sulayman sahib said to Janab Khadijah bint Nuh, "Your son is very blessed." She said, "Mawla, who knows whether it will be a boy or a girl?" He said, "It will be a boy, and he will be the Dai of his time." And he said also, "You will serve his *dawah* greatly." The believer sees with the light of Allah. The second thing that happened was that of Khadijah bint Nuh he used the term *rijal al-dawah* (رِجَال الدَّعْوَة), saying that she wears an earthly dress but in reality she is from the *rijal al-dawah*, one of the *rijal al-dawah*.

Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fawz, *'ala al-usul*, mentioned to Sayyidna Jafar, after the *intiqal* had happened, "I think that for the prosperity and well-being of the *dawah*, I should marry the wife of Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan." Sayyidna Jafar related this to his mother, and she said, "I will ponder this matter." After some time she agreed. So blessed a wife was she that from Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan, Sayyidna Yusuf bin Sulayman was born; and from Sayyidna Jafar bin Sulayman came Sayyidna Abd al-Tayyib and Sayyidna Ali. All four became *duat*:

- Sayyidna Yusuf and Sayyidna Abd al-Tayyib were *duat ghayr munfarid* (دُعَاةٌ غَيْرُ مُنْفَرِدٍ).
- Sayyidna Jafar and Sayyidna Ali sahib were *munfarid duat*.

Sayyidna Abd al-Tayyib wrote many books; his handwriting was very fast and very clear, and he wrote several works.

When Mawlana Khadijah bint Nuh became his wife, Sayyidna Ibrahim sahib was born, along with other children. Sayyidna Ibrahim sahib was imprisoned in Yemen by the Zaidi king and remained there for fourteen years. Of those fourteen years, Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan used to say: "Ghulam, the love of you Hindustanis for the *duat* is astonishing and strange. Our grandfather, when he was in prison, a Hindustani brother was there who would perform *Hajj* for him." In those days the performance of *Hajj* was a very difficult task, accomplished with great hardship. "For our grandfather, a Hindustani brother would perform *Hajj* every year." That is a great distinction indeed.

It was observed that the lineage of *duat* continued through Sayyidna Ibrahim and not through any of his brothers. He confirmed it: the line passed through none of his brothers. Sayyidna Ibrahim sahib had appointed his son Ismail, but Ismail passed away during his father's lifetime, and so the appointment was conferred on Sayyidna Muhammad bin Ismail, his grandson. The appointment rested upon his grandson.

*Kama kana sayakun* — as it was, so it will be. *Wa ma 'ulima sayulam* — what was known will be known; as it has been known, so it will certainly be known again. *Wa ma taht al-shams shayun bi-jadid* — there is nothing new under the sun.

The covenant then follows: a covenant of Allah, which is very firm. *Wa mithaquhu al-shadid* — and His covenant is strong, very strong. *Alayka ayyuha al-akh al-said* — upon you, O fortunate brother, in protecting the secrets of *al-Aliy al-Majid* (الْعَلِيُّ الْمَجِيدُ). This noble covenant requires that the secrets of *al-Aliy al-Majid* be protected.

*Wa man nadhara ila hadha al-kitab bi-ghayr idhn al-dai al-majid* — whoever looks into this book without the permission of the glorious Dai, *aw mimman lahu 'adw al-ilm* — or without the permission of one who holds the reins of knowledge, that is, the one in whose hand lies the rein of knowledge, who makes decisions in the *dawah* and possesses sound and penetrating insight, standing high in rank — *fa-qad baria min al-Mubdi' al-Muid* — then he is free of the Originator, the Restorer.

And the saying: *man adhaa sirrana adhaqahu Allah bard al-hadid* — whoever spreads our secret, Allah will make him taste the coldness of iron. Whoever broadcasts the secret will be made to taste the punishment of cold iron — a punishment that places him at the northern or the southern pole. The punishments are three:

- *'adhab al-adna* (عَذَابُ الْأَدْنَى)
- *adhab al-awsat* (أَذْهَاب الْأَوْسَاط)
- *adhab al-akbar* (عَذَابُ الْأَكْبَرِ)

Such a person falls into the *adhab al-awsat*. Asked whether this applies equally to one who reads without permission, he confirmed that it does.

With that the book is concluded, alhamdulillah. *Al-Aliy al-Wahhab* — the High, the Bestower — with His praise, *wa al-salat wa al-salam 'ala sayyidina Muhammad* — and blessings and peace upon our master Muhammad — *al-muntaqil min al-aslab al-tahirah ila al-arham al-mutahharah* — the one who transferred from pure loins to purified wombs. He kept transferring from the pure loins of his forefathers into the pure and holy wombs. None of his ancestors committed *shirk*; they were all pure and clean, for they were the *aimmah* of their time. How could they have committed *shirk*? All of them were righteous, the very essence of the universe, the *aimmah* of the time.

*Wa 'ala akhihi wa wasiyyihi* — and upon his brother and his legatee. Janab Ibrahim had two sons, Ismail and Ishaq. The line here descends from Janab Ismail, not from Janab Ishaq. From the progeny of Janab Ismail came the *aimmah*, *mustaqarr*, and from the progeny of Janab Ishaq came the *anbiya'*. The station of Janab Ismail is very high: he was present with Janab Ibrahim in the building of the Kabah, in the placing of the Hajar al-Aswad, and in raising the walls; and the genesis of Zamzam came through his feet. All of this stands to his glory. Yet his rank is less than his son's, and his son Qaydar's rank is less than his own, because Qaydar is the seventh Imam.

*Wa sallallahu 'alayhi wa ala akhihi wa wasiyyihi* — and may Allah send blessings upon him and upon his brother and his legatee — *al-muin al-aimmah al-ghurr* — the helper of the brilliant Imams — *bi-wasitat al-pak* — through the medium of the Pure one — *fi al-sirr al-mustasirr* — in the hidden secret. *Wa ala al-aimmah al-akhyar, majami' al-anwar* — and upon the righteous Imams, the collections of lights. *Wa 'ala Imam al-zaman, al-natiq, al-mubdi'... fi hal al-wiladah bi-alaniyah* — and upon the Imam of the time, the speaker, the originator, who at the moment of birth spoke openly. *Wa al-radi fi hadd al-tufuliyyah ila awan al-zuhur bi-nuraniyyah* — and the infant at the stage of childhood until the time of manifestation with luminosity. He progressed from childhood toward manifestation until his manifestation came with *nuraniyyah* (نُورَانِيَّةٌ). *Wa sallam 'alayhim* — and (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ).

Asked of whom this speaks, he answered that it speaks of the Imam of the time, whose rank is such that *Mubdi Taala* spoke with him openly in the state of *wiladah*, and who progressed from childhood toward manifestation until his manifestation came with *nuraniyyah*. And — and upon their forefathers, and upon their pure progeny, and upon their *duat*, who are *al-mayamin* — noble and honorable — *al-naibina manabahum ila qiyam yawm al-din* — their representatives until the establishment of the Day of Judgment.

It was then objected that the mention of *Mubdi Taala* here seems too elevated — that 'Aql al-Awwal or Aql al-Thani would make sense, but *Mubdi' Taala* would not, and that such a mention appears nowhere else. He answered that the term *Mubdi* (المُبدِع) may have been used while the reference is to the ranks below it. By *Mubdi Taala* the intent is not the one who brought existence from non-existence. That is not the intent — for what he did was to bring existence from non-existence, and that act was finished. After it, he has no further action. He has no further action at all. It was asked whether he would perform that act again, bringing existence once more from non-existence, and the answer was that he will never do so, that he will not do it again. It was asked: "What? Does he not have the power?" The answer came: "No — despite having the power, he will not do this." As for him — since when he is, what he is, what he is doing, and why he did it — the *duat* have no answer. There is only this: that he did it, and it was his *tafaddul* (تَفَضُّل) and his *mihrabani* that he did such a thing. That is the whole of the answer.

'Aql al-Awwal stands next. From the *marifah* he had of *Mubdi' Taala*, 'Aql al-Awwal became a *hijab* and a *bab*, and a continuous, flowing knowledge was established upon him that can never stop. For this reason it is said: *la takhudhuhu sinatun wa la nawm* — neither slumber nor sleep overtakes him. He feels no drowsiness and is not overcome by sleep, this Aql al-Awwal. Drowsiness makes a person partially heedless; sleep makes him completely heedless. Aql al-Awwal is neither partially heedless nor completely heedless. And *al-Hayy al-Qayyum* — the Living, the Self-Sustaining — he has become: alive and life-giving. *Al-Hayy al-Qayyum* is one of Allah's names and among the highest, and within it the *Ism al-Adham* is present. Certain words carry this quality — as is said of *Dhu al-Jalal wa al-Ikram* (ذُو الْجَلَالِ وَالْإِكْرَامِ) and *Ya Dha al-Jalal wa al-Ikram*, that within them the *Ism al-Adham* is present; so too *al-Hayy al-Qayyum*, which is the *tasbih* for Sunday. *Al-Hayy al-Qayyum* thus points toward Aql al-Awwal: that he is alive and life-giving.

What then did he do? The substance and support that 'Aql al-Awwal received from *Mubdi' Taala* he gave to 'Aql al-Thani — that is, to al-Munbaith al-Awwal — but only two and a half percent of it. He gave two and a half percent, as in *zakat*: of all that had gathered with him, he gave a portion of two and a half percent, because had he given the whole, his existence would have been consumed. In the same way, al-Munbaith al-Awwal gave to the ranks below him two and a half percent of his own. And so it continued.

<!-- editorial:begin -->
> **Editorial note (tradition-grounded).** 
> A note on a word this chapter turns on: *hijrah* comes from the Arabic root ه-ج-ر, which carries
> the sense of breaking away and leaving a thing behind — cutting the ties that bind a person to a
> place. The wider tradition preserves this root in its most famous instance, the Prophet's
> emigration from Mecca to Medina, a departure so decisive that the Muslim calendar takes its
> beginning from it. Hearing that weight in the word helps a modern reader understand why a *Dar
> al-Hijrah* — a house built at the site of such a departure — is treated here as worth
> commemorating centuries later, and why the text can say that a man migrated from his land and
> his city "except on account of the greatness of his station": the leaving is not flight but a
> severance undertaken deliberately, and the place of it is remembered accordingly.
<!-- editorial:end -->

## 27. The Mahdi, the Qaim, and the Straight Path

The first question concerns *Mawlana Mehdi* (مَوْلَانَا مَهْدِي), *sallallahu alayhi*. Two figures bear the name *Mehdi*: the eleventh Imam, who is *Mehdi*, and the fiftieth Imam, who is likewise a *Mehdi*.

The fiftieth is, in a certain sense, of the same order as *Rasulullah*: he does not fall within the circle of *Imamat* (إِمَامَة). *Mehdi Akhir al-Zaman* (مَهْدِيُّ آخِرِ الزَّمَان), the last *Mehdi*, stands outside that circle in the same way. He is neither an Imam nor the son of an Imam, yet his station (شَأْن) is immense. He is also called *Mim-e-Akhirah*. *Mim-e-Ula* is *Rasulullah*, *'ala dhikrihis salaam*, and *Mim-e-Akhirah* is *Mawlana Mehdi*, the fiftieth Imam.

The parallel has a precise significance. Just as *Rasulullah* broke the chain (سِلْك) of *Imamat* — being the twenty-ninth person to break that chain — so too did *Mawlana Mehdi* break it. After the forty-ninth Imam, the *Qaim* was due to come; but *Mawlana Mehdi* came in between. Today's Imam is *Abul Qaim* (أَبُو الْقَائِم), and after *Abul Qaim* the *Qaim* should have followed. *Mawlana Mehdi* came in between owing to his great precedents (سَوَابِقَات) and his exalted station.

The forefathers (جَدٌّ) of *Janab-e-Mehdi* were nonetheless Imams — just as the forefathers of *Rasulullah* were, though his immediate father was not.

A question was then raised, drawing upon a Quranic verse. God the Exalted said:

> *Taruj al-malaikatu wa al-ruh ilayhi fi yawmin kana miqdaruhu khamsina alfa sanah*

The angels and the Spirit ascend to Him in a day whose measure is fifty thousand years.

The question turned on *Fa mana al-yawm 'alayhi sallallahu alayhi wa al-dawr al-adhim, dawr al-kashf* (دَوْرُ الْكَشْف): the meaning of "the day" for him, (ع), is the great cycle, the cycle of unveiling (كَشْف). From *Janab-e-Mehdi* the cycle of *kashf* began; his cycle is called the cycle of *kashf*.

It is recalled that while studying with *Sayyidna Husayn bin Ismail*, the notion arose that *Mehdi* and *Qaim* are distinct. He said:

> *Al-Mehdi huwal Qaim, wal Qaim huwal Mehdi*

The Mehdi is the Qaim, and the Qaim is the Mehdi.

And he cautioned against treating them as two separate figures.

The question continued: *Fa al-mana 'alayhi sallallahu alayhi wa al-dawr al-adhim, dawr al-kashf, wa lahu mutimmoona min waladihi, wa huwa yakhudh zakaa* (زَكَاء). The meaning for him is the great cycle, the cycle of unveiling; for him are completers (مُتَمِّمِين) from his progeny; and he will receive the *khilafah* of *zakaa*, meaning the sun. The word *zakaa* appears in a *munajat* recited in *Sadaqallah*, composed by *Khassa Amir bin Sab*, where it is written: *Antum al-zakaa wa al-nasu*, "You are the sun, and the people are..." — meaning that *Janab-e-Ali* holds the position of the sun while others hold the position of stars.

As for the term *mutimmin*: just as there were *mutimmin* at the close of the cycle of 'Isa — those who come at the end of a *dawr*, or at the beginning of a *dawr*, being called *mutimmin* — so in the progeny of *Mawlana Mehdi* there is a *mutim*.

*Wa kam takunu khidmatuhu? Wa yakhudh khilafat al-ashir*: And what will his service be? He will receive the *khilafah* of the *'Ashir*. *Tafaddal alayna ya mawda' al-sirri wa al-sarair*: Bestow upon us, O repository of secrets and hidden matters. *Jazakallahu afdal al-jaza*: May Allah reward you with the best of rewards. *Bi-haqqi Sayyidina Muhammadin wa aalihi al-tahirin*: By the right of our master Muhammad and his pure family.

The answer follows. *Bi-awnillahi subhanahu wa mannihi wa yusrihi wa tayidi mawlana al-Malik, mahbat ashat al-malaik*: with the help of Allah, Glorified is He, and His grace and His ease, and the support of our master the King, namely *Sayyidna Hibatullah*, the place where the rays of the angels descend. *Anna mawlana al-Mehdi al-adhim, alladhi huwa qaim dawr al-sitr*: our master, the great *Mehdi*, is in truth the *Qaim* of the cycle of concealment (دَوْرُ السِّتْر).

*Wa lahu shanun 'adhim*, his station is very great; *wa fadlun jasim*, his virtue is immense. The author describes him not as a confluence (*jami'*) but as the *Qaim* of the *dawr al-sitr*. *Wa huwa majma thaman suwar, suwar 'idham samiyah*: he is the confluence of eight surahs, surahs of great and lofty rank. This rests upon the verse:

> *Wa yahmilu 'arsha rabbika fawqahum yawmaidhin thamaniyah*

And on that Day, eight will carry the Throne of your Lord above them.

That is, eight angels will bear the Divine Throne on the Day of Resurrection. *Wa sabu suwarin fi dawr al-kashf al-madi li-hadha al-shakhs*: and seven surahs belong to the past cycle of unveiling for this person, a *surah* having come every seven thousand years in the past *dawr al-kashf*. On this matter *Sayyidna 'Ali bin Hanzala* (سَيِّدُنَا عَلِيُّ بْنُ حَنْظَلَةَ), may Allah sanctify his soul, is cited: *Wa hiya yasadu fi asnaihi min suwar, sabun min al-qism alladhi, azimah fi thalathat alaf fi dawr al-fatrah* — and it ascends in its midst from the surahs, seven from the portion which is great, in three thousand years in the period of interruption (دَوْرُ الْفَتْرَة). These are three surahs, each representing one thousand years, yielding three in all.

*Wa hadha dawr al-sitr al-mutaqallibina fihi ahl al-sharra*: this is the cycle of concealment, in which evil people have prevailed. *Wa yawmuhu huwa dawr al-asna*: his day is the lofty cycle. *Wa al-imamah fihi mustamirrah*: the *Imamat* within it is continuous, as it was throughout the *dawr al-sitr*. *Min dhurriyyatihi, alladhina hum maani al-asma al-husna*: from his progeny, who are the meanings of the Most Beautiful Names.

*Wa dhakarta annahu yakhudh zakaa, fa-laysa dhalika kadhalik. Bal alladhi yakhudhuha bada intiha al-rubatati huwa al-rukn al-asham*: the questioner mentioned that he will receive the *khilafah* of the sun, but that is not so. Rather, the one who will receive it after the end of the four (رُبَاطَاتٌ) is the lofty pillar (الرُّكْنُ الْأَشَمُّ), meaning the *rukn al-asad* (رُكْنُ الْأَسَدِ), who will receive the *khilafah* of the sun.

*Fa-rif dhalika wa taaqqal ma hunalika. Wa 'alayka bi-al-tasawwur al-mustaqim. Li-takuna min sukkan jannat al-naim*: so understand this matter and comprehend what is there, and hold fast to straight understanding, so as to be among the dwellers of the Garden of Bliss.

*Wa amma al-Qaim, 'alayhi salamu, wa tahiyyatuhu wa ikramuhu, fa-innahu inda firaqi min hadhihi al-dar, yakhluf al-ashir*: as for the *Qaim*, may peace and greetings and honor be upon him, upon departure from this abode — at the time of passing away — he will succeed the *Ashir*.

A question arises concerning how long the *Qaim* will remain in the world. Only three days. The *qiyam* of the *Qaim* will last three days.

Whether the *Qaim* and *Mehdi Akhir al-Zaman* are two distinct personalities: they are indeed two distinct personalities. The *Qaim* of the *dawr al-sitr*, however, has been called *Mehdi*, and the station of *Mawlana Qaim* (مَوْلَانَا الْقَائِم) is certainly greater than that of *Mehdi*. That *Qaim*, peace be upon his mention, will receive the *khilafah* of the *'Ashir Aziz al-Jabbar*. The *Ashir* here refers to the second *juzi dai* (جُزْئِيّ دَاعِي) of this *kawr*. When he progresses beyond one *kawr*, he will become the *juzi dai* of the next *kawr*, and that will be the *Qaim* — and that too in a *juzi* (جُزْئِيٌّ) capacity, a partial one, not in a *kulli*, total, capacity, for many others stand in the line ahead.

The opening of Surah Ya-Sin illumines this:

> *Yasin. Wal-Quran al-hakim, innaka la-mina al-mursalin, 'ala siratin mustaqim*

Ya-Sin. By the wise Quran, indeed you are from among the messengers, on a straight path.

Allah grants such greatness as to say that he is from among the messengers, and then adds that he is on the straight path. At first this seems incongruous — like introducing a man as a PhD professor holding degrees from ten universities and then adding that he has passed his matriculation examination. Yet the meaning becomes clear: *'ala siratin mustaqim* refers to that line, the line of *juz's* (جُزْء), and among those standing in it the Prophet, (ع), is one. Before him in that line stand the *Thalathat Kubara'*, the Three Great Ones, and after them it is he.

There is a further verse in which Allah says:

> *Inna rabbi 'ala siratin mustaqim*

Indeed, my Lord is on a straight path.

This is also reported as a saying of the Prophet, that his Lord is on the straight path — referring to Abu Talib, the one who raised him, affirming that he too is on that path. From all of this, *sirat mustaqim* (صِرَاط مُسْتَقِيم) signifies this very path of *juz's*; it is the straight path. Yet for the *Aimmah al-Tahirin*, the Pure Imams, and for *Rasulullah*, the *sirat mustaqim* carries a different and higher meaning.

The recitation of *Ihdina al-sirat al-mustaqim*, "Guide us to the straight path," is a magnificent prayer. As *Haddrat al-Muqaddas* (حَضْرَةُ الْمُقَدَّس) once explained, it does not merely mean "place us on the straight path" but rather "make us walk upon it" — cause us to move forward along it. The chain extending from a *mumin* to the Imam passes through both the lower ranks (حُدُودٌ دَانِيَة) and the higher ranks (*hudud 'aliyah*). To walk that path is to move swiftly toward the *Bab al-Abwab*, which is the destination of every *mumin*s spiritual form (صُورَة). When that station of *Bab al-Abwab* is reached and its magnificent assembly — *fasl al-khitab*, the decisive speech — is attained, and when the Imam receives that assembly and conveys it to the *Farzand-e-Imam*, the son of the Imam, it becomes as it were a fixed deposit: wherever the *Farzand-e-Imam* goes, it accompanies them.

There are therefore two kinds of spiritual progress (تَرَقِّيَات):

- One in which a person strives and advances through their own effort.
- Another in which one advances by sitting in the lap of another, resting in that nearness.

Both are genuine forms of progress. The pace of advancement depends on each person's capacity, their *taqwa*, piety, and their *taharat* (طَهَارَة), purity. Furthermore, the *sadaqat*, charities, and good deeds performed after a *mumin*'s passing — when children recite on his behalf and perform righteous acts — reach him as well, causing him to progress more swiftly from whatever station he has attained.

A human's *surah* is not divided among the Imams; all the units gather in one place. A *surah sharif* that is progressing keeps on progressing. Whatever comes into the world — as a person passes away and after a thousand years returns — comes back in the same way, but minus the vegetative soul (نَامِيَة), minus the sensory soul (حِسِّيَّة), minus the rational soul (نَاطِقَة). Sometimes it even rises above his *moththalan ikhtirai*, his archetypal form. Its progress is very slow, yet it keeps progressing. In one *kawr*, its manifestations (ظُهُورَات) occur three hundred and sixty times, through the integration of the *walad*. For this reason *Sayyidna Ali bin Sulayman* said that in the human body there are three hundred and sixty *nufus*. This means that one of his *nafs* progresses in one *kawr*, in one *dawr*. When his integration through grace takes place, then one part out of three hundred and sixty progresses.

In the *duas* of *Mawlana Jafar al-Sadiq*, which are to be recited daily, there occurs in the twenty-seventh *tariqah* (طَرِيقَة) the phrase *Innaka 'ala siratin mustaqim*, "Indeed, you are on a straight path." Its address appears to be to Allah, as though saying, "O Allah, you are on the straight path." The answer is the same as before. "Allah" there does not mean *Mubdi' Taala*, the Originator, the Exalted, but the *juzi dai*, the partial missionary, of that *kawr*. The indication is toward him — that he too is on the *sirat mustaqim*, meaning that he stands in the line to take the *khilafah* of the *'Ashir*.

Everyone has to progress. *Allati ma yuhitu biha illa man safat suratuhu min al-rayb*: which cannot be encompassed except by one whose form has been purified from doubt. These matters can be understood and encompassed only by one whose *surah* has been completely purified from doubts and suspicions. *Wa tawalla amra 'alam al-tabiah*: and he took charge of the affair of the world of nature. Such ones become masters of the affairs of the natural world. *Wa yadharu fi afwaniha surur al-badiah… Fa-hafiz hadha al-jawab al-mukhtasar wa sunhu fa-innahu min al-sirr al-akbar*: and in its horizons appear wondrous joys; so preserve this concise answer and guard it, for it is from the greatest of secrets. This concise answer is to be known and guarded, for it is among the loftiest of secrets.

Here the matter rests for today, the need having been met.

## 28. Rolling Up the Sky: Cycles Without End

At the close of the book stand two appended questions. They were not part of the original composition; one man, Ibrahim bin 'Ali, put them to the author after the work was otherwise finished, and they were written down at the end. The first has already been answered. What follows is the second and last.

The question concerns the saying of God Almighty:

> *Yawma natwi al-samaa katayyi al-sijilli lil-kutub.*
>
> "The Day when We will roll up the heaven like the rolling up of a scroll for the books."

Just as the pages of a book are rolled shut, so the heaven will be rolled up. The word in the verse is *lil-kutub*, in the plural; where a copy reads *lil-kitab*, that is a slip of the pen, for the verse itself has *kutub*.

The questioner asks: *wa hal dhalika lahu haddun maruf* — is there a known limit for this, a fixed term at which the matter comes to pass? Or will it not happen until after the mountains and the stones have crumbled, *wa yaudu ila al-maadin*, and what remains returns to the mineral realm, so that these heavens must first enter the *madan* in order to rise? And then pass on to *al-nabat*, plant life, and move forward from there? Will the process continue until what is held is freed from its captivity, and until the ascent of spirits to the celestial spheres is accomplished and the bonds of the spheres are broken?

Then he adds: *ya Maulai, juiltu fidak* — O my master, may I be your ransom — will the great era of unveiling follow the era of concealment, like this era in which we now stand? Or will the eras succeed one another forever and ever? And he closes with the words of Scripture, *yalamu khainata al-ayun wa ma tukhfi al-sudur wa ila Allahi tasir al-umur*: He knows the treachery of the eyes and what the breasts conceal, and to God all affairs return. So ends the question.

### The Answer

The answer opens with the help of God, Glorified be He, and His favor, and with the support given by Maulai al-Malik, the landing place of the rays of the angels, meaning Sayyidna Hibatullah.

As for His saying, *yawma natwi al-samaa katayyi al-sijilli* — that will be at the ending of the bonds, *fa dhalika yakunu 'inda intiha' al-ribatat*. It comes at the end of the *kawr*, the minor cycle. *Bila shakk wa la rayb wa la irtiyab*: without doubt, without skepticism, without uncertainty, it will be so.

On that day *tubaddalu al-ardu ghayra al-ard*, the earth is exchanged for another earth. Length is created upon width, so that what was extended in one direction is turned into the other: the north becomes the south. *Wa dhalika ma taqtadihi 'inayat allam al-ghuyub*: that is what the providence of the Knower of all hidden things requires, and as He decrees, so it comes to be. *Yawma tamuru al-ardu mawra*, the day the earth sways in its great cycling, *wa tasiru al-jibalu sayra*, and the mountains set out and travel, quitting their places and fleeing.

As the Presence of the Best of Beings has said:

> *Kama badana awwala khalqin nuiduhu, wadan 'alayna.*
>
> "Just as We began the first creation, We shall repeat it. A promise binding upon Us."

A promise binding upon Him, which He will assuredly fulfill. After one *kawr*, another *kawr* comes. The creatures that issue from the wombs, and the bodies that are composed, are composed only at the opening of a *kawr*; and one *kawr* is followed without fail by the next.

As for the question whether the era of unveiling will follow the era of concealment, like the present era, the answer is yes. *Yataaqubu dawr al-sitr wal-kashf ka taaqub al-layli wa al-nahar*: the era of concealment and the era of unveiling succeed one another as night succeeds day, *bila tahwil wa la tabdil*, with no shifting and no alteration. So it continues until the Greatest Cycle is completed, *ila an yatimmu al-mawr al-azam*.

### The Measure of the Cycles

The reckoning proceeds by multiplication, not by simple addition.

1. Fifty thousand years make one *dawr*.
2. Four hundred thousand *dawr* multiplied by four hundred thousand *dawr* make one *kawr*.
3. Four hundred thousand *kawr* multiplied by four hundred thousand *kawr* make one *mawr*.

The duration is immense beyond ordinary reckoning. One *mawr* has already passed over the world. Nine remain.

The length of what remains was raised as a difficulty: nine *mawr* is a period past measuring, so how is it to be traversed? The *dai* who gave the answer replied that just as one *mawr* passed over without being perceived at all, so the nine will pass in the same way.

The manner of it is this. When a *mawr* has passed, the *'ashir*, the Tenth Intellect, who stands in that station, yields his place to the ninth *aql*, and the offspring of his rank moves up accordingly. The station of the intellect keeps changing — which is to say the *tadbirat* (تَدْبِيرَات), the dispensations, keep changing. The *ashir* ascends one degree with each *mawr*, so that across ten *mawr* he mounts ten steps and arrives at the first rank, the first station. Yet he does not pass onward into the second station, into the domain of the *Aql al-Awwal*. He is absorbed instead into the *Mumbaith al-Awwal*, the First Emitted Being.

Where do the ascending forms go — all the forms of the *mawr*, and the *'ashir* who vacates his place? To the domain of the *Mumbaith al-Awwal*. The domain of the *Aql al-Awwal* is the domain of *wahdat* (وَحْدَت), of unity: it admits no partner, it is one and has no companion. Just as forms gather at the *Bab al-Abwab*, so every gathering of ascending forms — those that rise as intellects and the countless others that climb alongside each one — assembles in the domain of the *Mumbaith al-Awwal*. At the end, two domains alone remain: the domain of the *Aql al-Awwal* and the domain of the *Mumbaith al-Awwal*. All the rest are absorbed, and after that no other being remains. This absorption belongs to the last *mawr*, after the ninth, while the process itself runs continuously throughout.

Concerning the two stations named in Scripture: *Sidrat al-Muntaha* (سِدْرَةُ الْمُنْتَهَى) stands higher than *Jannat al-Mawa* (جَنَّةُ الْمَأْوَى). In one respect *Jannat al-Mawa* corresponds to the *bab al-zahir* (بَابُ الظَّاهِر), the manifest gate, and *Sidrat al-Muntaha* to the *bab al-batin* (بَابُ الْبَاطِن), the esoteric gate.

By the arrangement of nature, God did not grant human beings knowledge of their former lives. The point is not remote: humanity has come from the era of unveiling, yet those who stand in the era of concealment retain no knowledge of it. God Almighty empties it from the mind and causes it to be forgotten. Every advance the world has made began again from an entirely new beginning. The unveiling is the work of the Aimmah.

And the judgment on that day belongs to God. He decides and He decrees.

### The Repetition Within the Great Cycle

Sayyidna Ibrahim bin Muhammad, may God sanctify his soul and illuminate his shrine, gave an illustration of this: *anna dawr al-kashf wa al-sitr yatakarraru fi hadha al-kawr al-azam al-mashhur wahid wa 'ishruna lakh marra* — that the era of unveiling and the era of concealment repeat, within this well-known great cycle, twenty-one *lakh* times, that is, two million one hundred thousand times, together with its fractions. The word *lakh* stands there in transliteration, taken into the Arabic. One *mawr* has already elapsed; and within the *kawr* of the second *mawr*, in which the world now stands, two million one hundred thousand alternations of concealment and unveiling have already transpired.

Then comes the exhortation:

> *Fa atruku al-ghaflata wal-nisyan. Wa Allahu yuwaffiquna wa iyyakum lima yurid al-Rahim al-Rahman, bihaqqi safwat Mudar wa 'Adnan, alayhim al-salawatu wal-salam ma taaqaba al-malawan.*
>
> "So abandon heedlessness and forgetfulness. And may God grant us and you success in what the Merciful, the Compassionate desires, by the right of the elect of Mudar and 'Adnan. Upon them be blessings and peace as long as the two alternate."

The elect of Mudar and 'Adnan are the Messenger of God and the Imams, the pure essences of those two ancestral lines. And *al-malawan* (الْمَلَوَانِ), the two that alternate, are the night and the day.

### The Gathering of Forms

In the present age likewise, the noble forms of all believers that are ascending, whether of lower rank or higher, are going to the *Bab al-Abwab*. A lifetime is spent in *nazar-maqam*, taking the name of the Imam of the Time at every moment, calling out, "O Imam, help us." That belief stands firm in its own place. Yet the *surat-i sharif*, the noble form, travels to the *Bab al-Abwab*: passing through the manifest gate, it arrives at the esoteric gate.

For this reason it has been said: *la hawla wa la quwwata illa billah al-ali al-azim* — there is no power and no strength except in God, the Most High, the Most Great. Neither the manifest gate nor the esoteric gate possesses power sufficient to compose the *haykal*, the spiritual body. That lies beyond them. The one who composes it is God, meaning the Imam of the Time, and He alone can compose the *haykal*. He will grant to His *fard* (فَرْد), the high dignitary, that vast and magnificent collection of *suwarat*, taking it from the *Bab al-Abwab* and delivering it to His *fard*. The *Bab al-Abwab* then passes away: once the *haykal* has been set down, he departs within three days. And the recompense granted to the *Bab al-Abwab* for his service is that he becomes the *qalb*, the heart, of the *fard*.

Of the same order is the service performed from ancient times by Maulai al-Kainat, Sayyidna 'Ali. The *suwarat al-anbiyya'*, the forms of the prophets, and the *hayakil* from Maulana Hunaid onward — from Maulana Hunaid to Maulana Hashim — were arriving, since the amalgamation of the *thalath suwar* had not yet taken place with him. And from Janab Hasan to Abul-Qasim, down to the Imam of today, the one who gathered all their *hayakil* is Sayyidna 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع). He gathered them for the Qaim. The whole collection accumulated with him he will entrust to the Qaim. He became, as it were, the *Bab al-Abwab* for the Qaim, and he will become the *qalb*, the heart, of the Qaim.

### The Scribe

The closing lines are the writing of the *faqir*, the one poor and needy before God: 'Ali bin Qutbuddin (قُطْبُ الدِّين), who sets himself down as a *faqir* before his *wali* and his *dai*, *al-awwab*, the ever-returning. He asks that whoever comes upon the book pray for him and seek forgiveness for him in this world and the next. He was among the great elders of this community, buried in Baroda beneath the neem tree, and this was his humility.

He composed other works as well, among them a *sirat* (سِيرَة) of Sayyidna Ismail bin Hibatullah written in the manner of *haqaiq* — the first book of the Dawah in which a life is set down in that manner, a *sirat* ordinarily being a record of historical events. It is a magnificent book, *Al-Bahirat al-Adillah*. In Najran it is said of it that it is not a book but *halwa*: there is the same pleasure in reading it as in eating sweets. Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan, may God sanctify his soul, once asked where in Hind 'Ali bin Qutbuddin lay buried, and whether he was the author of *Al-Bahirat al-Adillah*. On being told that he was, he said, "What a magnificent person he was."

He held no designated rank. He lived in the time of the first *mansub* of Baroda, Karamullah Sahab. In his day the Sunnis of the Sadikpuri sect complained to the king — a Hindu ruler of that time — that the Bohras and the 'Alawis held false beliefs and were misguided. The king summoned the Bohras, and they answered, "On our behalf, Ali bin Qutbuddin will reply." The Sulaymanis agreed to this, and the Alawis said the same. The Sunni scholars present put several questions to him, and he answered every one of them out of their own books, naming the page: in your book, on this page, is the answer. The king was astonished and dismissed them with honor. Then Ali bin Qutbuddin said, "Now I will put three questions to you, and I know you will not be able to answer them." He asked his three questions, and they could not reply. Much effort has been spent in trying to recover those three questions and their answers, without success. He was a magnificent elder of this community.

With that the book is complete. *Tammat hadha.* Praise belongs to God.

> *Allahumma nasaluka al-Fatiha hadiyyatan wa wasilatan saniyyatan zakiyyatan mardiyya ila ruhi Sayyidna 'Ali bin Hibatullah, qaddasa Allahu ruhahu wa nawwara darihahu, wa afada alayna min barakatihi wa anfasatihi wa taidatihi, wa taqabbal minna ya Allah al-ifadah wal-istifadah bi ahsan ma yakun, bi-jahi Sayyid al-Mursalin wa sayyidi Maulana Amir al-Muminin wal-Quran al-azim wa ahl baytihi al-tayyibin al-tahirin, salawat Allah wa salamuhu alayhim ajmain. Al-Fatiha.*
>
> O God, we ask You for the Opening as a gift, an offering raised, pure and pleasing, to the soul of Sayyidna 'Ali bin Hibatullah, may God sanctify his soul and illuminate his shrine, and may He pour upon us of his blessings, his breaths, and his support. Accept from us, O God, the giving and the receiving in the best manner there is, by the standing of the Master of the Messengers and of our master Maulana the Commander of the Faithful, and by the mighty Quran and the people of his house, the good and the pure. The blessings of God and His (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ).

<!-- editorial:begin -->
> **Editorial note (tradition-grounded).** 
> A note for the reader: this closing exchange belongs to a genre of question-and-answer in which
> a seeker puts to his teacher matters far beyond anything he could act upon. A related teaching
> preserved in this tradition draws the relevant distinction — that subtle, spiritual knowledge
> has no connection with practice; its connection is only with belief and affirmation, and it has
> no link with works or with the body's exertion. Read in that light, the vast reckonings above
> are not a timetable to be acted on but a matter for assent, offered to settle the heart rather
> than to direct the limbs. The wider tradition also records the saying of one of its masters that
> "we have answers to questions that have not yet even been asked" — a fitting frame for a book
> that ends by welcoming, rather than deflecting, a questioner's late and difficult inquiry.
<!-- editorial:end -->
